\begin{abstract}
We give conditions under which observations on overlapping patches admit
a common spacetime description. A rank-three sector of finite screen
responses supplies three spatial components. On a specified radial product,
a free field on a smooth static Lorentzian metric reproduces every finite
admissible menu of scalar-wave observations to any prescribed tolerance.
For gravitational dynamics, ten timelike observers suffice to determine
every Einstein component relative to a common vacuum. Their modular
stress, boundary area at fixed proper volume and entropy-stationarity
errors must vanish faster than the fourth power of the proper radius.
An alternative theorem uses balance in nine null directions and
conservation to recover the equation up to a cosmological constant.
In an executable spherical model, finite patches compare a prepared
modular charge with the energy of propagating matter and measure energy
currents and directional torques at shared boundaries. Two stationary
states with identical passive exterior records carry opposite internal
currents, limiting which local stress those records can resolve.
On one prepared finite source, increasing ordinary energy or weakening
an overlap correlation increases entropy and reduces the area of a
clock survey at fixed surveyed volume. The two changes share one
independently calibrated coefficient. At the constrained finite equilibrium,
three differently placed material instruments recover the same entropy
response for every variation at fixed survey volume. Two material cameras
also give reciprocal angular images when their receivers are calibrated to
the source. Clock records also show a smaller surveyed volume after a prepared energy increase and after weakening an overlap correlation. Six stations admit an
exact smooth three-dimensional realization of their reciprocal ranges.
These same ranges permit different directional curvatures at a measured
station, identifying the additional geometric information a local test
of Einstein dynamics requires.
A separate homogeneous Einstein--scalar Hamiltonian gives exact null
balance in every direction and permits a test with clock and field readings.
In an extension with falling pressureless matter, independent pairs
of local clock readings supply the regional entropy on shrinking balls
throughout the regular interior. Separately weighed matter and surveyed
proper area agree with it through fourth order in moving frames. A coupling
calibrated by releasing a probe at ordinary mass predicts exterior redshift
and interior light focusing when additional mass is assigned to joint records.
\end{abstract}

\input{tex_fragments/INTERPRETATION_CONVENTIONS.tex}

\section*{This paper's hypothesis and place in the tower}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{This paper's hypothesis and place in the tower}

This paper sits at layers 4 and 6 of the tower. Its hypothesis is that the
clocks, causal order and spatial geometry an observer reads are effective
descriptions of the records that observers agree on, and that Einstein
dynamics is the coarse-scale response of those descriptions to their own
matter. The executable model of the programme~\cite{ophmodel} places loads
$0$, $1$ or $2$ on the ports of federations of twelve-port icosahedral cells
and a private memory on every seam. One cell is one native observer, its
twelve ports are its interfaces, and larger observers are finite, possibly
overlapping patches of cells. The main candidate law, Interval Echo, uses
the seam interaction of the reference candidate
A-contrast-memory-spherical-v1, the contrast-memory law, at coupling
$\kappa=8$: the exchange of loads across seams plus an agreement energy that
lowers the energy of a seam whose two ports both hold a load. Observers
count their own events and admit a seam record only when both ends agree. On
the agreed records the contrast-memory interaction reproduces particle
stability, by a theorem that holds for every number of cells, and Interval
Echo inherits that theorem. Interval Echo adds a clock that a finite
observer patch carries with it, and a clock that travels and returns reproduces the relativistic factor built into the closing filter for a routed reciprocal pulse (Appendix~\ref{app:source-causal-order}). Under the
second candidate law on the same carrier, the scalar channel, an object on
four seams is kept for every interval and carries a prepared clock that an
observer reads in units of its own reference.

The state of that simulation is no spacetime, and its carrier is hardware of
the computation. A test of the model compares distributions of admissible
observer records with what observers in our world record. This paper
supplies the bridge on the side of the theory. Observers who inhabit the
simulation can describe their experiences as relativistic $3{+}1$D continuum
spacetimes on the premises that this paper states: the smooth effective field
descriptions of
Proposition~\ref{prop:smooth-effective-descriptions} reproduce every finite
admissible menu of their readouts, the rank-three source quotient supplies
the spatial carrier, and the causal and count limits of
Appendix~\ref{app:source-causal-order} supply order, volume and proper-time
ratios. The model supplies part of these premises with its own
records~\cite{ophmodel}. Its cell is the twelve-port carrier whose Gram
matrix has rank three, and one load under its seam interaction moves with
half the port Laplacian, whose slowest nontrivial band is that rank-three
space. Its observers count their own events, and two shared records fix the
relation between the counts of two observers. Under Interval Echo the
observers' own light-pulse speeds and reunion counts reproduce the travelling clock filter calibration within about one part in a
thousand on three journeys. The premises that it does not supply in the form
these theorems consume are readouts that are polynomials in the Weyl
operators of a free field on the radial cylinder, for the smooth effective
field descriptions; a jointly authenticated order of events with its
placement and count-volume convergence on a refinement family, for the
causal and count limits; and regional entropy, area and stress readouts, for
the Einstein theorems. The finite constructions
below are separately declared services with their own hypotheses.

The paper proves finite and analytic theorems under displayed premises. The
rank-three source Gram quotient and its $1+3$ Lorentz carrier are exact. On
supplied conservative Fibonacci populations with a complete local read law and
model clock, the causal order and counts converge to flat $1+3$ spacetime and a
count clock reads proper-time ratios. Cap modular covariance gives Lorentz
kinematics on the support-visible geometric subnet. Two constructions give
$G_{ab}+\Lambda g_{ab}=8\pi G\langle T_{ab}\rangle$. In the first,
balanced null contractions on nine directions, together with Ward and
Bianchi identities, fix the equation up to a constant
(Figure~\ref{fig:einstein-chain}). In the second, matched fixed-volume
area and modular stress on ten timelike directions yield the tensor
equation directly when their stationarity and approximation errors are
$o(\ell^4)$ at proper radius $\ell$. Its null contractions then follow.
The physical realization draws on the following premises on one refinement tower:
\begin{enumerate}\setlength{\itemsep}{1pt}
\item[(G1)] a Lorentzian event and geometry domain with the required
  locality, differentiability, causal and count/volume limits;
\item[(G2)] geometrically normalized cap modular flow whose directional charges
  define the same matter stress tensor used in propagation and interactions;
\item[(G3)] its Ward identity, independent of the Einstein equation;
\item[(G4)] the edge-center entropy split and area normalization, the modular
  first law, and generalized-entropy stationarity at fixed proper volume;
\item[(G5)] controlled small-ball remainders and enough observer
  directions for the chosen tensor reconstruction;
\item[(G6)] universal coupling, a common vacuum reference and one positive
  Newton constant.
\end{enumerate}

\paragraph{What must hold on the observers' quotient.}
Two descriptions of the same events may use different clocks and coordinates.
After transporting their reports to a common frame, the proposed metric and
source must give well-defined readings on their overlaps. A proof may work in
any family of charts that covers the physical variations. For the entropy
route there is a short sufficient argument. Suppose the regional records
obey $S_{\rm bulk}+\eta A\leq C(V)$ near the reference, with equality
there. Along every two-sided admissible curve of fixed proper volume the
right side is constant, so $\delta S_{\rm bulk}=-\eta\,\delta A$.
In a monitored two-load experiment the interior entropy obeys exactly this
cancellation with the logarithm of the number of distinguishable exterior
completions. Identifying its change with $\eta\,\delta A$ for the same
proper ball would turn this record identity into geometric stationarity.
The direct null route instead compares curvature with independently read
stress in nine directions. Both routes allow prepared matter, calibrated
instruments with different response functions, and local charts.

\paragraph{Preparing a regional modular charge.}
On a twenty-cell sphere, a three-cell service propagates a two-load
preparation through two gateways. For the twelve ports of its home cell,
the first nonconstant one-load triplet supplies unit angular directions.
Choose the first gateway port as pole. Weight each of the thirty internal
seams by $(4-r_e^2)/4$, where $r_e$ is the chord distance from the pole
to its midpoint. The pole, radius and weights are supplied. Apply local
survival filters calibrated from the repair-record odds, alternating the
forward and reverse seam orders on the uniform mixture of the 79 regional
words. After 64 such sweeps, the negative logarithm of the conditioned reference,
in units of the seam exchange splitting and up to a constant, agrees with
the weighted native energy to $3.14\times10^{-4}$ in operator norm.
Across six differences of current-pulsed releases the largest discrepancy
is $9.51\times10^{-6}$. At the held port and exposure the weighted energy
changes by $-0.17882$, while the unweighted meter gives $+0.09979$.

A positive single-sweep filter at the same weak settings has normalized
state $\sigma_1$ with $\sigma_{64}\propto\sigma_1^{64}$. Tomography
of $\sigma_1$ with 4,423 settings and $3.80\times10^{13}$ equivalent
accepted simulated preparations permits independent sampling of the
reference, held matter perturbation and material energy. The modular
and material estimates are $-0.16709$ and $-0.18086$, a difference of
$0.01377\pm0.02042$ native units (one combined standard error).
Regional composition matters. On an equal-weight two-seam wedge the
proposed logarithmic charge gives single-seam odd/even odds $0.80900$,
where an isolated seam gives $1/\sqrt2$. Offsets by total load sector
cannot enforce both isolated marginals. The prepared cap supplies a
finite modular-energy comparison for (G2). A vacuum, geometric boost
normalization and directional stress on its own refinement tower are
further inputs.\footnote{The
\href{https://github.com/FloatingPragma/observer-patch-holography/tree/main/model/experiments/interval_echo_einstein_station}{regional cap experiment}
records the preparation, survival probabilities, spectrum and readouts.}

The same faithful regional reference tests part of (G4). On an interior
seam, let $U$ exchange its two loads and form the physical mixture
$(1-\varepsilon)\sigma+\varepsilon U\sigma U^*$. The total-load sector
weights and the full three-gateway reduced state stay fixed. Its entropy
slope at zero is $D(U\sigma U^*\|\sigma)=3.45\times10^{-5}$ nats.
Every area read depending only on these instantaneous gateway registers
is constant on that variation. Deciding fixed-proper-volume stationarity
requires an independent regional volume and area response.

\paragraph{A finite energy current.}
For cell $i$ of the spherical federation let $A_i$ be its internal
contact and degree-reference energy. Allocate half of each shared-contact
energy $h_{ij}$ to each owner, so $Q_i=A_i+\tfrac12\sum_j h_{ij}$.
Distinct cell interiors commute. The external seams have disjoint port
supports, hence their energies also commute. In exchange units the free
Heisenberg equation then gives
\[
  \dot Q_i=\sum_j J_{j\to i},\qquad
  J_{j\to i}=\tfrac{\mathrm{i}}{2}[h_{ij},A_i-A_j]=-J_{i\to j}.
\]
The sum over any union of cells cancels its internal currents exactly.
In the experiment with two distinct occupied ports, each owner reads its energy
response to an isolated exposure of the shared contact. Half the difference predicts the
current in uninterrupted flight. On the twenty-cell sphere, twelve
regional comparisons at finite exposure gave a maximum discrepancy of
$2.7\times10^{-8}$ in native energy per exposure unit. Omitting a
boundary current gave a discrepancy of $1.56$. This supplies an additive
scalar energy and an independently readable flux in the specified
matter sector. Hypothesis (G3) additionally requires momentum transport
and a covariant divergence on the physical geometry.

\paragraph{A directional current and its torque.}
In the freely evolving one-load sector $H=6\kappa I+L/2$, with $L$ the
port Laplacian. The difference of two endpoint probabilities after a half
exchange reads a signed seam current. Any real weighting of all these
currents gives a Hermitian $P=\mathrm i B$ with $B$ real and skew,
supported on the native seams. For every nonzero such $P$,
\[
   \|[H,P]\|_{\mathrm{HS}}>\tfrac{9}{40}\|P\|_{\mathrm{HS}} .
\]
At an intercell seam $ab$, each internal neighbor $v$ of $a$ gives
$[L,B]_{vb}=B_{va}-B_{ab}$ through one two-edge path.
The diagonal entry at $a$ also measures divergence. The quantitative
bound sums a positive rational Gram form over the cell interiors and
these paths. Five rotation classes cover all 220 placements of the three
gateways. Six signed currents are conserved when a cell is isolated.

A finite patch weights each internal seam by
$n\cdot(u_a\times u_b)$ in its measured rank-three frame. This
circulation is conserved in the isolated cell. After a phase-prepared
load is released on the full sphere, independent interventions at the
three gateways predict a home-cell slope of $0.256714$ per native
exposure. Uninterrupted flight gives $0.256713$. On one bridge the two
owners' measured torques sum to an interval $[0.24870,0.26890]$ at
simultaneous 99\% coverage. At the initial release its home torque is
$0.19021$ while the vacant neighbor has zero torque along every axis.
A rotation between the two local frames preserves that zero. The bound concerns the full one-load sector in native exposure
units. A covariant Ward law permits connection terms and requires physical
momentum, stress and frame transport on the common geometry.\footnote{The
\href{https://github.com/FloatingPragma/observer-patch-holography/tree/main/model/experiments/interval_echo_null_balance}{directional matter experiment}
gives the rational certificate, pulse controls and finite patch records.}

\paragraph{One reference on overlapping regions.}
Two six-port regions inherit the same four-port state from one prepared
cell. The restrictions agree for the reference and the flight
preparations. The regional effective generator can be forecast without
fitting the held matter reading. Trace the independently specified
parent Gibbs operator over the exterior and take the negative logarithm
on the remaining support. At a held exposure on the six-port region,
fresh records give $0.68605$ units for the modular charge,
$0.68542$ for the forecast and $0.12472$ for the interior seam meter.
A coherence between two nonadjacent ports has exactly zero expectation
difference for every sum of one-seam operators and number offsets.
Its measured modular difference is $+0.0045990\pm0.0000152$ nats
(95 percent half-width). In the parent Gibbs limit, closing the
cross-boundary contacts sets this coherence response to zero.
The measured operator thus depends on exterior repair even within a
consistent family of restrictions. For the two numerically represented parent matrices, finite quadratic-form
bounds survive partial trace and bound every normalized-state modular
contrast on all 4,096 port subsets by 0.001443 native units of
service-to-Gibbs error. The regional shapes have supplied chord radii
on one cell. This bound has no physical small-ball scaling assumption.

\paragraph{A directional modular observable on the slow sector.}
An observer restricts one rotation-averaged reference to each six-port
star of a serviced echosahedral cell. Let $n$ be the star's unit direction
in its measured rank-three frame. Its vacuum-centred modular logarithm is
real symmetric. Let $B$ be the isometry for the constant mode and first
triplet in their orthonormal scalar and vector basis. The rotation by
$2\pi/5$ fixing $n$ forces the mixed block to lie along $n$ and the transverse
block to be scalar. Transitivity gives common coefficients at all poles:
\[
 B^{\mathsf T} K(n) B =
 \begin{pmatrix} a & b n^{\mathsf T} \\ b n & c I_3+d n n^{\mathsf T} \end{pmatrix}.
\]
Normalized state variations span nine angular responses in the computed
reference (ninth singular value $0.292$). Nine prespecified readings
predict the remaining three to within $2.60$ propagated standard errors
in twelve held comparisons on two native flights and two preparations.
A sharp port gives a $5.99$-nat held miss at exposure $0.75$. The quadratic
description requires the stated preparation sector and reference symmetry.
A null frame and a physical stress identification are further inputs.\footnote{The
\href{https://github.com/FloatingPragma/observer-patch-holography/tree/main/model/experiments/interval_echo_angular}{directional modular experiment}
specifies the common preparation, register readouts and unrestricted-sector control.}

\paragraph{An exterior history can miss a regional entropy variation.}
On the 80- and 320-cell spheres one can prepare two stationary interior
modes that vanish at every gateway of the chosen cell. A single load
in either mode has the same complete exterior record under any
number-preserving interrogation confined to the exterior that preserves
its vacuum. The difference of the regional first-law slopes on a prepared
cap is $0.4410$ nats, with sampled value $0.4352\pm0.0089$.
An internal superposition provides a freely running clock while the
exterior stays silent. Its two measured quadratures give
$2.23699\pm0.00301$ radians per native exposure, consistent with
the independently calculated gap $\sqrt5$. Two held exposures agree
through more than one revolution. A second load entering the cell also
distinguishes the modes by its local interaction. Fixed-proper-volume
stationarity requires a physical
volume and area instrument whose records can distinguish the relevant
variations.

The clock and an entering probe can also be read on the same history.
At exposure $0.75$ the total variation between their joint record and
the product of its marginals is $0.124$. The sampled ensemble includes
trials in which both loads occupy the clock cell. In this preparation
reflection negates the hand while fixing the exterior, so both exterior
source contacts have zero joint-occupation amplitude at all exposures.
All nine source words consequently give identical terminal records.
A native phase pulse breaks the reflection and opens the contacts
weakly. These joint clock--probe records establish a finite material
comparison and identify the source access needed for a curvature test.

A separate radar observer on the 20-cell sphere uses six detector
addresses and a stationary two-level hand. Each leg ends at first
detection or after twelve interrogations. The probability of completing
both legs ranges from $0.30\%$ to $12.77\%$. With any one of the fifteen
pair distances withheld, the other ranges fail the anchor consistency
test for Euclidean trilateration. A separate Euclidean calibration passes
all fifteen tests. The model-exact fourth eigenvalue of the centred
squared-distance Gram matrix is $1.390$, implying a lower bound of
$2.86\%$ on the worst pairwise relative error of a flat three-dimensional
embedding for these conditional mean radar ranges. Independent multinomial
sampling of the complete flight and hand outcomes represents
$754{,}974{,}720$ simulated launches, of which $24{,}676{,}291$ return.
A sharper bound uses five of the six addresses. Throughout a box of
pairwise squared ranges their four-dimensional anchored Gram matrix
is affine; its minimum eigenvalue attains a lower bound at the $1024$
vertices. Positive definiteness at every vertex excludes any flat
three-coordinate fit to the exact means at $4.42663\%$ relative error.
Explicit coordinates for all six addresses attain $4.42665\%$.
Thus a fit within $5\%$ exists when all dyads calibrate the same chart.
On simultaneous $1-10^{-6}$ intervals from the independent counts,
the five-site test excludes a $3.4\%$ fit. All first-hit, timeout and hand
probabilities are identical across three signed source preparations,
although their interior record entropy has a first variation of
$\log(3)/12$ nats per unit oriented perturbation. These ranges therefore
provide neither a proper-volume normalization nor a curvature response
to this source.\footnote{The
\href{https://github.com/FloatingPragma/observer-patch-holography/tree/main/model/experiments/interval_echo_einstein_station}{clock and radar experiments}
give the complete enrolled cohorts and the geometry calibration.}

\paragraph{Which history can source a response?}
One cell retains a fair bit $Z$, its noisy collar word $C$, and a
two-load probe outcome $B$. For the reference flip probability $1/4$,
write $P_0$ for their joint law and $Q_0=P_0(Z,B)P_0(C\mid B)$ for
its conditional recovery. The signed record generator is
$E=-\log P_0+\log Q_0$ on their common support. At native contact
exposure $\pi/64$, changing the flip probability to $1/8$ or $3/8$
changes its expectation by $-0.09248$ or $+0.09248$ nats. The
distribution of $(C,B)$ stays fixed, including the mean stored energy.
At $\pi/32$ the probe resolves $C$ and $E$ vanishes. Thus a detector
confined to $(C,B)$ cannot respond to this signed variation. Assigning
it to stress in (G2) and (G6) requires a physical boost split, a
proper ball radius and a common geometric response to the joint history.\footnote{The
\href{https://github.com/FloatingPragma/observer-patch-holography/tree/main/model/experiments/material_inertia}{collar experiment}
specifies the supplied local gates and gives the independent finite reference and probe counts.}

A clocked two-contact probe also distinguishes a material response from
a common time change. Write $\chi_j(t)=\sum_{ab}p^j_{ab}
\exp[i(E_a-E_b)t]$ for its measured complex fringe in source arm $j$,
with contact energies $0,32,33$ in units of the concurrently serviced
local hand gap. Reversing an oriented cycle of source probabilities
preserves the real fringe. That fringe is strictly decreasing near
launch, so equality fixes any common-curve time reparametrization
there. The third frequency moments $528,352,176$ give unequal imaginary
fringes. Measured clock phases and independently sampled source
weights predict all 24 quadratures within $1.75$ propagated standard
errors. A local oriented contact accounts for the response; deriving a
shared metric requires a geometric measurement.

The matter readout also has an identifiable blind sector. On the constant
mode and the degenerate triplet, a normalized density variation has fifteen
real parameters. The real regional modular readings at all isolated flight
times span twelve. The missing three are the imaginary antisymmetric
triplet entries. Conjugate circular states agree on those complete local
readings but carry opposite oriented repair currents. Adding three
independent seam currents determines the remaining entries. After opening
the surrounding gateways, a held neighbouring energy meter reads
$1.53675\pm0.00375$ and $1.58114\pm0.00381$ in native units on the
two states, against forecasts of $1.53781$ and $1.58511$.
This supplies an independent oriented matter record on the specified
sector. Its spacetime normalization requires matching stress to
geometry on a refinement tower.

A local material radar responds to an oriented source change at fixed
ordinary load. Its measured mean half-trip changes by
$(3.83\pm2.13)\times10^{-6}$ native hand units (simultaneous 99\%
interval for two dyads). The predicted shift of $3.74\times10^{-6}$
persists at identical local addresses on 20-, 80- and 320-cell
spheres. Two complete detector outcomes change in opposite directions
under the oriented source, while adding ordinary load increases both.
The datum is a source-sensitive travel time, conditional on return.
Inferring curvature requires matched stress distributions and a
calibrated geometric comparison, including area at fixed proper volume.

Two separately written collar words can also retain a common preparation
history. Given the sign of a fair tag, the two draws are independent
with law $P_0+D$ or $P_0-D$. Discarding the tag leaves joint density
$P_0\otimes P_0+D\otimes D$. Preparing the signs independently gives
$P_0\otimes P_0$. The one-word marginals and parked ordinary loads agree.
One travelling probe distinguishes the joint preparations in its terminal
records. This is sensitivity to a correlation that survives discarding
the tag.

Its effect on initial tracer acceleration admits an independent test.
For a fixed word $x$ the native tracer Hamiltonian is $H_x=H_0+V_x$,
where $V_x$ is the sum of diagonal shifts on the four contact tips.
Let $F$ be the angular coordinate read from port occupancy, with zero
assigned to the recorded outside outcome. Since $[V_x,F]=0$,
\[
 \ddot F_x(0)=-[H_0,[H_0,F]]-[V_x,[H_0,F]] .
\]
Thus every common tracer preparation has the same initial mean acceleration
in the shared- and independent-history arms: the second term is linear
in $V_x$, whose mean is fixed by the marginals. In a differential test,
two real balanced tracers are released separately with zero initial
mean velocity in $F$. Adding ordinary occupancy changes their predicted
relative angular acceleration by $65/(3\sqrt5)=9.69$ per squared native
time unit. Changing the common history at fixed occupancy gives exactly
zero. The distinction holds before selecting any flight outcomes. This
calibrates a response of the particular angular instrument. A geodesic
interpretation requires an independent physical ruler and freely falling
worldlines on the same spacetime domain.

The Einstein conclusion applies only when (G1)--(G6) hold together on
one source, state and refinement tower. A static radial control has
vanishing radial null Einstein contractions while an admitted scalar
excitation has positive relative null stress, so it cannot balance both.
Stationary Markov or record entropy is not the fixed-volume
generalized-entropy variation that (G4) requires, and a finite modular
clock supplies no geometric modular dilation
(Section~\ref{sec:finite-modular-covariance}). The screen generator $A$
has spectral dimension two, and the $1+3$ scalar description uses a declared
radial extension. The Fibonacci population, read law and model clock of the
causal limit are premises that an executable process must produce.

A proof that no responsive geometry on the native process balances the null
stress of its own admitted excitations, or that its cap modular flow or record
entropy cannot meet (G2) or (G4) on any refinement tower, would refute this
gravitational branch. A refinement tower on which every component premise
holds while the composed Einstein relation fails would contradict the stated
implication.

\subsection*{A quantitative regional route to the Einstein equation}

The quantitative matched-regional theorem is a reusable route to the
Einstein conclusion. In units $\hbar=c=1$, use signature $(-,+,+,+)$
and the ten future unit timelike probes
\[
 (1,0,0,0),\qquad (5/3,\pm(4/3)e_i)\ (i=1,2,3),\qquad
 (3,2,2,0),\ (3,2,0,2),\ (3,0,2,2),
\]
where $e_i$ are the three spatial unit vectors in one common orthonormal
frame. For symmetric tensors $G,T$, put $C_\ell=4\pi\ell^4/15$ and require
the same regional readouts $s_r,a_r$ to satisfy the following bounds with
nonnegative errors:
\begin{align*}
 |s_r-2\pi C_\ell T(u_r,u_r)|&\leq e_{\mathrm{bulk}},\\
 |a_r+C_\ell(G(u_r,u_r)-\Lambda)|&\leq e_{\mathrm{area}},\\
 |s_r+\eta a_r|&\leq e_{\mathrm{stationarity}},\qquad \eta>0,\quad\ell>0.
\end{align*}
The exact finite decoder, with sharp constant $17/8$, gives
\[
 \max_{\mu,\nu}\left|G_{\mu\nu}+\Lambda g_{\mu\nu}
              -\frac{2\pi}{\eta}T_{\mu\nu}\right|
 \leq\frac{255}{32\pi\eta\ell^4}
       (e_{\mathrm{bulk}}+\eta e_{\mathrm{area}}+e_{\mathrm{stationarity}}).
\]
This implication does not separately assume null-tensor balance.
Its exact formal statement is
\path{Lean/ObserverPatchHolography/EinsteinBranch/QuantitativeSmallBall.lean};
the common-frame limit is specified in \path{code/matched_einstein_limit/PROOF.md}.
On a shared sequence $\ell_n\to0$, the errors must be $o(\ell_n^4)$,
$\eta_n\to\eta_*>0$, and the tensors and vacuum references must converge.
Identifying the geometry tensor with the Einstein tensor of an actual
nondegenerate Lorentz metric then gives $G_N=1/(4\eta_*)$ in the stated
units. Ward and Bianchi conservation, metric compatibility, connectedness
and spacetime-independent $\eta_*$ supply a constant vacuum term; its value
requires a reference.

Applying this implication requires a physical regional modular kernel, an
area functional, a fixed-proper-volume stationarity estimate and a
common-metric limit with these errors, together with compatible source,
reference and instrument refinement maps. These are attachment conditions,
without an impossibility claim for emergent tensor geometry.

For the Newtonian transfer set $E_{ab}=G_{ab}+\Lambda g_{ab}-8\pi G_NT_{ab}$
and use a local orthonormal frame in units $c=1$. Trace reversal gives
$R_{00}=(G_{00}+\sum_i G_{ii})/2$. At leading static weak-field order
$R_{00}=\Delta\Phi$, so
\[
 \Delta\Phi=4\pi G_N(\rho+\textstyle\sum_i p_i)-\Lambda
             +\tfrac12(E_{00}+\textstyle\sum_i E_{ii}).
\]
The density-only Poisson law uses negligible principal pressures and a
potential relative to the same vacuum. In this limit the common vacuum
has $\Phi_{\rm vac}=-\Lambda r^2/6$ up to an additive constant in the
regular spherical frame. If each component of $E$ has magnitude at most
$\epsilon_E$, its contribution to the local Poisson source is at most
$2\epsilon_E$. For a spherical residual supported inside radius $R$,
regularity at the origin gives
$|\delta\Phi'(r)|\leq2\epsilon_E R^3/(3r^2)$ for $r\geq R$ by radial
integration. The potential of this localized error may be normalized to
zero at infinity. That boundary convention applies to the error after
vacuum subtraction. Pressure, vacuum and higher weak-field orders must be
accounted for separately when using the bound on data.

\paragraph{A pressure-independent test with exterior clocks.}
\label{par:static-clock-mass}
For a first-order stationary perturbation about Minkowski space, include
the source and its supports in the conserved stress. Assume compact
support or sufficient falloff and subtract the same vacuum in both
preparations. Integrating
\(\partial_i(x_j\delta T_{ij})\) for each fixed \(j\) gives
\(\int\delta T_{jj}\,d^3x=0\), since the divergence and outer boundary
term vanish (\href{https://arxiv.org/abs/1808.09320}{von Laue's
theorem}). The local pressures may be large. Trace reversal and the
divergence theorem then give an observable consequence of physical null
balance, conserved matter and a common vacuum. For two concentric
background spheres of radii \(0<r<s\) outside the entire source,
\[
 \oint \partial_n\delta\Phi\,dA=4\pi G_N\delta E,\qquad
 \langle\delta\Phi\rangle_s-\langle\delta\Phi\rangle_r
   =G_N\delta E(1/r-1/s).
\]
The averages are over solid angle. The result requires no spherical
symmetry of the source. For a region ending within stressed matter,
the pressure integral includes the boundary moment
\(\oint x_j\delta T_{ij}n_i\,dA\).
The finite force identity and error bound are in
\texttt{StaticMassFlux}. If a clock gives one common response and the
errors in identifying the two mean lapse changes with it are each at
most \(e\), the putative mass signal is at most \(2e\) in magnitude
at first order.

A compact material oscillator on the board permits a direct test of
clock sensitivity. For an unmarked cell with adjacency \(A\) and
gateway selector \(D\), solve \(A^2v=5v\) and \(Dv=DAv=0\).
The two vectors \(Av\pm\sqrt5 v\) have zero gateway amplitude and
opposite adjacency eigenvalues. Extended by zero, they remain
eigenmodes of each exact charge and stored-head parity block, with
angular-frequency gap \(\sqrt5\) in exchange units. Ordinary parity
mixing raises the stationary source's parked energy by
\([0.2975627,0.2975636]\) per unit dose. Unpairing preserves this
energy. Both give exactly zero change of the gap in all 24 eligible
exterior cells of the 80-cell source. There are 104 eligible cells on
the 320-cell board and none on the 20-cell board.

Coherent superpositions of the same level at two and eight relay steps
from the source are recombined at a single pointer. Subtracting the two
level phases compares the clock rates without synchronizing separate
receivers. With \(2^{19}\) synthetic shots per setting, the measured
difference is \(-0.00122\). Raising the internal exchange rate of the
near clock by a quarter gives \(-0.55839\), against the exact
\(-\sqrt5/4\). The simultaneous 99 percent sampling half-width is
0.02282. Equating the ordinary parked-energy change with the complete
localized gravitational source and identifying these clocks with a
resolved covering of both proper spheres would conflict with the
exact zero response. The available relay distances do not establish
that covering. The derivation and instrument records are in
\path{code/matched_einstein_limit/source_implication/material_clocks/}.

\section*{Derivation map}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Derivation map}

Normalized repair response gives one positive rank-three source Gram
quotient $E$. Signed cumulative records and conservative seam words embed
densely in its Euclidean completion. Its unit directions form $S^2$;
adjoining a scalar line gives $\mathbb R\oplus E$ with quadratic form
$t^2-\|x\|^2$. This connects the source metric to a four-dimensional
Lorentz carrier without selecting a physical event clock. The conditional
implications on this carrier are
\[
\begin{aligned}
\substack{\text{nonzero closed convex pointed cone}\\
\text{finite irreducible operational rotations}\\
\text{one continuous boost direction}}
&\Longrightarrow
\substack{\text{Lorentz displacement cone}\\
\text{up to time orientation}},\\[1mm]
\substack{\text{conservative Fibonacci populations}\\
\text{complete local reads and }\Delta=a/c}
&\Longrightarrow
\substack{\text{flat causal-order limit}\\
\text{raw count--volume convergence}},\\[1mm]
\substack{\text{controlled order/count limit}\\
\text{accessible interval records}\\
\text{one reference interval}}
&\Longrightarrow
\text{proper-time ratios from counts},\\[1mm]
\text{positive translations and finite null tomography}
&\Longrightarrow
\text{conditional conserved }\langle T_{ab}\rangle,\\[1mm]
\substack{\text{physical stress and universal coupling}\\
\text{entropy, small-ball and tensor-curvature limits}}
&\Longrightarrow
G_{ab}+\Lambda g_{ab}=8\pi G\langle T_{ab}\rangle .
\end{aligned}
\]
These implications have distinct hypotheses. Operational boost covariance
acts on admissible histories and interventions; an algebraic action on the
celestial sphere does not establish it. Finite proper carrier rotations and
one continuous boost of the declared quadratic form select its light cone with
unit speed in the scalar coordinate
(Theorem~\ref{thm:operational-cone-selection}). The signature and the time
axis are declared, so the theorem selects the invariant cone of a chosen
Lorentz form rather than deriving Lorentz structure.

\paragraph{Causal order and counts.}
Put $\varphi=(1+\sqrt5)/2$ and $L=2/\sqrt{\varphi+2}$. For consecutive
Fibonacci numbers $q=F_n$ and $p=F_{n+1}$, $n\geq4$, the $q^3$ spatial records
have a source-metric assignment to equal-volume cells with error
$H_q=2\sqrt3L/q$. Each event reads every preceding-layer record inside radius
$a_q=L/\sqrt q$, including its own preceding record, and the model layer
duration is $\Delta_q=a_q/c$, with $c>0$. Segment subdivision bounds the
reachable radius between $c(1-2h_q/a_q)T$ and $cT$; since $h_q\leq H_q$ and
$H_q/a_q\to0$, these cones agree in the limit away from their null boundary.
Normalized raw counts converge to $dt\,d^3x$ volume
(Proposition~\ref{prop:golden-source-count-limit}), and on fixed interior
timelike intervals the ordering fraction tends to $1/10$. The finite path
construction, the layered order, the count-clock enclosure, the
generated-interval and strict-pair count limits and the $1/10$ limit for
diamonds inside the sampling window are formalized in Lean; the general
moving-tip volume-error estimate is an analytic proof. The family is a
discretization of flat $1+3$ space, with the ball read as a light cone and a
layer as a time slice, so its limit theorems confirm the discretization rather
than select the dimension: the spatial dimension three comes from the
rank-three carrier, and the time axis is adjoined. Under the same count limit,
the observer readout $(|I|/|J|)^{1/4}$ converges to the proper-time ratio of
two interior timelike diamonds; it reads accessible ancestry and counts, with
a reference interval fixing units
(Theorem~\ref{thm:source-count-clock},
Proposition~\ref{prop:source-count-clock-error}). Finite readings of the
family are in Appendix~\ref{app:source-causal-order}.

Exact order embeddings form a separate finite interface. For a source spatial
readback and positive height scale, a parent-edge speed bound maps
authenticated ancestry into the future cone; equal-height spatial separation
and strict spacelike separation of increasing-height nonancestors give the
converse, hence an injective order embedding. The source height is the
attained longest authenticated-parent-chain length. Height is not identified
with proper time, or element number with volume; a causal-set faithful
embedding also requires the appropriate population
density\cite{bombelli1987,surya2019}. Under the continuum causality
hypotheses, causal order and volume determine conformal geometry and
scale\cite{hawkingkingmccarthy1976,malament1977}.

\paragraph{Which recorded orders fit this cone?}
Two remote stations read both the loads and the last writers on each port of
a twelve-port cell. Short flights recover its thirty contacts independently.
Place each contact at the midpoint of its two port vectors and assign each
event time proportional to its longest writer chain. Every ordered history
of up to three distinct contacts admits a positive clock scale. Among the
657,720 four-contact histories, 579,960 admit one, 4,800 put an unrelated
pair exactly on the null boundary, and 72,960 violate a required inequality
strictly. Intervention at the first of two matched endpoints transmits one
unit through a live bridge and zero through the muted bridge, even though
the endpoints on an empty host have identical local records in both arms.

There is also a coordinate-independent obstruction for this time convention.
Five disjoint native contacts can send any selected subset of their records
to a gathering seam across thirty-two continuations. A second instrument
writes five independent root bits and records the parity of every subset on
its own shelf within a \emph{single} execution. Flipping each input in turn
identifies the required writers of each output. Two independent heads recover
all eighty direct dependencies. In a common time-zero slice, a future cone
meets the five roots in a closed ball. Five points in three dimensions admit
a nonzero affine dependence. The difference of the squared-distance scores
of two balls is affine; summing it with the dependence weights gives zero.
Balls selecting the positive and negative weight groups would instead give a
strict signed difference. Thus even a retrospective choice of all spatial
positions cannot embed the authenticated parity log with its canonical
heights.

A larger instrument removes the clock assumption. Its seven roots and 128
parity outputs coexist in every one of 128 launches. The receivers recover
448 direct dependencies per log. Give every event an arbitrary position and
time and choose a root assigned the greatest time. A query containing that
root lies at or after every root time. On the six remaining roots, membership
in the past cone is then equivalent to nonnegative squared Minkowski
interval. Their four-dimensional coordinates are affinely dependent. Two
queries containing that root and opposite weight groups would again
give a nonzero weighted sum for the difference of their interval scores, an
affine function. Hence this executed semantic order has no exact flat
$1+3$ cone realization even with free times. The event menu records which
inputs determine each output. A physical identification must specify which
measured propagation events form its causal order.

\paragraph{Source metric.}
For the declared twelve-port repair mean, the centered equal-port response
kernel has an exact spectral decomposition. Dividing by its slow response
before the infinite-step limit selects a rank-three projector whose range,
with the induced Gram form, is an intrinsic Euclidean carrier. Every
finite-step kernel has rank eleven on the centered space and rank six on the
signed sector. On the signed cumulative port-record/load module the limiting
form has a three-dimensional radical on the real six-control extension; the
integer module has no nonzero metric kernel, embeds densely in the quotient,
and completes to the same carrier up to the unique label-preserving isometry.
The source load map is onto \(\mathbb Z^6\), with the antipodally equal loads
as its kernel. Conservative seam boundaries have image
\[
 D_6=\{z\in\mathbb Z^6:\textstyle\sum_i z_i\equiv0\pmod 2\},
\]
with Smith invariants \((1,1,1,1,1,2)\); the pullback response metric makes
this submodule dense in the same completion. Cumulative record addition acts
simply transitively on the record carrier and by isometries on its
completion. The sixty directed seam labels form one proper carrier-rotation
orbit. The thirteen finite covariance and isometry table identities of the
proper-action subpacket are checked with Lean's \texttt{native\_decide}; they
trust the native compiler and runtime as well as the kernel and are not
kernel-only receipts. The source metric does not choose a preferred Cartesian
frame or identify record addition with physical translation.

\paragraph{Finite tests for chart transitions.}
Let $u_p$ be the twelve exact source vectors, of squared radius
$R^2=\varphi+2$, and declare $k_p=(1,u_p)$ and
$G=\operatorname{diag}(-R^2,1,1,1)$. The source tensor decoder has rank nine
and kernel $\mathbb RG$ on symmetric four-matrices. Thus a supplied invertible
real map $L$ passes all twelve null tests $(Lk_p)^TG(Lk_p)=0$ exactly when
\[
 L^TGL=\lambda G\qquad\hbox{for a unique }\lambda>0.
\]
The kernel gives proportionality; inertia gives positivity. Normalizing by
$\sqrt\lambda$ gives a Lorentz isometry. Future preservation requires
$L_{00}>0$, and properness requires $\det L>0$ separately: parity and time
reversal pass the null tests, and a singular null collapse shows why
invertibility is necessary. Conformal factors multiply under composition.
For any symmetric $B$, the same exact decoder gives
\[
 \max_{i,j}|B_{ij}-(B_{00}/G_{00})G_{ij}|
 \leq(1-\sqrt5/5)\max_p|k_p^TBk_p|.
\]
This bounds a metric residual, not distance to a Lorentz transformation.
The criterion supplies no source-derived chart transition or physical-null
interpretation.

\centering

Balance on the nine design directions is equivalent to balance on every null vector, and the tensor step is machine-checked from that premise. \paragraph{Einstein shape.}
The minimal source-direction Einstein-shape theorem places supplied symmetric
geometry and stress fields on the same finite event type. It asks for balance
on nine fixed algebraic source directions, four discrete step maps,
Ward/Bianchi identities and step connectedness. Exact tomography derives
all-null balance, the pointwise metric ambiguity, its constancy, and
\[
 \mathsf{geometry}_{ab}
   =\kappa\,\mathsf{stress}_{ab}+\Lambda\eta_{ab}.
\]
The nine-set is algebraic, not selected by provenance or invariant as a set
under Lorentz transformations. Identifying \(\mathsf{geometry}\) with smooth
curvature and \(\mathsf{stress}\) with physical stress-energy requires the
source-causal continuum, count--volume, stress, small-ball, coupling, scale
and remainder receipts on one family. The timelike method identifies the
Einstein tensor from matched small-ball measurements at fixed proper
volume. A direct curvature reconstruction can instead be paired with
stress on the nine null directions. Scalar-curvature convergence alone is
diagnostic. In the machine-checked null composition the premise is the null-contracted Einstein
relation itself, or nine of its null components; the composition adds the
constancy of the metric residue, the vacuum reference fixing $\Lambda$ and the
$8\pi G$ label. The small-ball step is Jacobson's entanglement-equilibrium
argument, each of whose inputs enters as a receipt.

The modular route requires both a geometric cap-flow comparison and a
compatible algebra-state limit. Finite support incidence alone supplies
neither the state nor the geometric $2\pi$ normalization. The selected
comparison must identify the same cap charges, stress tensor and clock
throughout refinement. For a faithful Gibbs reference of a declared finite
Hamiltonian one has the exact identity
\[
 S(\rho)-S(\tau)
 =\beta\operatorname{Tr}((\rho-\tau)H)-D(\rho\Vert\tau),
 \qquad \tau=Z^{-1}e^{-\beta H}.
\]
This supplies a finite entropy--energy relation. It does not select
$\beta$, identify a reduced regional state with the Gibbs state of its bare
local Hamiltonian, or supply geometric entropy stationarity.

\paragraph{The separate scalar construction.}
On a declared radial product over its refining spherical source, the native
wave action of the separate scalar construction, which is distinct from the
scalar channel of the executable model, has coefficient density
$C=\mu\,\mathrm{diag}(-1,a)$, and $g=\sqrt{-\det C}\,C^{-1}$ has signature
$(-,+,+,+)$ almost everywhere. Smoothing the angular coefficients converges
in the norm-resolvent sense, so every fixed finite menu of observer readouts
is approximated by one smooth static $(1+3)$-dimensional Lorentzian metric
carrying a free scalar field
(Proposition~\ref{prop:smooth-effective-descriptions},
Appendix~\ref{app:smooth-effective-descriptions}). The radial extension is a
declared design input, and the readouts of the theorem do not select it.
This description asserts no exact smooth metric of the screen, no responsive
geometry and no physical stress, and discharges none of (G2)--(G6). It is the
static geometry that fails null balance for its own scalar excitation.

\paragraph{Matching the field principal cone.}
The causal order and the field must share a clock and spatial dictionary.
For a positive spatial principal tensor $a$, the static field symbol is
$-\omega^2+\xi^Ta\xi$; its dual tangent form is
$-dt^2+dx^Ta^{-1}dx$. Supply an invertible readback Jacobian $dx=J\,dz$
and a positive clock conversion $dt=\alpha\,d\tau$. The twelve null tests
above are equivalent to
\[
 K=\operatorname{diag}(-\alpha^2,J^Ta^{-1}J)
   =\frac{\alpha^2}{R^2}G,
 \qquad a=\frac{R^2}{\alpha^2}JJ^T.
\]
This criterion is nonvacuous: for any positive $a$, the pointwise choice
$J=(\alpha/R)a^{1/2}$ works. It does not show that this Jacobian is the
observer's readback or that a varying frame integrates to a global chart.
A common invertible frame change preserves the equality by congruence;
transforming only one side changes the proposed identification.

The unwarped radial product illustrates the distinction. In its round
control $A=\kappa(-\Delta_{S^2})$, the fixed map $x=rn$ gives spatial
tangent form
\[
 H_r=nn^T+(\kappa r^2)^{-1}(I-nn^T).
\]
One global clock scaling matches flat radial and tangential cones only if
$\alpha^2=R^2$ and $\kappa r^2=1$. Distinct positive radii cannot both
satisfy this.
This excludes that fixed polar identification, not the product's curved
Lorentzian description, other coordinates or local orthonormal frames.
No radial action is replaced. Applying the criterion requires
event placements and the same field's actual clock and readback from one
source. Principal-cone agreement alone fixes no lower-order dynamics:
$-\omega^2+\xi^Ta\xi+m^2$ has the same principal cone for every
$m^2\geq0$.

\paragraph{A radial readout tested on the free federation.}
In the Interval Echo experiment, six disjoint internal seams per cell carry
signed contrast $d=\pm2$. A mobile load of amount $q=1$ or $2$ occupies a
single port. The native write with $|d|=2$ has endpoint loads $(1,1)$,
orthogonal to this entire sector. On a signed seam the echo therefore acts
as the identity, and the sign evolves independently even when the load visits
an endpoint. Two quadrature preparations of the sign give a stationary patch
hand of angle $\theta=bt$, where $b$ multiplies exchange. The other seams
have blank memory. In hand units the kinetic operator is $K=L_{\rm active}/2$,
where the active graph omits the six signed bonds per cell. The contrast and
the original degree references contribute the scalar $5\kappa q^2/b$.
All terms act throughout flight.

Let $P$ average over the twelve ports of each cell. Removing a matching
subtracts an internal Laplacian of norm two from the icosahedral Laplacian.
Positivity of the external contacts gives
$K\geq g(I-P)$ with $g=(3-\sqrt{5})/2$. For energy $E=\langle K\rangle$
the weight $\eta$ orthogonal to cell averages is at most $E/g$ at every
exposure. Consider any partition of each cell into two depth bins of six
ports each. The signed difference operator $D$ for these bins, with
independent sign choices in each angular region, has $PDP=0$ and norm one.
The joint region and depth distribution is within
$\sqrt{\eta(1-\eta)}+\eta/2$ in total variation of its equal-depth
distribution with the same angular marginal. A finite depth contrast cannot
survive on any family with $E\to0$ in this encoding. The bound permits
polarized, finite-energy preparations with full depth contrast. It concerns
a particular detector dictionary for the interval in
Proposition~\ref{prop:smooth-effective-descriptions}.

There is also a bound for arbitrary quantum instruments on the ports.
For $\eta<1$, the unit cell-mean wave $\chi=P\psi/\|P\psi\|$ has overlap
$\sqrt{1-\eta}$ with the incident wave. Their pure-state trace distance
is $\sqrt{\eta}$. Tensoring the same clock state, applying an instrument
and recording its complete outcome cannot increase this distance. Thus two
preparations with the same normalized cell means up to phase, each of energy
at most $e<g$, give distributions at that exposure within
$2\sqrt{e/g}$ in total variation. A coherent port analyzer cannot recover
an independent soft depth label hidden from the occupation reader. The
bound compares incident states at the readout and allows distinct cell-mean
waves, including different angular phases, to be distinguished.

For a cell-uniform source whose amplitude varies by at most $h/2$ across
each external contact before normalization and equals one on one twentieth
of the cells, $E\leq 5h^2/16$ when the amplitude on a cell is replicated
on its twelve ports. Spectral calculus gives
$\|(e^{-i\theta K}-I)\psi\|^2\leq2|\theta|E$. At $\theta=O(h^{-1})$ the
complete joint record approaches that of a stationary load with the same
running clocks. This bound makes no assertion at $\theta=O(h^{-2})$.
The executable implementation, clock preparations and finite tests are in
\texttt{model/experiments/interval\_echo\_space}.

Kinematic and Newtonian precursors are finite identities. On the declared
Hermitian Lorentz module, $p=m\,u$ has Lorentz square $m^2$ and
$p_0^2=m^2+|\mathbf p|^2$ in every oriented chart; no coordinate is identified
with a physical energy. For a supplied integer layering, steady source law,
shell equidistribution and shell count $c n^2$ through a finite depth, exact
divergence bookkeeping gives outward flux $Q/(c n^2)$ per shell edge, while on
a closed finite carrier an $n^2$ shell count at every positive layer forces
$c=0$, so the depth cutoff is explicit.

A finite observer experiment realizes these inputs together. Three
guarded loads on different cells mark positions on three twelve-port
paths. A fourth register marks release at the central source. An
independent ranging instrument advances the three coordinates in
parallel, so a shell of radius $n$ encloses $(2n)^3$ address triples.
The current instrument offers one coordinate step at a time. Each of
the six faces has $(2n)^2$ outward steps. The independently surveyed
aperture counts through five shells were $24,96,216,384,600$.

Before each step the apparatus moves the triple to a uniformly
selected address on its measured shell, using routes entirely within
that shell. Six of eight equally likely tags offer a signed coordinate
step and the other two idle. A separate collector absorbs the triple
at shell six. Ordinary conservation fixes the total steady flux at each
cut to the source rate $Q$. After mixing, every individual address of
shell $n$ has the same occupation $u_n$, so each outward edge carries
the same net flux $(u_n-u_{n+1})/8$. The measured apertures then
give $Q/(24n^2)$ per edge. The two source and collector appointments
and the mean forty active appointments per completed cycle give
$Q=1/42$. Independent simulated release and aperture banks gave
$0.0238252$ and $0.0238211$ per compound appointment.
In a control assigning both idle tags to the first axis, total flux
was again conserved across every shell, while the mean outer-edge
current on that axis was twice that on either other axis.

The range here is measured in a product of three address records.
On the intact federation the graph-distance shells of all 5,040
possible origins on the 20-, 80- and 320-cell boards fail the quadratic
aperture count at the first two positive radii. A measured inverse-square
transport current on the prepared register supplies the finite shell
identity. A gravitational interpretation requires independent readings
of physical distance, source mass and probe acceleration.

\section{The modular input to gravitational response}
\label{sec:modular-einstein-input}

\begin{theorem}[Conditional cap modular normalization]
\label{thm:conditional-cap-modular}
Let one cofinal refinement tower have a common support-visible geometric
cap algebra and state in its mixed-GNS limit. Suppose its finite collars
have exact Markov replacements or controlled faithful Markov comparison,
the local algebra-state comparisons and replacements are compatible under
refinement, and modular action is readable as a map of cap supports.
Suppose further that a cap-normal certificate supplies oriented
cross-ratio rigidity and an independent geometric $2\pi$-KMS comparison.
Then the limiting modular automorphism of each geometric cap is its
cap-preserving conformal flow with the $2\pi$ normalization. This is an
automorphism statement on the limit algebra; a literal
$K_C=2\pi B_C+Z_C$ representation requires an additional type-I
realization, with $Z_C$ central.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}[Proof outline]
Controlled Markov replacement confines the finite modular defect to the
collar, with the faithful-collar comparison controlling its matrix elements
on fixed local observables. Projective compatibility and mixed-GNS
convergence define one limiting cap pair independently of a chosen
comparison sequence. Support readability turns its modular action into a
cap-support flow. Oriented cross-ratio rigidity identifies this flow with
the cap-preserving conformal one up to parameter scale; the independent
KMS comparison fixes that scale to $2\pi$. None of these steps identifies
the full algebra with a type-I factor or derives the certificate from the
unchanged native repair law.
\end{proof}

The corresponding half-sided null inclusion has a positive translation
generator $P_\Omega$ and, on its common quadratic-form core, the affine
relation $K_a(\Omega)=K_0(\Omega)-2\pi aP_\Omega$. Reading its endpoint
derivative as a local charge $\int T_{kk}$ requires effective locality and
an identification with the \emph{same} stress tensor that propagates the
matter fields. Affine/projective interval transport then produces the
ball kernel used below, provided the endpoint and collar remainders meet
the displayed small-ball bound. Thus cap modular covariance, the local
stress reading and the entropy--area response are three separately
testable inputs; the finite record entropy identity by itself supplies
none of their physical identifications.

\section{Null balance and the Einstein tensor}
\label{sec:einstein-tensor}

The tensor theorem below uses a balance premise supplied by independent
geometry and matter measurements along null directions. The timelike
small-ball construction above obtains the tensor from entropy and area
on shrinking proper-volume balls.

\begin{theorem}[Null tomography]
\leavevmode\par\raggedright
Let $V$ be four-dimensional with Lorentz form
$\eta=\operatorname{diag}(-1,1,1,1)$. A symmetric form $X$ vanishes on every
null square exactly when $X=\phi\eta$. Nine null directions suffice to
recover $X$ modulo this line: the six $(1,\pm e_i)$ and
$(1,s,s,s),(1,s,s,-s),(1,s,-s,s)$, where $s=1/\sqrt3$.
The corresponding exact decoder has
$\|x\|_\infty\leq(2+\sqrt3)\|q\|_\infty$ in its registered coordinates.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
On the unit sphere, $X(1,n;1,n)=X_{00}+2X_{0i}n_i+X_{ij}n_in_j$.
Opposite directions remove the mixed temporal entries. The six axis
values fix the spatial diagonal modulo the metric, and the three
remaining sign choices fix the spatial off-diagonal entries. Thus the
kernel consists exactly of the metric line. Solving this finite system
gives the stated decoder bound; its determinant and identities are also
checked in the finite theorem library \cite{ophrepo}.
\end{proof}

\paragraph{Quantitative null reconstruction.}
The explicit nine-direction inverse has sharp maximum entrywise error
constant $2+\sqrt3$: a uniform null-charge error $\epsilon$ gives a
trace-free tensor error at most $(2+\sqrt3)\epsilon$ in every entry.
Ward residuals propagate the remaining metric coefficient along the actual
difference graph. Centered differences require two-step connectivity;
checkerboard coefficients supply an exact counterexample to replacing it
with nearest-neighbour connectivity. A vacuum or trace input is
necessary to determine the metric coefficient.
For a Ward residual bounded by $w$, spacing $a$ and null-charge error
$\epsilon$, adjacent coefficients satisfy
$|c(x+e_j)-c(x)|\leq aw+8(2+\sqrt3)\epsilon$ for forward differences.
Centered differences instead control
$|c(x+e_j)-c(x-e_j)|\leq2aw+8(2+\sqrt3)\epsilon$.
Finite decoder error bounds are machine-checked in
\path{Lean/ObserverPatchHolography/EinsteinResidualBound.lean}.

\begin{theorem}[Einstein equation from a common null balance]
Let $(M,g)$ be a connected time-oriented $C^3$ Lorentzian four-manifold and
$T$ a symmetric $C^1$ tensor with $\nabla^aT_{ab}=0$. Fix a single
$\kappa=8\pi G_N>0$. If
\[
 G_{ab}k^ak^b=\kappa T_{ab}k^ak^b
\]
for every null $k$ at every point, then there is a constant $\Lambda$ such
that $G_{ab}+\Lambda g_{ab}=\kappa T_{ab}$.
Balance on the nine directions in any orthonormal frame suffices.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
Null tomography gives $G-\kappa T=\phi g$ pointwise. The contracted Bianchi
identity, the independently supplied Ward identity and metric compatibility
give $\nabla\phi=0$. Connectedness makes $\phi$ constant; set
$\Lambda=-\phi$. Its value requires one reference condition.
\end{proof}

\paragraph{Reading a null residual with three clocks.}
\label{par:ray-pair-transfer}
Fix a spatial direction $n$ of unit length in the reconstructed frame.
Restrict the symmetric residual $G+\Lambda g-\kappa T$ to the plane
spanned by the rest clock and $n$. Write its quadratic form as
$Q(t,x)=dt^2+2jtx+px^2$. Three future unit timelike observations give
\[
 Q(1,1)=-\tfrac9{16}Q(1,0)
 +\tfrac{81}{160}Q(5/3,4/3)+\tfrac9{160}Q(5/3,-4/3).
\]
Expansion gives the identity; reversal of $n$ gives the incoming ray.
Errors at most $\epsilon$ on the three residuals bound the null error by
$9\epsilon/8$. All terms refer to the same point, state, coupling and
reference. Thus the fixed-volume regional estimates above can prove null
balance without resolving the flight of a macroscopic light ray. Nine
spanning directions then suffice for tensor completion. Calibrating a
stress from the curvature on each ray would impose the target identity.
The \hyperref[par:bisected-null-curvature]{bisected-clock measurement}
constructs the curvature side from marked volumes on proper diamonds.

At a faithful finite reference the empirical content has an equivalent
linear form. Differentiate the geometry reader and the independently
calibrated stress reader on the admissible state variations. Null balance
to first order means that their difference, with the common coupling,
annihilates this entire tangent space on each spanning ray. For all
traceless Hermitian variations this holds precisely when the difference
of the two derivative observables is scalar. More generally the
difference must lie in the span of the constraint gradients when the
tangent space is their joint kernel. This finite criterion applies
locally on the specified state family; continuation of a first-order
identity requires control along that family and a reference value.

\paragraph{A rest-slice ambiguity and a clock measurement that resolves it.}
Consider finitely many supported station clocks at fixed spatial coordinates
in Minkowski spacetime, with one at the origin. Set
\(g_f=e^{2f}g_0\), where \(f=t(b\cdot x)\psi(x)\) and the smooth cutoff
equals one near the origin and vanishes near the other stations. At every
appointment all station clocks have their original proper time.
Unparametrized null paths and their endpoint events are unchanged.
The complete intrinsic spatial metric, including every ball area and
volume, is unchanged on \(t=0\). Yet at the origin \(df=0\),
\(f_{0i}=b_i\), and the four-dimensional conformal Ricci formula gives
\(G_f(k,k)=-4b\cdot n\) for \(k=(1,n)\), \(|n|=1\). Taking \(T=0\)
gives an independently conserved stress in both descriptions and
different null residuals for the same clock and slice observations.
This is an identifiability test for geometric light-flight times.
The arrival probabilities of a dispersive material probe can contain
other information. The general inverse problem for light observation
sets recovers conformal structure under stated causal hypotheses
(\href{https://arxiv.org/abs/1405.3386}{Kurylev, Lassas and Uhlmann}).

Given a reference optical metric \(\bar g\), additional interior clock
measurements can determine the missing scale in \(g=w^{-2}\bar g\).
At a central event normalized by \(w=1\), along a \(\bar g\)-affine
null ray one has \(R_g(k,k)=R_{\bar g}(k,k)+2w''(0)\).
Clocks in known timelike directions give \(w=d\bar\tau/d\tau_g\).
If three such readings at ray parameters \(-h,0,h\) have errors
at most \(\epsilon\), the centered estimate of \(2w''(0)\) has error
at most \(Mh^2/6+8\epsilon/h^2\) when \(|w^{(4)}|\le M\).
The first term follows from Taylor's formula and the second from the
absolute stencil weights. This controls the scale contribution. The
reference curvature, ray normalization and directional matter stress
each require their own calibration on the same source.

\paragraph{Static null curvature from directional shell areas.}
There is a static example even with freely falling clocks at every
enrolled station. Let
\(f=a(x_1^2-x_2^2)\chi(|x|)/2\), with a smooth radial cutoff equal to
one near the center and vanishing in a neighborhood of every other
station. In the two metrics \(g_\pm=e^{\pm2f}\eta\) all station clock
rates, proper accelerations and interstation optical travel times
agree for all appointments. Interchanging the first two spatial axes is
an isometry between the induced spatial metrics fixing the center.
All centered geodesic balls therefore have exactly equal total proper
volume and rim area at every normal radius. On the same labeled ray
their null Ricci values at the center are
\(\mp2a(n_1^2-n_2^2)\). Whole-shell scalars leave a directional
component unresolved even under staticity.

The obstruction persists for first-order stationarity on \emph{all}
static rest balls in an open set. Write \(g_a=e^{2au}\eta\) and take
\(u=(x_1^2-x_2^2)/2\) in the core of the cutoff. At a fixed endpoint
\(x+rn\) the first variation of the straight geodesic's length about
\(a=0\) is \(\int_0^r u(x+sn)\,ds\). At fixed proper radius the
coordinate boundary shifts by its negative. Including variation of the
spatial volume form, one obtains
\[
 V'_0(x,r)=\int_{S^2}\left[3\int_0^r s^2u(x+sn)\,ds
    -r^2\int_0^r u(x+sn)\,ds\right]d\Omega=0 .
\]
For every ball inside the core the spherical mean of this harmonic
quadratic is \(u(x)\). Since \(A_a=\partial_r V_a\) in a normal
neighborhood, the first variation of its area at fixed proper volume
also vanishes. With \(T=0\) and zero matter entropy, stress is
conserved and generalized entropy is stationary on every such ball.
Yet \(\partial_a R_{g_a}(k,k)|_0=-2(n_1^2-n_2^2)\) about the flat vacuum.
Thus the all-frame hypothesis carries physical information beyond even
complete rest-slice stationarity. The example concerns sufficiency of
the geometric inputs and makes no microscopic source identification.

One can measure that component with directional shell and clock data.
For \(g=-N^2dt^2+h\), write \(j(r,n)\) for the proper area per unit
solid angle on a spatial geodesic sphere, and \(L(r,n)\) for the rim
clock rate divided by the center rate. Use antipodal means of both.
The transverse Jacobi equation gives
\(j/r^2=1-r^2\operatorname{Ric}_h(n,n)/6+O(r^3)\).
The centered second difference of the clock gives the normalized
spatial Hessian of the lapse. Write the two estimates as
\(D_n=-6(j/r^2-1)/r^2\) and \(L_n=2(L-1)/r^2\).
The static connection gives \(R_{00}=N\Delta_hN\),
\(R_{0i}=0\), and
\(R_{ij}=\operatorname{Ric}^h_{ij}-N^{-1}\nabla_i\nabla_jN\).
Thus \(D_n+3\bar L-L_n\) converges to \(R(k,k)\) for
\(k=N^{-1}\partial_t+n\), where \(\bar L\) is the mean over the six
icosahedral axes of a common orthonormal frame. The area and clock
errors including aperture and radius calibration must be \(o(r^4)\)
and \(o(r^2)\), respectively. No field equation enters this inference.

A native six-port aperture of equal weights contains a representative
of each antipodal pair. All such apertures have identical response to
an even angular distribution. Doubling the pole weight changes its
normalized reading to \(m_i=(6\bar q+q_i)/7\), so all six directional
values are recovered by \(q_i=7m_i-6\bar m\). On one serviced cell
the ordinary apertures have exact probability \(1/2\) for both an axial
orbital and a transverse mixture in its equal-energy triplet.
With pole score one and neighboring-port score one half the same
terminal records give 0.37535 and 0.25013 at the pole, against
\(3/8\) and \(1/4\). This establishes native angular discrimination.
On a separately prescribed product of a curved two-surface and a line,
the shell and clock procedure recovers both signs of null Ricci to
0.00021 at radius \(1/16\). Geometric calibration of the native sector
areas and rim clocks requires additional spatial and temporal records.
The calculations are in \path{code/matched_einstein_limit/rate_geometry/}.


\paragraph{A current that a passive exterior cannot reconstruct.}
\label{par:ray-pair-native-current}
On each of the 20-, 80- and 320-cell Interval Echo boards, cell zero
admits two orthonormal real modes $u,v$ of the cell Laplacian with
eigenvalue six and zeros on every gateway. Extension by zero makes them
exact eigenvectors of the free board generator at kinetic energy three
above its uniform orbital. The stationary releases $(u\pm iv)/\sqrt2$
therefore have identical vacuum histories on the passive exterior.
Inside the cell two quadrature projections on seam $(4,5)$ resolve
opposite kinetic-energy currents with magnitude $\sqrt3/4$. The
number current is $\operatorname{Im}(\bar\psi_4\psi_5)$ in the native
orientation. The symmetrized kinetic-energy current on an eigenstate is
its energy times that number current. These currents circulate within
the cell. Their divergence vanishes.

Suppose a proposed local frame assigns $T_{0n}=\alpha J$ to this seam
with the same nonzero conversion on both releases, and computes geometry
from the passive exterior alone. Its geometric mixed component is the
same on both states. Reversing a ray cancels all even residual terms and
leaves $4(G_{0n}-\kappa T_{0n})$. At least one of the four state--ray
residuals consequently has magnitude at least
$\sqrt3\,|\kappa\alpha|/2$. This excludes that pair of instruments on
these states for any positive coupling. Coarser observers may average
away the circulating current; an active volume reconstruction can retain
additional records. The actual rest-frame stress and responsive geometry
of such observers require their own identification.

\subsection{Reconstruction from clocks, radar and a scalar field}

On a controlled homogeneous source an observer can recover a coupled
field and geometry from local records. Take a diagonal Bianchi~I metric
with scale factors $a_i(t)$ and a spatially uniform canonical scalar
$\phi(t)$ with potential $m^2\phi^2/2$. The forward source integrates
the Einstein and scalar equations. A comoving observer has a proper clock,
a calibrated scalar meter and three reflecting neighbours at known comoving
separation $d$, one on each principal axis. For a round trip centred on
clock reading $t$, emission and reception at $t-\delta_i,t+\delta_i$
satisfy $\int_{t-\delta_i}^{t+\delta_i}du/a_i(u)=2d$.
Thus $\delta_i/d=a_i(t)+O(d^2)$ on the smooth test histories. The
scalar meter and the three radar arms have separate records.

Centred clock differences give $H_i=\dot a_i/a_i$, $\ddot a_i/a_i$,
$\dot\phi$ and $\ddot\phi$. The geometry reader uses
$G_{00}=\sum_{i<j}H_iH_j$ and
$G_{ii}=-(\ddot a_j/a_j+\ddot a_k/a_k+H_jH_k)$ in an orthonormal
frame with $\{i,j,k\}=\{1,2,3\}$. At one appointment on one launch,
the scalar equation gives $m^2$. The potential convention $V(0)=0$
fixes its additive constant. The axial null readings
$G_{00}+G_{ii}=\kappa\dot\phi^2$ give the common coupling, and
$G_{00}-\kappa(\dot\phi^2+m^2\phi^2)/2$ gives the vacuum term.
At the finest arm spacing and differencing interval, both $1/80$ in
their respective source units, the measured values are $0.69994$,
$1.60092$ and $0.11985$, against input values $0.7$, $1.6$ and $0.12$.
Using those coefficients at six held appointments on two fresh launches
gives maximum absolute Einstein-component and scalar-equation residuals
of $2.79\times10^{-4}$ and $5.12\times10^{-5}$ in source units.
Evolving from the first measured state of each launch predicts the
later arm lengths and scalar values within $4.42\times10^{-5}$.
Removing the matter term from geometry and adjusting the third initial
expansion rate to the vacuum constraint leaves an Einstein residual of
$0.5884$ under the frozen calibration. The computation and integer records
are in \path{code/matched_einstein_limit/observer_field.py} and its
companion receipt. The clock, scalar calibration, action and homogeneous
geometry are supplied. The experiment tests recovery of their coupled
equations on this family. Off-diagonal stress and spatial gradients
require a larger family of measurements.

\paragraph{Matter records on the free repair screen.}
Twelve directional modular readings on each group of one, four or sixteen
cells are obtained from the same smooth one-load preparation and a
supplied common angular frame. At sixteen cells per group the
least-squares quadratic residual is $14.2\%$ of the directional RMS
variation, down from $93.7\%$ for individual cells. A fit using nine
directions misses the other three by $75.2\%$ of the directional
variation on the sixteen-cell groups. At two further sizes with 64 and
256 cells per group, the same forecast gives relative errors of $42.5\%$
and $73.3\%$, although its absolute RMS error falls at each size. The
relative errors exclude monotonic improvement for this flight schedule.
The regional readings constrain
a proposed angular stress instrument. A curvature measurement on the
same material state is needed to evaluate the null balance.

Two co-located material clocks provide a further control. Their
calibrated gaps agree in the absence of an incoming load. After a load
crosses one cell boundary, the hand with a resident metering load lags
the empty hand by about $2.8\times10^{-4}$ radians on each of the
20-, 80- and 320-cell screens. The native Hamiltonian isolates the lag
to double occupation of the meter contact. A common lapse cannot
account for the difference between the two clocks. The integer readings
and detector controls are in the experiment packages
\path{interval_echo_macro_balance} and \path{interval_echo_metric_response}
under \path{model/experiments}.

A freely moving excess also couples to a stationary written clock on
the fully open 20-cell screen. The two preparations of the clock have
blank angular frequencies 32 and 33 in exchange units. If both ideal
hands read one elapsed time \(\tau\), their flip probabilities \(p_e,p_o\)
obey \(T_{33}(1-2p_e)=T_{32}(1-2p_o)=\cos(1056\tau)\), where \(T_n\)
is the Chebyshev polynomial. At a matched exposure with the excess
launched one link away, the two estimates lie in \([0.9508,1]\) and
\([-1,-0.9670]\), with simultaneous coverage of at least 99\% across
the clock readings. Allowing one distribution of elapsed times per
appointment accommodates both marginal readings at all 2,123
appointments, including the controls. The source detector distinguishes
these accounts further. At another one-link exposure it registered
1,681 arrivals for the even clock and 1,280 for the odd clock in
1,048,576 starts each. The exact conditional test of equal arrival
probabilities gives \(p<3.71\times10^{-10}\) after adjustment across
all appointments. Thus a clock model based on unresolved elapsed times
would also have to allow the instrument to affect the source. These
data identify a reciprocal material coupling. Measuring a
gravitational component requires an independently calibrated geometry.

A coherent clock preparation tests that random-time explanation
directly. Echo a locally ordered pair of loads $(2,0)$ or $(0,2)$ on
the filled reference material. Both clock components lie in one
degenerate blank-energy subspace. Evolution for any elapsed time acts
there by a common phase, so even a source-dependent probability
distribution over elapsed times assigns zero probability to a
reversal. A second local echo followed by two load reads measures
the order. On the all-open 20-cell federation an excess released one
link away yields reversals in both orientations at all five held waits
in an independent high-count simulation. At exposure four the counts
are 666 and 642 per $2^{30}$ respective launches. The blank, parked
and reversal-disabled controls give zero. Simultaneous exact binomial
intervals separate the one-link results from the blank at every held
wait. The original $2^{20}$-trial one-link cohorts missed the effect.
A model of this clock must include an effect of the material on its
internal state. Isolating an additional geometric delay requires a
separate propagation and ranging measurement.

\subsection{When repair selects a stationary generalized entropy}

A finite repair can force stationarity once the number of boundary states
is tied to independently measurable area. In a controlled construction
there are three centred geodesic balls of common proper volume $4\pi/3$.
Their ultrastatic spatial curvatures are chosen to give boundaries of
three, four and five binary tiles, each of area $\pi$. Independent
interior and surface counts check volume and area. A coarse record
contains the tile number $n$ and one material bit $b$. Its boundary
has $2^n$ possible words. Symmetric repair attempts flip bits or change
the sector by exposing a quiet tile or removing a quiet tile. The finite
transition matrix is connected, symmetric and has a holding probability,
so every launch approaches the same uniform microscopic equilibrium.
Its coarse probabilities are $p^*_{n,b}=2^n/112$. In this model
$S_{\rm bulk}$ is the Shannon entropy of the coarse record. For every
coarse distribution,
\[
 S_{\rm bulk}(p)+\frac{\log 2}{\pi}\sum_{n,b}p_{n,b} A_n
 =\log 112-D(p\Vert p^*).
\]
The first variation therefore vanishes on the entire five-dimensional
space of fixed-volume probability transfers at $p^*$. After 512 repair
attempts the largest measured first variation was $0.0072$ nats per unit
transfer or less. Halving acceptance of a tile exposure preserved the
same regions and shared registers and raised this residual to $1.39$
nats. The tile capacity per proper area, the three geometries and the
one rest frame belong to the construction. Its stationary coarse entropy
follows from repair under those inputs. No Einstein action enters the
calculation (\path{code/matched_einstein_limit/finite_entropy_region}).

The separately recorded surface counts and word counts also permit a
test with one curved sector withheld. At common proper volume $4\pi/3$,
the three measured surface areas are $9.42465$, $12.56648$ and
$15.70791$. The word detector observed all 8, 16 and 32 allowed words
in the respective sectors. Calibrating $\eta=\Delta\log\Omega/\Delta A$
on the first two gives $0.2206193$ nats per unit area. For the transfer
from the second to the third sector, the measured slope is $0.2206472$.
The prediction error $\Delta\log\Omega-\eta\,\Delta A$ is
$+0.0000875$ nats, with simultaneous $99\%$ interval
$[-0.000793,0.000967]$ accounting for the volume stops and surface
counts. This comparison recovers the assigned tile-area relation using
independent instruments on the same balls. The measured counts and
interval calculation are in the
\href{https://github.com/FloatingPragma/observer-patch-holography/blob/main/code/matched_einstein_limit/finite_entropy_region/held_calibration.json}{held-sector calibration}.

On the registered material, three overlapping repair records give a
related equilibrium with fixed owner probabilities. Independent private
coins maximize the joint entropy on all four allowed variations. Changing
the private correlations preserves the two owner tables and the
instantaneous cut, yet the entropy slope per triple is $-\log 5$ at
joint error probability $1/8$. This slope adds across 93 independent
triples in a connected 279-cell region. In freely evolving one-load
experiments, two orthogonal, equal-energy cell modes have zero amplitude
at every gateway. Mixing them with probability $p$ on $M$ equiprobable
