descendant cells gives regional entropy $\log M+h(p)$, with $h$ the
binary entropy. Every passive exterior history that leaves the vacuum
and free contacts intact is independent of $p$. The entropy slope at
$p=3/4$ is $-\log 3$ for every such volume and area reader. At $p=1/2$
it vanishes, and the free generator preserves every mixture. These are
tests of which equilibrium and which observable the repair selects.

A separate regional geometry test preserves a tetrahedral volume
inferred from clocked first returns. On the same preparation a
free-pulse ruler gives a fractional volume derivative
$2.98\times10^{-7}$ per unit mixture. The respective withheld distances
miss by $51.2\%$ and $66.9\%$. These are differences in finite outcome
probabilities. They exclude identifying that pair of rulers with one
fixed proper volume. The native repair, orbital and ranging records
are in \path{model/experiments/interval_echo_einstein_station}. A physical
area per accessible boundary degree of freedom on shrinking geodesic
balls of this source would connect the entropy and geometry tests.

\paragraph{When a boundary capacity implies stationarity.}
\label{par:capacity-stationarity-test}
A lossless encoding into a boundary of dimension $d$ gives the bound
$S\leq\log d$. At fixed $d=2$, a diagonal bit with probabilities
$(p,1-p)$ obeys the bound throughout its state space and has entropy
derivative $-\log 3$ at $p=3/4$. The finite condition that converts
a count into a first-variation law concerns the occupied reference
and the variation of its accessible multiplicities.

For example, let a finite regional word $i$ have $d_i>0$ exterior
completions. Uniform equilibrium on the compatible full words gives
$p_i^*=d_i/Z$, $Z=\sum_i d_i$. For any regional distribution $p$,
\[
 H(p)+\sum_i p_i\log d_i=\log Z-D(p\Vert p^*).
\]
The derivative of the right side vanishes at $p^*$ on every normalized
two-sided variation. If $A_i,V_i$ are independently calibrated geometric
readings on these words, then on fixed-mean-volume transfers
$\delta p$ the generalized-entropy derivative is
$\sum_i\delta p_i(\eta A_i-\log d_i)$. It vanishes on the whole
fixed-volume tangent space exactly when $\log d_i-\eta A_i=c+\lambda V_i$.
This follows by taking the annihilator of the normalization and volume
constraints. Further linear constraints add their rows to this span.
More quantitatively, if the measured reference is a faithful $p$,
the largest residual over $\|\delta p\|_1\leq1$ at fixed volume obeys
\[
 e_{\rm stat}\ \leq\
 \inf_{c,\lambda}\max_i|\log d_i-\eta A_i-c-\lambda V_i|
 +\max_i|\log(p_i/p_i^*)|.
\]
Indeed, subtract the two constraint rows from the residual and apply
the dual norm bound. Uniform control of these two errors by $o(\ell^4)$
suffices for the stationarity term of the matched-regional theorem on
this word algebra. For a nonlinear reconstruction use its area and volume
gradients at the reference. The native monitored word equilibrium realizes the first
identity with counted exterior completions. A common positive conversion
to proper area and the physical choice of this equilibrium are independent
identifications.

Two material rulers give a direct test on a different native preparation.
On a four-station tetrahedron each ruler assigns six ranges, a volume
and a surface area. The ordered three-by-three collar word table admits
four independent probability transfers preserving both one-word laws.
At the faithful correlated reference there is a unit Frobenius transfer
in the common kernel of both volume gradients and the first-return area
gradient with regional word-entropy slope $+0.1769704$ nats.
Choosing the free-pulse area instead gives $+0.1463704$ nats.
The transfers calibrated on the twenty-cell board keep the corresponding
three log-derivatives below $1.8\times10^{-13}$ in magnitude on the
80- and 320-cell boards. At the uniform table the word-entropy gradient
vanishes exactly. There, unit fixed-volume transfers change the respective
log areas by $1.46\times10^{-7}$ and $6.05\times10^{-7}$ on the twenty-cell
board. These population derivatives test both reconstructed tetrahedral
volumes and the indicated source-word entropy. Their geometric domain is
set by the held range checks. The full conditional word laws and independent
Cayley--Menger check are in the
\href{https://github.com/FloatingPragma/observer-patch-holography/tree/main/model/experiments/interval_echo_einstein_station/regional_geometry}{regional geometry experiment}.

The sign test can hold both operational volumes fixed on a continuous
family. The source has four independent fixed-marginal probability
transfers. Solving two independent volume equations leaves two free
directions. The volume Jacobian has a nonzero two-column minor by
rational elimination on the deposited clock moments. The implicit
function theorem supplies a local surface of exactly matched volumes.
For one transfer the source charge rises by $0.161753$ nats per unit,
and both areas increase, with slopes $7.25151\times10^{-8}$ and
$2.82552\times10^{-7}$ in the respective squared clock units. For any
differentiable area $F(A_1,A_2)$ with both partial derivatives
nonnegative, $dF\ge0$ there. Thus a positive multiple of this area
cannot cancel the measured charge. A second transfer increases the
first area and decreases the second. Positive changes of ruler units
cannot reconcile those signs.

Rational bounds on the differentiated face areas certify the signs
for the recorded population moments. Both signs persist on the
finite preparations at transfer parameters $\pm1/32$ and on the two
larger boards. Separate word counts give $0.16064\pm0.00328$ nats
per unit (one counting standard error) on the first transfer.
At $2^{60}$ simulated releases per edge and arm the two direct
clock histograms give standard errors $0.0472$ and $0.0000780$
on the area differences, far above the finite signals
$4.53\times10^{-9}$ and $1.77\times10^{-8}$. The area signs follow
from the population evolution. This result tests the two operational
tetrahedra and the selected word charge.
Transferring a local experiment to larger hosts keeps the station addresses
fixed. It supplies no estimate of the error divided by the fourth power
of a shrinking physical radius.

\paragraph{A fixed-volume test across observers.}
Consider five four-dimensional constant-curvature sectors, with
$K=-1,-1/2,0,1/2,1$. At each radius $\ell=2^{-j}$, $j\geq2$, assign
the volume of a flat ball of radius $\ell$ to all five sectors. In the
reference frame let $A_i$ be the corresponding surface area of sector
$i$. Give each sector the same number of surface registers. Each
register has $B=\ell^{-6}$ ordinary symbols, and each complete tile of
area $1/B$ admits one additional symbol. There are
$n_i=\lfloor B A_i\rfloor$ complete tiles. Refreshing the permitted
symbols and proposing a uniform sector change accepts a contraction
only when all lost tiles carry ordinary symbols. Its probability is
$(1+1/B)^{-\max(n_i-n_j,0)}$ for a proposal from $i$ to $j$. This
positive coarse kernel satisfies detailed balance with
$p_{i,b}\propto(1+1/B)^{n_i}$, where the refreshed material bit $b$ is
fair. Thus the observed bulk entropy gradient on a transfer from $j$
to $i$ is $-(n_i-n_j)\log(1+1/B)$. With area coefficient one in the
chosen units, the difference of two rounding errors costs at most one
tile. Integration of $1/(1+t)$ then gives
\[
 \left|\delta S_{\rm bulk}+\delta A\right|
 \leq \log(1+1/B)+\frac{|\delta A|}{2B}=O(\ell^6).
\]
Every spacelike rest slice through the centre, formed by the exponential
map of a timelike observer's orthogonal plane, has the same area at
fixed volume in a maximally symmetric spacetime. This proves the
$o(\ell^4)$ stationarity bound on this family for a common sector
ensemble. Across ten timelike observers, two launches and nine independent
probability transfers, the largest measured $|\delta S_{\rm bulk}+\delta
A|/\ell^4$ was $0.400$, $0.162$, $0.053$ and $0.044$ at radii $1/4$
through $1/32$. At the finest radius the area term alone reached $2.515$.
Clearing obstructing symbols on contraction gave a residual of $2.529$.
The records use independent terminal bulk counts, an interior stopping
population, a held interior count and a surface count.

The distinction between frame symmetry and a directional matter response
is measurable. Take the product of flat $(t,z)$ spacetime with a curved
two-plane of sectional curvature $K$. Keep the reference-frame sector
ensemble common to all observers. Expansion of the area element on the
observer's rest slice, followed by restoration of the flat proper volume,
gives the area contrast against the flat sector as
$-(4\pi/15)K(1+u_x^2+u_y^2)\ell^4+O(\ell^6)$. The common scalar bulk
reading lacks the transverse factor. For $K=1$ and $u=(3,2,2,0)$ the
limiting normalized residual is $-32\pi/15$. At $\ell=1/32$ the
largest measured magnitude for this observer was $6.750$, while the
rest observer and both longitudinal boosts gave magnitudes below $0.043$.
These two geometries and their surface alphabets are specified comparison
sources. Their difference isolates which observer dependence a bulk
readout needs on an anisotropic geometry.

\paragraph{One equilibrium on overlapping regions and tilted proper balls.}
In the partition-repair candidate, two cells sharing a face store one
of the five endpoint pairs $(0,0),(1,1),(1,2),(2,2),(2,1)$. An unbiased
lazy walk around this cycle selects the uniform pair law. A region sees
both letters on an internal face and one on a cut. A visible nonzero
letter has two possible partners, while zero has one. Thus each open
cut shutter contributes $-\log 2$ to the regional modular energy.
The independently updated cell volume bit has odds $1:2$ for occupancy.
The product equilibrium gives $dS+(\log 2)dA=(\log 2)dV$ on \emph{every}
overlapping selection of cells for every normalized perturbation,
including interfacial correlations. Here $A$ counts open shutters.
In a separate flat cubical assembly, rods measure exposed faces and
the bulk reader counts fair bits on strictly internal faces. The
incidence identity $6n=2m+b$ for $n$ cells, $m$ internal and $b$
exposed faces gives $\eta=(\log2)/2$ in unit rods. Independent simulated
terminal counts on an ordinary excitation give $[0.34006,0.35310]$
nats per square unit. With this gain, the entropy-plus-area response
to a held correlation in an overlapping six-cell sheet lies in
$[-0.01319,0.01327]$ nats per unit perturbation (joint $99\%$ coverage).

A curved comparison uses two ultrastatic metrics that realize the
same fifteen six-station distances. Their moving geodesic balls have
different areas at equal proper volume. At each of three scales
the ten observers through the same event read one common population
of persistent worldline records. Opposite axial observers intersect
the same material lines. Place one pair of lines across each of the
21 distinct boundaries, with every other pair wholly inside or outside
that boundary. A fair, observable tag $g$ selects the metric. On cut
$e$ keep its total number of allowed pairs $N_e$ constant across tags
and give each visible endpoint letter exactly
$d_{eg}=\lfloor M\exp(\eta A_{eg})\rfloor$ partners, where $A_{eg}$
is the independently integrated proper area, $M=\ell^{-5}$ and
$\eta=1$ in these units. The marginal has $N_e/d_{eg}$ equally
probable letters. Whole pairs give tag-independent entropies. Thus
$\delta S=-\sum_g\delta p_g\log d_{eg}$ and, for every normalized
tangent of the common record law at equilibrium,
\[
 |\delta S+\eta\,\delta A|\le 2\ell^5\|\delta p_{\rm global}\|_1 .
\]
At $\ell=1/4096$ the transfer to the positive metric in the
$xz$ frame gives $\delta A/\ell^4=-20.10712$ and
$(\delta S+\eta\delta A)/\ell^4=0.0000475$ by quadrature.
Exchanging the two partner counts gives $-40.21428$ for the latter.
The bound uses the ideal areas. A finite change of the fair tag
probability to $5/8$ costs $0.03158$ nats of relative entropy even
with the ideal capacity law. The calculation supplies the metrics,
the tag switch and the capacity per area on these particular balls.
Recovering them from native motion, measuring the action stress
independently and controlling finite changes away from equilibrium
are separate requirements for Einstein dynamics.\footnote{\href{https://github.com/FloatingPragma/observer-patch-holography/tree/main/model/candidate_laws/interval_echo_partition_repair}{Regional repair, independent cubical measurements and moving-ball integrals.}}

\paragraph{Three matched readings in a homogeneous string material.}
A second realization listens to two clock bits after their publication by
native Interval Echo observers. On the 33 enrolled addresses the ordinary
and held sampling laws have the same single rates, $11/33$ and $18/33$.
Their joint rates are zero and $8/33$. Copying a published symbol through
a normalized channel and summing over its extra outputs preserves the
conditional native state on every original transcript. Induction
therefore preserves every adaptive native experiment, including its
conditional reference states, provided the added material sends no
signal back. Timing matters: on the original 80-cell survey, recording
whether the moving charge reached port 1 halfway through its flight
changed its later arrival probability by $[0.01217,0.01324]$ at joint
$99\%$ coverage, even after discarding the path mark. Exterior screens
on centered geodesics can also leave two different interior proper-area
responses indistinguishable. An interior cap of half-angle $30^\circ$
with absolute area error below $0.339r^4$ distinguishes the particular
pair of smooth metrics tested.\footnote{\href{https://github.com/FloatingPragma/observer-patch-holography/tree/main/code/matched_einstein_limit/source_implication/full_geometry/station_balls/timelike_areas/exterior_screens}{Native path-marker counts and interior cap comparison.}}

In the added material the two published moments set homogeneous
proper sheet densities $\rho_o,\rho_c$; write $\rho=\rho_o+\rho_c$.
Each straight sheet carries unit energy and longitudinal tension per
proper length. Varying its area action with the transverse material
labels fixed gives $T_{ab}=\mathrm{diag}(\rho,0,0,-\rho)$ in a comoving
orthonormal frame. Each population is conserved separately. In the
product of flat $(t,z)$ spacetime with a transverse surface, the rod and
bearing rule gives rotation $2\pi\rho$ per unit enclosed transverse area.
It fixes the surface curvature $K=2\pi\rho$. Consequently
$G_{ab}=\mathrm{diag}(K,0,0,-K)$, with ordinary calibration $\eta=1$
and flat empty reference. The result holds for the finite densities in
this family.

Ten timelike observers through the same event match the proper volume
$4\pi\ell^3/3$. With $C_\ell=4\pi\ell^4/15$, the Jacobi fields give
$\Delta A=-C_\ell G_{uu}+O(\ell^6)$. The separate bulk reader acts on
one population of memory pairs shared by the overlapping balls. Seven
distinct spatial cuts per scale suffice for the ten frames. Placing
two endpoints on opposite sides of each cut with a fixed radial
margin $1/160$ keeps the layout independent of the selected source
and makes the construction compatible with fourfold refinement. A cut
pair in the reference gives one with probability $3/8$. Its calibrated
gap is $\log(5/3)/(2\pi)$. Weighted work $T_{uu}W$ rotates the pair,
where $W=\int_B(r^2-s^2)/(2r)\,dV=C_\ell+O(\ell^6)$ uses proper
radial distance $s$ and the measured stopping radius $r$. The binary
entropy then gives $\Delta S=2\pi C_\ell T_{uu}+O(\ell^6)$. Fully
included pairs undergo unitary rotations and preserve entropy; including
their modular responses gives regional relative entropy $O(\ell^8)$.
The maximum held $|\Delta S+\Delta A|/\ell^4$ over ten frames is
$0.04679,0.002674,0.0001662$ at $\ell=1/32,1/128,1/512$.
Halving only the joint defect or omitting only its memory pulse gives
$5.742$ or $11.485$ at the finest radius. These are calculated
populations. The result supplies all three leading coefficients on this
added string material. Its proper rods, tension and work-to-memory
coupling are ingredients of the comparison law. Identifying such an
action from native matter, and deriving a galactic response, require
independent observations.\footnote{\href{https://github.com/FloatingPragma/observer-patch-holography/tree/main/model/candidate_laws/interval_echo_partition_repair/published_strings}{Published clock archive, string action, ball survey, regional memories and changed-law controls.}}

Finite rods at $\ell=1/512$ reconstruct the held source's ten independent
Einstein tensor components within $6.0921\times10^{-5}$ of the sheet
prediction in maximum norm. At $\ell=1/32$ in the $xy$ frame,
three independent simulated endpoint banks give a held-minus-ordinary
entropy change of $[8.9676,12.4516]\times10^{-6}$ nats (joint $99\%$).
This interval contains the ordinary-gain area prediction
$11.0024\times10^{-6}$ and excludes the half-strength value
$5.5042\times10^{-6}$.

Across the three radii, 105 simulated banks record binary endpoint outcomes
on seven distinct cuts of the sheet material. For the held source at
$\ell=1/512$, the entropy reader reconstructs $T_{00}=0.57575633$ against
the action prediction $19/33$. Increasing the coincidence stock at fixed
ordinary mass gives $0.24242070$ against $8/33$. The simultaneous 99\%
sampling radius per tensor component is $0.00024308$ at that scale.
The binomial simulations use $2.53\times10^{15}$ copies per bank and
$16{,}777{,}216$ coherent exposures per copy on the finest board.
Finite-radius bias is bounded separately. Along the specified continuation
the number of trials at each radius is finite, and summable failure budgets
bound the additional entropy error by $o(\ell^4)$ with joint coverage at
least 99\%.
\footnote{\href{https://github.com/FloatingPragma/observer-patch-holography/tree/main/model/candidate_laws/interval_echo_partition_repair/published_strings/counted_tower}{Binary counts, confidence bounds for all radii, and ten-component decoding.}}

An unmodified 99-state Interval Echo seam implements the memory rotation
itself. For either adjacent-load alphabet, $(0,1)$ or $(1,2)$, an exchange
of duration $2\theta$ preserves the pair spectrum $(5,1,1,1)/8$ and its
mean native energy $51/8$. A single endpoint gives
$p(\theta)=1/4+\tfrac12\sin^2\theta$ and $p(\pi/6)=3/8$.
Thus an independent dwell change at fixed sheet stock produces
$dS/d\theta=(\sqrt3/4)\log(5/3)\simeq0.2212$ at that reference while
the sheet stress, native energy and proper area are constant. The
vacuum modular gap on the cut is $\log(5/3)$, whereas the sheet action
is independent of its two memory labels. The bulk--stress relation holds
along the specified changes of sheet density. Extending it to an
operator identity on all memory variations fails this test. Assigning
the dwell from proper weighted work is an additional physical
coupling.\footnote{\href{https://github.com/FloatingPragma/observer-patch-holography/blob/main/model/candidate_laws/interval_echo_partition_repair/published_strings/NATIVE_PAIR.md}{Native seam Hamiltonian, coherent measurements and energy distribution.}}

A reservoir realization selects the string material from arbitrary
incoming memories, even when they are entangled between balls. Encode
each four-word memory into three ticket bits. Exchange each bit once
with a fresh independent fair bit and record the displaced bit. At the
third exchange the incoming ticket at the material end is uniform
conditional on every possible displacement record. Five tickets write
the first word, and one writes each of the other three. Discard the
private ticket and apply the work pulse. On every branch the surveyed
material is exactly the product of the required rotated pair states.
All three finite residuals therefore retain their $O(\ell^6)$ bound
at the appointment for arbitrary initial memory states. This argument
uses the source's sheet action, rod transport and work transducer.
Entropy carried away by the preparation apparatus is outside the
surveyed material. A missed ticket address leaves two output states
at trace distance $3/4$ in the same three-tick control.
\footnote{\href{https://github.com/FloatingPragma/observer-patch-holography/tree/main/model/candidate_laws/interval_echo_partition_repair/published_strings/nested_stress/selection}{Local selection instrument, conditional records and error bounds.}}

An intervention after this appointment gives a quantitative boundary
to the conclusion. Moving probability between two opposite-load
eigenwords changes neither the total load distribution nor the seam
energy distribution, while its whole-pair entropy slope is $\log 5$.
Three concentric rest balls of radii $r,4r,16r$ containing that pair
read the same slope. Any stress variation confined inside the smallest
ball contributes $B_s=\pi(sE-I_2/s)$ at radius $s$ for two fixed
moments $E,I_2$. Hence $4B_r-17B_{4r}+4B_{16r}=0$. The entropy slopes
give $-9\log 5$ in this comparison. A compatible sequence of such
transfers with weights $r^4$ rules out a uniform $o(r^4)$ identification
on unrestricted preparations of this particular apparatus. A subsequent
complete reservoir sweep erases the transfer from its surveyed
memories.\footnote{\href{https://github.com/FloatingPragma/observer-patch-holography/tree/main/model/candidate_laws/interval_echo_partition_repair/published_strings/nested_stress}{Three-radius stress bound, finite seam measurements and preparation domain.}}

\paragraph{A falling sphere with two independently weighed stocks.}
The spherical mode of Interval Echo with published records assigns
ordinary rest mass $M_o$ to traveller frequency and collar rest mass
$M_c$ to the frequency of two posts occurring at the same service.
The ordinary survey reads $(M_o,M_c)=(1/3,0)$. Reweighting the
archived addresses changes their coincidence frequency at fixed
individual post frequencies and gives $(1/3,8/33)$.
The law supplies pressureless material, a shell pull proportional to
enclosed mass per squared area radius, common clocks and light signals,
and a memory pulse driven by regional work. These additions act after
each native publication and preserve the probabilities of experiments
using the original records.

Label shells by initial area radius $q$ in units of the body's initial
radius. The enclosed mass is $M(q)=(M_o+M_c)\min(q^3,1)$.
For the incoming zero-energy motion, the freely falling clocks and
spherical areas obey
\[
 R\dot R^2=2GM(q),\qquad \ddot R=-GM(q)/R^2,\qquad
 ds^2=-dt^2+R_q^2\,dq^2+R^2d\Omega^2 .
\]
The last expression describes the recorded light speeds and clock rates.
Varying the worldline action of each conserved mass gives
$T^i_{ab}=\rho_i U_aU_b$ with $\rho_i=M_i'(q)/(4\pi R^2R_q)$,
where $i=o,c$. Differentiating the metric gives
$G_{tt}=\partial_q(R\dot R^2)/(R^2R_q)$. The radial pressure coefficient
is proportional to $2R\ddot R+\dot R^2$ and the angular coefficient to
its shell derivative. Both vanish by the shell equations. Hence
$G_{ab}=8\pi G(T^o_{ab}+T^c_{ab})$ on each smooth shell before focusing. The
small-ball survey stays inside the homogeneous core, away from the
density jump at its edge. In the exterior $M'=0$, and the same geometry
gives vacuum tidal effects.
This pressureless shell geometry is classical.\footnote{
\href{https://doi.org/10.1093/mnras/107.5-6.410}{H.~Bondi, Spherically symmetrical models in general relativity}.
\href{https://github.com/FloatingPragma/observer-patch-holography/blob/main/Lean/ObserverPatchHolography/EinsteinBranch/SphericalDust.lean}{Formal shell identities and finite-direction residual}.}

Ten observers at one regular interior event determine their velocities
from exchanged signals. Their clock and beacon records locate
endpoints of spacelike rods on the exponential rest slice. Each
observer stops at proper volume $4\pi\ell^3/3$. At a fixed rod radius
$r$, positive mass gives a volume deficit
$-4\pi G_{uu}r^5/45+O(r^7)$. At fixed volume the observer extends the
rod slightly and measures the resulting area change $a_\ell$.
Mass differences and circumferences on two separate material shells
give the rest density. Parallel transport of the observer along a
survey ray gives the local dust Lorentz factor $\gamma(s)=\gamma(0)/a(t(s))$,
where $a(t)=R(t,q)/q$ in the interior and $a(0)=1$. Thus the work
meter samples $\rho(0)\gamma(0)^2/a(t(s))^5$. The boundary memory's
entropy response to its weighted integral $W$ is
$s_\ell=2\pi W+O(W^2)$ on the prepared material. The regional entropy
includes the memory endpoints in the ball. The work reservoir and
survey readouts belong to the apparatus.

For the sum $T=T^o+T^c$ and $C_\ell=4\pi\ell^4/15$, the three
measurements on these same balls satisfy
\[
 s_\ell-2\pi C_\ell T_{uu}=O(\ell^6),\qquad
 a_\ell+C_\ell G_{uu}=O(\ell^6),\qquad
 s_\ell+\frac{a_\ell}{4G}=O(\ell^6).
\]
Antipodal rays remove the odd spatial terms. The clock bins, angular
and radial surveys, volume stop and independent shell weighing obey
the indicated absolute error bounds. Calibrating $G=1/4$ by a release
on the ordinary source incurs $O(\ell^2)$ error, hence $O(\ell^6)$ in
the entropy--area sum. Dividing by $C_\ell$ and inverting the measured
ten timelike directions gives $O(\ell^2)$ tensor error. The simulation
enrolls $\ell=1/512,1/2048,1/8192$. At the finest radius the rods give
$G_{00}=0.863636368$, compared with $19/22$. Separate simulated endpoint
banks at that radius, using the measured release gain $\widehat G_\ell$,
place the held $|s_\ell+a_\ell/(4\widehat G_\ell)|/\ell^4$ below
$0.00016$ with simultaneous 99\% coverage. Halving only the collar
pull or omitting only its pulse raises the corresponding lower bounds
above $1.37$ and $2.74$.\footnote{\href{https://github.com/FloatingPragma/observer-patch-holography/tree/main/model/candidate_laws/interval_echo_published_records/spherical_dust}{Clock and rod surveys, regional preparation, endpoint counts and proof of the three error bounds.}}

The three entropy and area estimates apply to the installed array at its
appointed event and prescribed radii. On that same array a comoving ball of
intermediate radius $3\ell$ cuts no endpoint pair for sufficiently small
enrolled $\ell$. Its entropy change is zero while its measured dust work
is positive. Balls centered away from the parked line can likewise miss
the memories. Moving the array to such a center prepares another
experiment. The shell equations establish the effective metric equation
throughout each regular shell independently of this local entropy survey.

Two stationary clocks at area radii $2$ and $4$ and probes released from
rest read the same exterior mass parameter $\mu=G(M_o+M_c)$.
Upon adding collar mass the received-to-emitted frequency ratio changes
from $0.978019294$ to $0.960442075$. Pulse spacing then gives
$\mu=0.14393939394$ and proper-clock free release gives $0.14393939392$.
A separate interior light beam measures its angular distance $D$ along
an affine clock $\lambda$ normalized by the emitter. Its shear vanishes
by spherical symmetry for the central radial beam. The screens measure
$-2D''/D$, while shell weighing and frequency measure $8\pi G T_{kk}$
for $k=dx/d\lambda$. At $\lambda=1/8$ the two held readings are
$1.247057$ and $1.246636$ with a deterministic discrepancy bound
below $0.00063$. At $\lambda_*=1/4$ the observed angular distance gives
$2(\lambda_*-D(\lambda_*))=0.00332121029$. The associated weighted
matter integral
$\int_0^{\lambda_*}(\lambda_*-\lambda)8\pi G T_{kk}D\,d\lambda$
agrees within $1.2\times10^{-9}$. Both changed collar rules violate the
finite beam bounds. The timelike proof uses the three regional estimates
above. The beam measures a further consequence on this material.
A galactic application requires an independently inferred distribution
of the coincidence stock.
\footnote{\href{https://github.com/FloatingPragma/observer-patch-holography/blob/main/model/candidate_laws/interval_echo_published_records/spherical_dust/EXTERIOR_READER.md}{Independent exterior clock and release calibrations}.
\href{https://github.com/FloatingPragma/observer-patch-holography/blob/main/model/candidate_laws/interval_echo_published_records/spherical_dust/null_beam.json}{Finite affine clocks, angular screens and weighted matter records}.}

\paragraph{One regional clock material throughout the falling body.}
Place pairs of comoving sign emitters throughout the core at separations
$d$. Each end emits a fair bit at its local clock appointment. The
blank has joint law $P_0(A,B)=(1+AB/2)/4$. Each pair has a small
exposure proportional to the independently weighed initial density
$\rho_0$ and its volume, separation and angular weights. Its
correlation responds to the conformal-time difference per separation
$z=(\eta_B-\eta_A)/d$. A shared table prepared in advance and two
local gates implement the factor $F(z)=(1-z^2)^{-1}$ on a bounded
speed range. This lookup is a constitutive material rule. The public
register retains each observed sign and fills a missing sign from the
same blank conditional on whichever sign was measured. Every blank
slot then has the same entropy across geometric masks. Only pairs
with both ends inside contribute to the entropy contrast. The
measurement is conditional on settings and records the provenance
of each slot.

At an interior event let $a$ be the local scale, $v$ the observer's
velocity and $\gamma=(1-|v|^2)^{-1/2}$. The comoving projection of
a small proper ball has first-order stretches $(1,1,\gamma)/a$. The
volume shared by two Euclidean balls of radius $\ell$, integrated
over their separation, is $\pi\ell^4$. In comoving direction $n$
the projection supplies $\gamma/[a^4\sqrt{1-(v\cdot n)^2}]$, and
the local clock gates supply $a$. The spherical mean of
$F(v\cdot n)/\sqrt{1-(v\cdot n)^2}$ equals $\gamma$. With exposure
coefficient $8\pi\rho_0/15$, the regional entropy thus gives
\[
 s_\ell=2\pi C_\ell\,\frac{\rho_0\gamma^2}{a^3}+O(\ell^5)
       =2\pi C_\ell T_{uu}+O(\ell^5).
\]
The finite construction enrolls permanent bands $(D/2,D]$ of
separation. Each finer cutoff adds shorter pairs and retains old
appointments and outcomes. Spatial and separation spacings of order
$D^2$, angular and lag resolutions of order $D$, and a smallest
separation of order $\ell^2$ give the uniform error on compact
interior windows and bounded boosts. Regional relative entropy is
$O(\ell^{12})$. The independently surveyed fixed-volume area has the
$O(\ell^6)$ error above. At the ordinary coupling,
$s_\ell+\eta_\ell a_\ell=O(\ell^5)$. This covers displaced centers
and velocities between the ten tensor probes. The entropy belongs to
one sample per end in the fixed public register. An instrument that
retains additional temporal histories has a different entropy.
On a displaced body-diagonal observer outside the ten-probe menu, a
finite sample of the pair addresses at $\ell=1/96$ and dust time $1/32$
gives $s_\ell/\ell^4=3.0514$, against $3.0453$ from weighed material
and $3.0465$ from the area. Freezing the pair clocks gives $1.8486$.
Separate finite counts test the lag dependence of the emitted signs.
\footnote{\href{https://github.com/FloatingPragma/observer-patch-holography/blob/main/model/candidate_laws/interval_echo_published_records/spherical_dust/clock_pairs/REFINEMENT.md}{Finite source, local error estimates and refinement proof}.
\href{https://github.com/FloatingPragma/observer-patch-holography/blob/main/model/candidate_laws/interval_echo_published_records/spherical_dust/clock_pairs/regions.json}{Held and altered regional measurements}.}

A complete census of the finite tickets provides an instance of the
typed record theorem on the blank, ordinary and held preparations.
The exterior pulse spacing distinguishes the stocks. Independent
joint sign counts and their Shannon entropies also distinguish them.
Consequently the finite inter-instrument maps agree on these three
completed records. Retaining every earlier appointment makes those
maps commute with projection. Independent random launches can
produce different counts at the same measured geometry, so these
deterministic maps apply to the completed census.\footnote{\href{https://github.com/FloatingPragma/observer-patch-holography/blob/main/Lean/ObserverPatchHolography/EinsteinBranch/ClockPairRecords.lean}{Formal census, refinement maps and random-sample control}.}

The pressureless-body theorem concerns the sources in its shell law.
The original binary posts have a single independent correlation at
fixed marginals. With the measured source-address reference
$(12,10,3,8)/33$, their modular change is $\log(5/16)$ times the change
in coincidence mass. The later 81-word detector resolves two
independent correlation directions at fixed marginals. Its calibrated
interaction has a nonzero two-by-two minor, whereas one product of
independently encoded local bit probabilities has vanishing minors
after subtracting individual-station terms. This identifies which
source information a transfer of the pressureless result must preserve.

\paragraph{Selection by independently measured matter energy.}
On a 246-station guided cycle of the active law, successive addressed
contacts cancel the diagonal contrast and realize the hopping action
with neighboring matrix element $-1/2$. A bond interferometer measures
the energies of its momentum orbitals. Rotate to those addresses, read
the occupancies and propose random pairs. Transfer a load only when
the two measured energy intervals certify released work. If $m$ of
123 loads are above the gap there are $m$ lower holes. The probability
of removing one misplaced load per comparison is exactly
$m^2/\binom{246}{2}$. Every half-filled input reaches the same filled
negative band almost surely. Starting with alternating sharp station
loads took 128,334 comparisons in one simulation. Independent entropy
and interference reads on this selected matter give both signs of local
energy. Over the 24-station apertures the largest relative discrepancy
for three held regional changes was $0.0192\%$. Exact equality of the
two finite operators fails on four stations.

The same source allows an independent propagation test. Prepare one
sender orbital full or empty by exchanging it with a spare rail and
read the receiver's occupation in both arms. For a common
number-conserving quadratic evolution $U$, the two initial covariances
differ by the sender projector. The receiver difference is exactly
$|U_{rs}|^2$ for any incoming background. One native mode circuit
implements the same propagation even when the reset changes the
filling parity. Across six separations and 81 signal ages, the
receiver differences on the cold source and two held excitations
agree with this prediction within $2\times10^{-15}$ in the
calculated probabilities. One held excitation changes the measured
energy of bond 51 by $2.65\times10^{-4}$. An interacting native
four-station segment gives a different observation. Adding a spectator
next to the sender changes the signed receiver difference at age
three from $+0.00319$ to $-0.98706$. This is a source-dependent
propagation signal. Proper length requires an independent clock and
material calibration.\footnote{\href{https://github.com/FloatingPragma/observer-patch-holography/tree/main/model/experiments/interval_echo_einstein_station/critical_exchange/signal_ruler}{Reset instrument, calibrated propagation, held excitation and interacting control.}}

Removing the guide tests the physical status of the selected state.
With all contacts open its native energy is at least 3900, while a
sharp preparation at identical load has energy 1008. An exterior
receiver's hit probability grows as $t^2/8+O(t^3)$ at release. Cold
selection by observers on the guide is thus a property of the
addressed matter action. The source furnishes a one-dimensional
ordering of intervals.\footnote{\href{https://github.com/FloatingPragma/observer-patch-holography/tree/main/model/experiments/interval_echo_einstein_station/critical_exchange}{Signed interval readings and release control};
\href{https://github.com/FloatingPragma/observer-patch-holography/tree/main/model/experiments/interval_echo_einstein_station/critical_exchange/selection}{work selection, terminal counts and the low-energy criterion}.}

A three-dimensional field comparison tests the corresponding matter
premise on moving balls. Take the negative band of a massless two-component
Weyl hopping action and use a common detector that mixes a vanishing
fraction of independent tracial modes into both preparations. In the
continuum the ball modular generator is $\pi\{w_R,H\}$ for
$w_R=(R^2-|x|^2)/(2R)$, with the velocity fixed by the action.
For smooth finite particle or hole packets on one physical preparation,
the restricted covariance difference has squared Hilbert--Schmidt norm
$O(R^6)$. A detector occupation floor of order the largest enrolled
radius bounds the relative entropy by $O(R^5)$. Spatial inversion
cancels the constant kernel term in the modular expectation. The
linear term gives the symmetric action stress and hence
$\Delta S=(8\pi^2/15)R^4 T_{uu}+o(R^4)$. A single sequence of finite
grids serves each finite menu of radii and moving frames. In a
12-by-12-by-12 simulation, the opposite moving observers measured
entropy changes $0.07426$ and $0.06712$, with independent weighted
energies $0.08079$ and $0.07231$. The smooth-field proof controls the
small-radius limit. Its detector algebra and field propagation are
specified in flat spacetime. A test of full gravity requires an
independent proper-area response on this same field source. The
parallel-sheet rods give zero Ricci contraction along both axial
null rays $k_\pm=(1,0,0,\pm1)$. On the Weyl source the average of the
two corresponding stress contractions is $\rho+T_{zz}>0$.
A cosmological constant drops out of both contractions. These records
exclude identifying the sheet rods with the responding geometry of
this field at any positive coupling.
\footnote{\href{https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.07888}{F.~La Piana and G.~Morsella, The fermionic massless modular Hamiltonian};
\href{https://github.com/FloatingPragma/observer-patch-holography/tree/main/code/matched_einstein_limit/source_implication/modular_orbit/weyl_balls}{finite covariance proof, order of limits and moving-observer records}.}

\paragraph{Radiation with a measured homogeneous scale.}
A separate construction couples a sharp Weyl radiation occupation to a
scale $b$ read by proper clocks and light. In conformal time $\eta$ its
geometric action is $-AV_c\int (db/d\eta)^2d\eta/2$, with comoving
volume $V_c$. This is the Einstein--Hilbert action restricted to
homogeneous, isotropic, spatially flat metrics, up to an endpoint term,
with $\kappa=6/A$. The lapse equation sets $(db/d\eta)^2=2\rho_c/A$
for excitation energy $\rho_c$ per comoving volume. Pulse spacing and
independent calorimetry calibrate the coupling on an eight-pair
radiation occupation and test it on a 56-pair occupation.
In each moving observer's actual exponential ball the fixed-volume area
obeys $\Delta A=-C_R G_{uu}+O(R^6)$. A growing family of smooth packet
detectors fits causally inside the ball even when its rim is retimed.
On each fixed finite menu of frames and comparable shrinking radii,
their independently measured field entropy obeys
$\Delta S_{\rm read}=2\pi C_R T_{uu}+o(R^4)$.
The eight-channel rest survey at $R=0.51$ gives $0.033353$ nats against
the leading $0.105859$. The coefficient follows from the analytic
refinement. Extending this result to a sharp ultraviolet boundary
requires control of the modes admitted by its moving rim.

The stress used here is the work above the comoving filled sea.
For an absolute covariantly renormalized Weyl stress the Euler trace
anomaly contributes even in this geometry. With proper-time expansion
rate $H$ one has $\dot H=-2H^2$ and Euler density
$E_4=24H^2(H^2+\dot H)=-24H^4$. The local anomaly cancels between
states on the same metric. Comparing the absolute stresses on this
expanding solution and a flat vacuum requires its vacuum contribution.
Both calibrated occupations are radiation. A realization in native
Interval Echo and a comparison with ordinary massive matter require
their own common source.\footnote{\href{https://github.com/FloatingPragma/observer-patch-holography/tree/main/code/matched_einstein_limit/source_implication/modular_orbit/weyl_balls/radiation}{Radiation action, packet entropy, proper-volume surveys and controls}.
\href{https://arxiv.org/abs/1610.02304}{F.~Bastianelli and R.~Martelli, On the trace anomaly of a Weyl fermion}.}

\paragraph{Vacancies and excesses on one native neutral preparation.}
Stir one vacancy and one excess among eight stock stations using
uniformly offered native exchanges. On the 56 ordered words the
transition contracts deviations from the uniform distribution by at
most $5/7$ in Euclidean norm. From any initial word 64 offers bring
the total variation error below $1.65\times10^{-9}$. A material owner
records whether the marked station has a nonunit load. This separates
two populations whose mean incident energies are $56.21429$ and
$9.71429$. After the stirring, all thirty cell contacts evolve freely
between the registered radar appointments. Charge conjugation maps
the complete vacancy return history into the excess history, including
timeouts, on each charge-blind stationary population.

The same owner's measured near-to-quiet mean return-time ratios are
$[1.00013,1.00445]$ toward one receiver and $[0.99288,0.99730]$
toward another, at simultaneous 99\% coverage. A single rescaling
of that clock cannot produce both signs. A charge-resolving material
read separates the two probes' mean return times by more than $0.10$.
These tests establish a common, material-sensitive radar record for two
probes of one native source. Turning those records into a calibrated
three-dimensional proper-ball survey also requires moving clocks,
an area measurement and the regional stress at the same event.
\footnote{\href{https://github.com/FloatingPragma/observer-patch-holography/tree/main/model/experiments/interval_echo_einstein_station/critical_exchange/neutral_cell}{Native selection, energy measurements and both probes' complete histories.}}

On the active four-station equilibrium a more selective stress meter
is necessary. A two-sided accessible preparation curve keeps the radar
volume and all three pairwise reduced states of the reversal flag and
two head coins exactly fixed. Its entropy slope is between $0.0060922$
and $0.0060931$ nats per Hilbert--Schmidt unit. Multiplying the recorded
reversal sign by the two native coin outcomes $Y\otimes Z/2$ gives a
slope between $1.1068$ and $1.1075$. In particular, no sum of pairwise
work terms can measure this first variation. Even allowing independent
axes and scalar gains in the two reversal sectors fails for a shared
bilinear coupling. A multistep interrogation can generate a genuinely
three-register response.\footnote{\href{https://github.com/FloatingPragma/observer-patch-holography/tree/main/code/matched_einstein_limit/source_implication/full_algebra/correlation_rank/shared_action}{Fixed-volume curve, native Pauli measurement, and all-volume-price test.}}

\paragraph{Time stability of a mobile-source reference.}
On a unit-filled native federation, a freely moving vacancy or excess
correlates two armed seam memories across a cell boundary. Independently
acquired sharp outcomes at exposure four define a faithful joint reference.
With the source coupled, its four frequencies depart from that
reference at all four neighboring exposures $4\pm1/64$ and $4\pm1/128$
on both boards and for both signs. The smallest simultaneous 99\%
lower bound on total variation was $0.0030558$. Symmetric differences
resolve changes in the joint entropy and both one-head entropies in
each arm. The instantaneous joint slopes from the native generator, in
nats per exposure for vacancy and excess respectively, are $-4.14007$
and $+4.29523$ on 20 cells and $+1.13942$ and $-1.09493$ on 80 cells.
Removing the defect without selecting an outcome restores uniform
occupation. The two-head generator on this sector is $16I$ and
preserves every generated reference. Time stability then follows
from the externally prepared uniform background. The continuing-contact
state fails this dynamical equilibrium check. The regional variational
premise additionally requires a physical area term and independently
fixed proper volume. The acquired counts and two step sizes are in
\path{model/experiments/interval_echo_einstein_station/mobile_collar_source/stationarity.json}.

\paragraph{Ordinary source calibration and a complete-probe test of geometry.}
An excess or a vacancy occupies a blank intercell dimer. An independently
prepared source on a second dimer has stationary exchange-even and
exchange-odd orbitals separated by one measured energy unit, with equal
port populations and zero mean current. Calibration on one 20-cell bridge
predicts all 13,992 held pointer frequencies on nine bridge placements
across 20, 80 and 320 cells, including opposite source and probe signs and
a quarter mixture of the source orbitals. The largest frequency error is
$0.000155$ against a simultaneous 99\% envelope of $0.000838$.
An independent dimer clock resolves all four waits to within $0.000095$
native exposure. Raising the same metered regional energy by changing
the reversal parity of the compensating and contact seams excludes the
ordinary calibration on 4,680 of 4,752 held frequencies. The preparations
can differ in stress components that the regional energy meter does not read.

For a probe prepared as $I/2$, every unitary on its complete two-port
space preserves every binary analyzer probability at $1/2$. Classical
mixtures of such unitaries have the same property. The pure ordinary
source instead induces a displacement of at least $0.42656$ on one
analyzer at every placement and both signs at exposure four, allowing for
simultaneous sampling errors. Sharp disconnecting coins and vanishing
contrast give independent controls. To test the same unfitted prediction
in free flight, an initial load forms two correlated heads in four
exposures and is then removed without selection. A fresh signed probe
starts uniformly over all 240 or 960 ports. All 144 near endpoint and
phase measurements resolve deviations from $1/N$ for a normalized
one-probe analyzer on $N$ ports. Independent copies of the same individual
head marginals resolve an additional joint correlation contribution in
115 of the 144 comparisons. Direct endpoint occupancies alone resolve
the uniform-launch effect in all 48 near settings and the correlation
contribution in 36. No distant setting resolves either effect,
and all 288 fully mixed head controls include $1/N$. On these complete
probe spaces a source-dependent classical unitary geometry alone cannot
account for the measured quantum contact. A shared geometric contribution
alongside this interaction requires separate proper-range and stress
readouts. The two frozen protocols and terminal frequencies are in
\path{model/experiments/interval_echo_einstein_station/shared_geometry_probe/}.

\paragraph{Exterior information and measured aperture on the registered material.}
In a region containing two mobile loads, interleaving the free evolution
with sharp occupation reads selects a uniform equilibrium on all allowed
pair words. The regions contain one to three cells on each of three
host meshes. A given interior word with $k$ loads on the exterior-facing
rails admits $d_k=\binom{m}{k}$ exterior words on $m$ such rails. At
equilibrium its probability is proportional to $d_k$. Consequently
$\delta H_{\rm int}+\log(2)\,\delta\langle\log_2 d_k\rangle=0$ on every
normalized interior transfer. An independent exterior observer measured
the conditional cut-word capacity using exterior records alone. A single
calibration gave $0.694068$ nats per cut bit. Across 54 held transfers
the largest residual was $0.02583$ nats. Nine of these tests fixed both
mean native preparation energy and mean occupied cut load. Interior
entropy changed by $0.409$ to $0.476$ nats per unit transfer and the
exterior measurement canceled it within $0.00373$ nats. Biasing the
monitored controller against increasing cut load left a residual as
large as $1.39151$ nats under the frozen calibration. Uniformity here is
selected by the specified sequence of evolution and sharp reads.

An independent aperture survey launches loads uniformly on the inside
or outside of a region and records crossings, including quiet outcomes.
Unitarity makes the two total crossing intensities equal. Their probability
ratio therefore measures the fraction of accessible launch addresses.
On successive refinements the blank ancestral patch had 12, 48 and 192
addresses, with short-exposure apertures 3, 6 and 12. The dimensionless
ratio of cubed aperture to squared launch volume halved at each step.
It is invariant under an isotropic conversion of three-dimensional area
and volume to physical units. On the occupied patch, a source variation
preserving both register marginals and all three finite apertures on the
calibrating board gave a bulk entropy slope of $+0.007654$ nats per
unit transfer. Fresh memory samples on the three hosts gave $+0.00752$,
$+0.00793$ and $+0.00741$, each with standard error $0.000275$.
The corresponding held aperture slopes had magnitude below
$9\times10^{-18}$ in the numerical outcome laws. These conditional
word entropies and reciprocal flight measurements give operationally
distinct tests of the proposed boundary and volume identifications.

Equal-size regions permit an independent shape test. A five-cell cycle
and an open five-cell path give 60 accessible launch addresses and
apertures of five and seven seams. Extending both by the same cell gives
72 addresses and six or eight seams. Native crossing probes resolve
these aperture differences on three meshes. Training an aperture
coefficient on the first shape pair misses by $0.079$ to $0.093$ nats
when both observers query the same release supported on their common
cells. At fixed shape, preserving mean native energy and mean cut
load leaves both the blank aperture and the resident-conditioned
short-exposure tag aperture fixed, while the interior entropy changes
by $0.394$ to $0.445$ nats per unit transfer. Across all 24 held
comparisons the separately measured exterior completion capacity
cancels the interior change within $0.00604$ nats under one calibration.
The shape choice is an apparatus setting. The monitored evolution
selects a word equilibrium within each setting.

Two gateway receivers also distinguish loss of information in the readout
from a small signal in samples. Take two stationary equal-energy interior
orbitals and vary the imbalance of their opposite circular combinations.
Separate interior tomography gave a finite-difference entropy slope of $-0.5541$ nats
per unit Bloch displacement. For each enrolled one-gateway launch the
complete reduced state of its adjacent cell was insensitive to this
displacement at both measured exposures, including a phased incident
probe. The numerical trace distances were below $5\times10^{-16}$ with
a configuration-cutoff allowance below $2.2\times10^{-13}$ per release.
A phased load launched across two gateways and a joint sharp-occupation
measurement on the two receiving cells distinguished the same preparations.
A fixed decoder on fresh simulated records measured probability differences
of $3.83$, $3.76$ and $3.88$ parts per million on the three host sizes,
each over 37 standard errors. An observer confined to either enrolled
single receiving cell lacked this joint record.

A related comparison uses written binary heads and a positive ring
Laplacian on twenty triangular faces. Apply opposite quarter-turns to
a head strictly inside a ten-face disk. The two phase preparations agree
in total mean energy and in every exterior reduced state at release.
After one unit of free evolution their two held mixtures were
distinguishable on the six perimeter heads for all twelve interior seam
choices. The total-variation distances were $0.00653$ to $0.07928$.
A sharp read of the source head with its outcome discarded erased the
contrast under identical subsequent evolution. Yet the best common
coefficient multiplying the perimeter word-entropy slope left $99.72\%$
of the norm of the interior von Neumann entropy response. This
comparison tests a different field preparation on the written-head
extension. Both the native two-gateway read and the written perimeter
demonstrate how interior phase enters an observer record. Neither
experiment supplies a calibrated regional area for that phase variation.

\subsection{Testing a geometric interpretation of boundary completion entropy}

Can a material ruler recover the entropy of the allowed exterior words
at fixed proper volume? For a finite comparison, take a region with
$V$ ports, $m$ boundary contacts and $N$ mobile double loads. Prepare
the identity density on the fixed-number binary sector and park the
exterior contacts during the free evolution. The identity is stationary
on this sector. An interior sharp word implies that $K$ loads occupy
boundary contacts. Its exterior completions are the $\binom mK$
distinct equiprobable cut words. The mean completion entropy and the
interior Shannon entropy are therefore
\[
 C_m=\mathbb E\log\binom mK,\qquad H_{\rm int}=\log\binom VN-C_m .
\]
An independent material instrument samples a regional address and
launches a distinguishable tag when that address is vacant. On a
contact to an empty exterior rail, the calibrated half-swap transmits
the tag with probability one half. Quiet launches are retained. Scaling
by the independently surveyed launch volume gives the mean available
aperture $A_{\rm tag}=m(1-f)$, where $f=N/V$.

For $h(f)=-f\log f-(1-f)\log(1-f)$ the finite entropy estimate is
\[
 0\le m h(f)-C_m\le \log(m+1)+(V-m)/(V-1).
\]
Indeed, for $x=k/m$ the probability of $k$ under the binomial law of
parameter $x$ lies between $1/(m+1)$ and one, since it is a mode of that
law. The remaining Jensen gap is $m$ times the mean binary relative
entropy of $K/m$ against $f$. Using $\log y\le y-1$ and the
hypergeometric variance gives the second term. Consequently two
equal-volume shapes whose cut sizes grow in a fixed ratio greater than
one have a capacity difference per additional available contact tending
to $h(f)/(1-f)$.

The nominated parent regions shrink in the spherical subdivision chart
while their microscopic cut sizes grow. At the finest of four such
settings, on a mesh of 327,680 cells, two independent equal-volume shape
pairs gave $1.374$ and $1.377$ nats per additional transmitting contact at
half filling, against the prediction $2\log 2=1.386$. At three-quarter
filling the corresponding slopes were $2.230$ and $2.223$. A fluctuation
of $K$ can also move entropy while leaving mean aperture fixed. Two
simultaneous tags on different boundary contacts measure a second
factorial moment of $m-K$. A signed transfer between five equally spaced
$K$ classes with weights $(1,-4,6,-4,1)/8$ fixes both complete tag
flags, including coincidences, and the mean native energy. Each flag
probability and the conditional mean energy are at most quadratic in
$K$. On a twenty-contact cut the independent interior entropy slope was
$0.007361\pm0.000403$ nats per unit transfer; the population value is
$0.007420$. These tests treat the transmitting aperture as a possible
geometric readout. Its relation to word entropy depends on the matter
preparation and on which contact correlations the instrument measures.

\paragraph{Which observations distinguish a change in completion entropy?}
A central observer clocks a binary relay through a monitored twelve-port
cell while the two residents are parked. A radius of two outward
appointments includes the centre, five other inner stations and five
rim stations. Both residents lie in this ball for 55 of the 66
equiprobable two-load words. Compare two preparations, each an equal
mixture of two admitted words. Every individually addressed vacancy
report has the same distribution in the two preparations. Both have
nine vacant slots in the ball and a mean of four on its rim. A
permutation taking one preparation to the other commutes with the
incoming Hamiltonian, so their entire energy distributions agree.

The preparations differ in the number $K$ of residents on the rim.
The first has $K=1$ with certainty; the second has $K=0$ or $2$ with
equal probability. The mean log number of rim completions of an
interior record changes by
$\tfrac12\log(2/5)=-0.458145$ nats. At the uniform monitored reference
the interior entropy has the opposite first variation. For two distinct
rim receivers chosen uniformly, the probability of coincident occupation
rises from zero to $1/20$. Independent single and paired reports gave
a completion change of $-0.45676$ nats in simulation. The pair reads the
correlation missed by the individual counters. The vacant slots and
their radial increment are finite shell markers. Identifying proper
area requires an independent geometric response on this same ball.
The \href{https://github.com/FloatingPragma/observer-patch-holography/blob/main/model/experiments/interval_echo_einstein_station/equilibrium_refinement/CLOCK_BALL.md}{timed shell experiment}
specifies the relays, energy symmetry and count comparison.

An independently prepared load-one tag can read this correlation through
its propagation while both load-two residents move. Launch the tag at the
twelfth station, outside the clock ball. A single inward transmission
probability, calibrated on moving one resident onto the rim, predicts
$-0.88993$ nats for the original held transfer. For a second measurement,
uniformly permute the inner addresses and the rim addresses separately on
each fresh copy before launch. This preserves the rim occupancy $K$ and
erases the placement within each class. At two shutter exposures,
$1/2$ and $1$ in generator units, measure the inward transmission for
each known class and then for each unknown preparation. A separate
material hand checks the shutter phases. The two measured rates and
normalization determine the three class probabilities. The ideal
response determinant at exposures $t,2t$ has leading term
$(42811/192)t^6$. Using independent simulated calibration and source
counts gives $-0.456786$ nats for the completion change, with
simultaneous $99\%$ interval $[-0.48912,-0.42445]$ containing
$\tfrac12\log(2/5)$. The recovered distributions predict outward
transmission at the withheld exposure $1/4$ on both preparations.
This active \href{https://github.com/FloatingPragma/observer-patch-holography/tree/main/model/experiments/interval_echo_einstein_station/equilibrium_refinement/tracer_flux}{timed-tag measurement}
recovers completion information in the randomized three-load experiment.
Its geometric interpretation requires an independent area measurement.

The five rim stations also compare their angular coverage. Their mutual
clock distances form a five-cycle. Interpolate its equally spaced
addresses on a circle and let a detector light when an occupied site
falls within its arc. Choose a common arc width and any nonnegative
angular detector weights. Calibrate the integrated lit area on a
single resident placed uniformly on the rim. Every two-resident
placement has the same completion score $\log 10$, but the overlap of
the two lit arcs depends on their separation. Counting the near and
far pairs inside each arc gives a sharp minimum worst-case error of
$\tfrac12\log 5-\tfrac23\log(5/2)=0.193858$ nats over all single
widths and weights. The
\href{https://github.com/FloatingPragma/observer-patch-holography/tree/main/model/experiments/interval_echo_einstein_station/equilibrium_refinement/rim_geometry}{rim aperture comparison}
also gives smooth auxiliary metrics that agree on the six marked
stations' distances and the ball's proper volume at the surveyed
radius, while assigning different areas there. Independent volumes of
neighboring proper balls can resolve that ambiguity. In a smooth
geodesic shell of measured proper thickness $\delta$, the volume
increment divided by $\delta$ gives its mean proper area; if
$|dA/dr|\le M$, the difference from the inner boundary area is at most
$M\delta/2$.

\paragraph{Local density and the area of a proper ball.}
Even exact counts of every angular sector at every radius can give the
same result on spaces of opposite curvature. In a space of constant
sectional curvature $K$ with circumference radius $S_K(r)$, a smooth
marker density $m_K(r)=(r/S_K(r))^2$ converts proper volume to
$r^2\,dr\,d\omega$. Its count at the center has the same calibration
for every $K$. Neighboring observers can measure the missing geometric
response if they share proper geodesic ranges and a common marker
population. Normalize their counts by exposures and flat reference
volumes. At the center let $C,H$ be the readings at radii $\ell,\ell/2$.
Let $B$ be the mean of six half-radius balls displaced by $\pm\ell$
along three orthonormal axes. In normal coordinates the normalized
count at radius $s$ is $m+s^2(\Delta m/10-m\,\mathrm{Scal}/30)+O(s^4)$.
Averaging the paired displacements gives
$B-H=\ell^2\Delta m/6+O(\ell^4)$. Hence
\[
 \widehat{\mathrm{Scal}}
 =\frac{18B+22H-40C}{[(4H-C)/3]\,\ell^2},\qquad
 A\big|_{V=4\pi\ell^3/3}
 =4\pi\ell^2-\frac{2\pi}{15}\,\mathrm{Scal}\,\ell^4+O(\ell^6).
\]
Per-reading errors $o(\ell^2)$ suffice for the reconstructed area to
have error $o(\ell^4)$ for bounded geometry and positive smooth density.
At $\ell=1/16$ on the supplied $K=-1,+1$ geometries, the
\href{https://github.com/FloatingPragma/observer-patch-holography/tree/main/code/matched_einstein_limit/fixed_volume/displaced_markers}{displaced-marker counts}
give scalar curvatures $-5.99771,+6.00579$. Independently counted
surfaces at matched volume give $-5.99813,+6.00230$. The native test
requires the displaced observers to realize the same proper-ball
construction on the source used for the entropy reading.

The one-cell, two-resident source supplies an angular component of this
construction. Launch a separate load-one tag from each possible port
and measure its short-time arrival at the other ports. The adjacent tag
exchange has Hamiltonian matrix element $-1/2$ for either a resident or
a vacancy. The first-order amplitude remainder is at most $441t^2/8$.
It separates the 30 edges from every other pair at $t=1/512,1/256$ for
every admitted resident state, including coherent preparations.
At $t=1/512$ on the uniform ball, the largest non-neighbor upper bound
was $1.06\times10^{-9}$ and the smallest neighbor lower bound was
$8.95\times10^{-7}$, with simultaneous $99\%$ coverage across the
survey. Both held correlation stocks gave the same graph. The
icosahedral graph recovered by this
\href{https://github.com/FloatingPragma/observer-patch-holography/tree/main/model/experiments/interval_echo_einstein_station/equilibrium_refinement/tracer_flux/bearings}{bearing measurement}
has a canonical unit-sphere representation. Pair opposite vertices
to obtain six unoriented unit axes and write $P_i=v_i v_i^\top$.
Their Gram matrix has entries $1$ on the diagonal and $1/5$ elsewhere,
so they span all six symmetric components and $\sum_i P_i/6=I/3$.
The six opposite pairs can therefore replace the orthogonal triad.

The 55 readable two-resident words give actual candidates for the
displaced stations. For the twelve directed angular vertices $u_i$,
word $(i,j)$ has load position $2(u_i+u_j)$. Five words share the origin, and two
shells have 25 words each. Ten antipodal pairs on the inner shell
admit positive weights whose directional second moment is $I/3$.
On the outer shell every paired axis has squared projection below
$1/3$ along the exterior bearing, excluding a positive isotropic
average of those pairs. On the inner shell the
uniform word counts in the full central, half-radius central and
displaced half-radius balls are respectively $30$, $5$ and $1$.
After normalization by aperture volume the estimate gives
$\ell^2\widehat{\mathrm{Scal}}=-264/65$ on this flat coordinate
realization. Thus the recorded positions implement a finite displaced
survey. Their atomic populations require independent calibration
before they can measure smooth proper volume.

\paragraph{Curvature from ranges between paired stations.}
On a smooth spatial slice, displace mirrors along each of two unit
axes $u,v$ to both signs of proper radii $r,s$ of order $h$. Write
$d_{\sigma\tau}$ for the four cross distances. The expansion of
squared geodesic distance in normal coordinates gives
\[
 \frac{3}{r^2s^2}\left(r^2+s^2-
 \frac14\sum_{\sigma,\tau=\pm1}d_{\sigma\tau}^2\right)
 =R(u,v,v,u)+O(h^2).
\]
The four signs cancel both the unknown tangent angle term and the
fifth-order term. Twice the sum over an orthonormal triad gives scalar
curvature. Range errors $o(h^3)$ suffice for $o(h^4)$ error in the
fixed-volume area. Smooth ultrastatic clock controls with unequal
radii recover $-1.99939$ and $+2.00062$ for spatial scalar curvature
$-2$ and $+2$. On the finite two-resident source, 990 reversible
material transfers give ten triads whose angular bearings are
orthogonal and whose opposite endpoints pass the radial midpoint
test at radii $(1,2,2)$ in exchange appointments. In each triad all
four held ranges between a radius-one and a radius-two axis equal
three. A minimizing segment arriving at the center would then have
to continue to two different endpoints at the same distance. If
these ratios persist on shrinking balls of bounded curvature, the
least possible maximum relative correction for these five stations
is $(3-\sqrt{5})/(3+\sqrt{5})\simeq14.59\%$ when the corrected
axes retain true geodesic antipodes. If all four endpoints can shift
freely and both diameters enter the fit, the sharp lower bound becomes
$(\sqrt{13}-3)/(\sqrt{13}+3)\simeq9.167\%$. A Euclidean placement
with the two midpoints on opposite sides of the center attains it.
The \href{https://github.com/FloatingPragma/observer-patch-holography/tree/main/code/matched_einstein_limit/fixed_volume/polarized_ranges}{paired-station experiment}
therefore tests the interpretation of an exchange appointment as
proper length independently of marker counts.

Twelve observers also time a freely moving tag on the same two-resident
source. The first detection at the remote observer starts the return
leg; the first subsequent detection at the sender ends it. All 132
ordered station pairs were sampled on three resident preparations at
each of two shutter times. A separate material-exchange clock gives
three limiting half-round-trip readings, $11,14,15$. The three leading
spectral directions inferred from these times agree with the
independent one-way angular frame to subspace-angle sine below $0.040$.
Fitting all pair times as Euclidean distances in three dimensions,
at any common speed, requires more than $2.19\%$ relative distortion
at simultaneous $99\%$ coverage. In a convex normal three-ball of
radius $R$ with $|\mathrm{sec}|R^2\le1/9$, comparison of geodesics
with their normal coordinates bounds this distortion by $1/52$.
The \href{https://github.com/FloatingPragma/observer-patch-holography/tree/main/model/experiments/interval_echo_einstein_station/equilibrium_refinement/tracer_flux/bearings/round_trip}{free-tag clock records}
therefore recover a common long-wavelength frame while excluding
these raw transit times as proper lengths on such a ball.

On the clock-defined one-cell ball, forward and reverse tag flights
supply an independent nested-volume experiment. Launch one or two
mobile markers among the two residents, retain every attempt, and
compare the final resident word with an independent draw from the
same preparation. Reciprocity recovers the mean number of allowed
marker placements, $\mathbb E\binom{v_r}{j}$ for $j$ markers and $v_r$
vacancies. At radius two all four preparations give nine single or
36 double placements. At radius one the held change preserves the
single-marker reading and raises double placements from $10$ to $10.5$.
Calibration on the ordinary one-resident transfer then predicts zero
or $+\log(5)/8$ nats respectively. Independent interior counts give the
held completion contrast in $[-0.45920,-0.45717]$ nats at simultaneous
$99\%$ coverage. The
\href{https://github.com/FloatingPragma/observer-patch-holography/tree/main/model/experiments/interval_echo_einstein_station/equilibrium_refinement/nested_volumes}{reciprocal marker counts}
distinguish available placements from the information stored in their
correlations. A second instrument inserts physical plugs in a random
rim order, reversing each trial. With $k$ occupied among $m$ remaining
sites, the next insertion succeeds with probability $(m-k)/m$.
Stopping at $m=k$ gives a total negative log success probability
$\log\binom{5}{k}$. Independent class and insertion counts give the
held change in $[-0.45846,-0.45785]$ nats. This is an operational
measurement of completion information. Converting its dimensionless
units to proper area requires a separate geometric measurement.

A second experiment lets the two residents move during the range
measurement. On the 80-cell host, four exterior-facing ports time
resident signals that reach a second port and return home. The six
half-round-trip ranges give a candidate tetrahedral volume and area.
On a locally fixed-volume transfer from zero to one occupied rim port,
calibrating $\delta H_{\rm int}+\eta\,\delta A=0$ gives
$\eta=0.00045116$ nats per squared clock unit. On a held transfer
from zero to two occupied rim ports, the measured entropy and area
both decrease. Any positive coefficient gives the same sign for the
two contributions. A separate two-sided change of the word
probabilities preserves mean energy and every launch and return
probability while changing the interior entropy at a rate of
$(4\log4-3\log6)/3=0.056633$ nats per unit increase in the probability
of double rim occupation. A fifth station's ranges also fail the
prediction from a flat three-dimensional tetrahedron. The
\href{https://github.com/FloatingPragma/observer-patch-holography/tree/main/model/experiments/interval_echo_einstein_station/equilibrium_refinement/area_survey}{resident signal experiment}
therefore tests stationarity for this specified range instrument.
Stationarity at fixed native proper volume remains a separate premise.

Two residents on the same monitored trajectory provide a further
geometric measurement on the 80-cell preparation. Start when one of the
four rim ports is occupied. At a randomly timed later inspection,
record which pair of the other three ports is occupied. From those
three pair frequencies infer the spread of the image across their
triangular face. A control repeats the construction with two independent
launches, choosing one resident from each. For the held preparation
that preserved the earlier clock records within the precision of its finite
preparation lottery, the changes of the two normalized image areas have
opposite signs. With the reference triangle areas held fixed, the
same-trajectory change lies in $[2.17,2.61]$ squared clock units and the
independent-launch change in $[-6.25,-5.66]$, in simultaneous $99\%$
intervals from simulated counts.

Using every start, including misses, gives a separate area reading.
For this reading with reference triangle areas, ordinary calibration on
the zero-to-one rim transfer gives $\eta=0.00071406$ nats per squared
clock unit. On the held transfer preserving mean native energy and
mean rim occupation, the residual $\delta H_{\rm int}+\eta\,\delta A$
lies in $[0.7469,0.7487]$ nats per unit transfer at simultaneous
$99\%$ coverage. A separate certified change of the source preserves
the original clocks, all twelve true pair-hit probabilities and mean
native energy exactly, while reducing interior entropy by more than
$0.00468$ nats. Thus paired propagation adds information about the
residents' correlations. The registered clocks and pair pixels leave
the proper-area premise open. The
\href{https://github.com/FloatingPragma/observer-patch-holography/tree/main/model/experiments/interval_echo_einstein_station/equilibrium_refinement/area_survey/joint_screen}{two-resident screen experiment}
gives the acquisition, held predictions and finite-source bound.

There is also a test along the source's own monitored evolution. Let
$P$ be its half-unit transition on the 276 sharp words. Prepare a
small two-sided perturbation of the uniform stock, release it, and
compare its records before and after one further interval. A rank-36
certificate applied to the clock and image responses of $P-I$ gives
a physically admissible direction in which mean native energy, all
twelve launch/return distributions and the twelve absolute pair
pixels agree across that step. In units of the change in double
rim occupancy, the first variation of the interior entropy difference
is $4\log(4)/3-\log(6)=0.056633\ldots$ nats. On one exact positive
preparation the step increases interior entropy by more than
$0.00084$ nats while those records agree. The conditional
stationarity test thus fails for every area formed solely from these
readings on this class of preparations. On a finite-ticket preparation
a second interval changes the first clock's launch probability by
$[9.4077,9.6742]\times10^{-7}$ at simultaneous $99\%$ coverage. The
\href{https://github.com/FloatingPragma/observer-patch-holography/tree/main/model/experiments/interval_echo_einstein_station/equilibrium_refinement/area_survey/joint_screen/relaxation}{timed comparison}
resolves the age of the prepared source. The uniform stock itself is
invariant under the monitored evolution.

\paragraph{A clocked range and a freely evolving reference.}
A material hand times a sharp tag routed by contact swaps. Balls defined
by this range can shrink relative to the measured host radius while
resolving more microscopic steps. On hosts of 80, 1,280, 20,480 and
327,680 cells the ratios of the chosen radius to the host range were
$3/32$, $6/128$, $12/512$ and $24/2048$. The dimension read from the
logarithm to base two of the count ratio at doubled radius was $1.565$,
$2.010$, $2.018$ and $1.999$. At the finest setting the count rose from
1,158 to 4,630. A homogeneous three-dimensional proper ball would have
a leading ratio of eight. This dimensional test applies to the routed
clock and its accessible launch measure. It does not assign a dimension
to every possible relational measurement in the material.

There is a separate exact constraint on autonomous selection. Let $D$
be the dimension of a full native two-double-load sector. Two orthogonal
rank-four product spaces of cell orbitals, with projectors $P_A,P_B$,
have the same energy and zero amplitude at every gateway. Place the
first load in a dark orbital of the same cell and the second in a dark
orbital of either of two further cells. Then
\[
 \rho_\alpha=\frac{I+\alpha(P_A-P_B)}{D},\qquad |\alpha|<1,
\]
commutes with the free generator with every host contact active. All
$\rho_\alpha$ have identical probabilities for every total-energy
outcome, and tracing out the interior gives the same exterior density.
At $\alpha=3/4$ the interior sharp-word entropy slope is
$-\log(13/11)=-0.167054$ nats per unit probability transferred between
the spaces. Inclusive local readings on the twelve enrolled regions
resolved this slope by at least $33.6$ standard errors. Replicating
orthogonal pairs inside a fixed ancestral region preserves a nonzero
coarse response. This establishes preparations with matching energy
statistics that free flight cannot bring to the monitored record
equilibrium. A preparation-independent attractor and a particular
physical vacuum have different hypotheses.

At half filling an independent single-cell check uses two conjugate
reflections $S,T$ of the same native generator $H$. A polynomial in
the shifted Hamiltonian multiplying $S-T$ gives a traceless conserved
operator with zero partial trace onto the three cut rails and zero
trace in every energy eigenspace. A positive density biased in this
operator, using a supplied spectral preparation, therefore changes
neither the entire cut state nor any energy-outcome probability. Its
interior sharp entropy slope was $-0.21540\pm0.00021$ nats per unit
transfer. Thus even at the filling where mean aperture predicts the
shape-transfer slope most closely, free stationarity permits inequivalent
interior responses.

An intra-region survey provides an additional joint test on the same
prepared states. From all routed station ranges form a three-dimensional
hull, recording its mean volume and boundary area as candidate measures.
Transfers among ten or fifteen equal-energy stationary orbital blocks
can preserve both measured means and the full exterior state. On twelve
five- and six-cell shapes the resulting interior von Neumann entropy
derivatives were $1.149$ to $1.460$ nats per unit probability
transferred. The sharp interior read alone changed by $0.085$ to
$0.115$ nats. These hulls are evaluated on parked-region preparations.
They test the proposed area and volume functionals directly, even
before asking whether the distances admit a proper three-dimensional
chart.

\paragraph{A field vacuum and its induced distances.}
On a single written cell the positive ring Laplacian has a unique
uniform zero-energy state in its 458-word connected sector. For each of
six compact or open port regions, fixing the vector of boundary fluxes
makes every allowed interior word compatible with every allowed exterior
word in that sector. The uniform pure state thus has one Schmidt pair
per flux label. Regional von Neumann entropy is exactly the Shannon
entropy of the measured flux labels, between $0.698$ and $2.024$ nats.
There is no additional conditional entanglement within a fixed label at
this reference.

One composite rotation of written heads across the cell gives a measured
flux-entropy slope of $+0.15953\pm0.00063$ nats per radian while keeping
all twelve aggregate signed crossing probabilities of the enrolled shape
fixed to first order. Retaining individual material launch and destination
addresses reveals the perturbation. With the charged launch fixed,
the two held distributions differ by $0.00460$ in total variation at
opposite angles of magnitude $1/64$. A fixed decoder resolved the
difference by 434 standard errors. A natural independent length candidate
is the diffusion distance between the resulting launch distributions.
Across three exposure times and three choices of charge mixture, a
three-dimensional chart fitted to eight stations missed held pair
distances on four other stations by $35.0\%$ to $96.3\%$. These distances
resolve variations in the quantum field. Their failure of the chart
test, together with the routed-volume scaling and the nonunique
stationary sector preparations, leaves the proper-area and vacuum
identifications of the Einstein branch undischarged on these sources.
The measurements and controls are archived with the
\path{interval_echo_einstein_station} experiments.

\paragraph{A propagating field predicts two material probes.}
Two opposite phase preparations on this written-field extension have
identical passive ring-energy and current histories. After half an
exposure, apply a second neutral field pulse on the link used by the
charged survey. A receiver sharing no vertex with that pulse records
a four-setting energy-click contrast of $0.003329$ after one further
exposure (simultaneous 99\% interval $[0.002491,0.004167]$).
The contrast equals the real overlap of the two propagated field packets
at that receiver. Both pulse signs give identical energy readings on vacuum.
