calibrated hands use unit, 32-fold and 33-fold phase frequencies.
For any common random elapsed time, the probability of a wrong Ramsey
outcome obeys $p_g\leq g^2p_1$ at matched analyzer phases. It follows
pointwise from $|\sin(gx)|\leq g|\sin x|$ and therefore survives
averaging. The measured Ramsey port probabilities violate the bound
while the source remains in flight. A local detector--matter
interaction accounts for the contrast in the joint generator. These
clocks directly test the possibility of a common time-only account.
A stress measurement and a proper optical ruler on that same moving
source are required for a gravitational curvature measurement.%
\footnote{\href{https://github.com/FloatingPragma/observer-patch-holography/tree/main/model/experiments/material_inertia/sourced_survey}{Observer exchange-energy capture};
\href{https://github.com/FloatingPragma/observer-patch-holography/tree/main/code/matched_einstein_limit/source_implication/full_geometry/station_balls/reciprocal_screen/range_contraction/formed_ranges}{clock ratios on freely formed correlations};
\href{https://github.com/FloatingPragma/observer-patch-holography/tree/main/model/candidate_laws/interval_echo/openwave/row_27_metric/formed_pair_clocks}{clock comparisons with a moving source}.}

A two-station test uses the load from a moving clock hand as the signal.
A successful local dimer transfer releases that same load into a ten-port
guide beside a stationary written source. At the other end, the arrival
modulation divided by the receiving clock frequency gives $32.01586$ in
one direction and $0.0312348$ in the reverse direction for unit loads.
Double loads in the same apparatus give $1.000007$ and $1.000003$.
A separate timing and transport calibration predicts the held modulation,
including silent starts. Reciprocal transfer on one material is therefore
compatible with a local contact shift. The two materials exclude a common
lapse for this response. Identifying a gravitational redshift requires
an independent optical signal and exterior clocks on the same source.%
\footnote{\href{https://github.com/FloatingPragma/observer-patch-holography/tree/main/model/candidate_laws/interval_echo/openwave/row_27_metric/frequency_link}{Reciprocal material frequency-transfer records}.}

\paragraph{Directional focusing and a separately metered flux.}
At exposure four the two screens supply a matrix \(J\) of centroid
changes with respect to the two launch biases. Denote derivatives in
exchange time by dots and set \(B=\dot J J^{-1}\),
\(C=\ddot J J^{-1}\). In the same parallel screen frame, a common
affine retiming \(\lambda(t)\) and a common tidal matrix require
\(C_1-C_2=(\ddot\lambda/\dot\lambda)(B_1-B_2)\).
Two entries require the same real number to lie respectively in
\([-9.906,-9.900]\) and \([19.772,19.779]\).
Thus these resolved images do not propagate as a common Jacobi field
under that identification. A different physical screen transport or a
limit suppressing probe-dependent propagation would require independent
calibration.

A separate instrument kicks the central bridge on a tagged, equally
weighted mixture of launches from each end. Local energy continuity,
including the boundary terms of the receiving cell, fixes the signed
energy current in native units. The ordinary source change gives
current slopes in \([-3.1100,-3.0651]\) and \([-2.0737,-2.0445]\)
for vacancy and excess probes, respectively. The forward-minus-reverse
responses of \(-\operatorname{tr} C\) to that change have opposite
signs, in \([9742,10088]\) and \([-13606,-13239]\). An oriented null
pair in one orthonormal frame obeys
\(T(k_+,k_+)-T(k_-,k_-)=4T_{0n}\). Energy density and pressure cancel
from this test. The signs exclude a common signed conversion between
the two instruments for a common affine exchange clock and a fixed
orientation. Calibrating each probe on ordinary matter separately gives
observed-to-predicted collar responses of \([1.05125,1.05695]\)
and \([1.03003,1.03492]\), respectively.

A common clock correction can be tested even if it varies with the
source. Write \(f\) for the odd focusing, \(s\) for the difference
of the two expansions, \(j\) for the bridge current and
\(u=\ddot\lambda/\dot\lambda\). A common physical conversion
with fixed units requires \(f+us=cj\) for both probes, with one
constant \(c\). The reference measurements admit compatible
values. At that reference the ordinary perturbation requires
\(\delta u=(c\,\delta j-\delta f-u\,\delta s)/s\).
The vacancy requires \(89767<\delta u<131335\), while the excess
requires \(262529<\delta u<296075\). An independent conversion
of this extensive current to flux per proper section could vary with
the source. That conversion would need a separate area and clock
measurement. In particular, suppose the local bridge section is a
fixed positive fraction of the measured tetrahedral boundary area
\(A\), with fixed lapse. Then \(c=-C/A\), \(C>0\).
The measured stationary entropy gain gives \(\delta A<0\) on both
arms, hence \(\delta c=C\,\delta A/A^2<0\). Solving the two odd
response equations simultaneously for \(\delta u,\delta c\)
requires \(\delta c>0\) on both arms. The measured area contraction
has the wrong sign for this proposed conversion. Identifying separate
screens with rays at one spacetime
event, and the bridge energy current with proper regional flux, are
additional physical hypotheses. The measurements and source enclosures
are in \path{code/matched_einstein_limit/source_implication/},
under \path{jacobi_screen/} and \path{directional_stress/}.

\paragraph{Separating diffraction from a change of clock or flux units.}
Three calibrated phase settings on the five-port screens preserve the
incident mean state, the aperture throughput and the bridge current.
Denote the forward-minus-reverse expansion and exchange-time focusing
at one appointment by \(s_i,f_i\) for setting \(i\).
If all three measure the same odd curvature with the same ray
normalization, their points \((s_i,f_i)\) must lie on one line,
\(f_i+u s_i=h\), even if both the clock correction \(u\) and the
flux conversion in \(h\) depend freely on the state. An independent
version uses identical sharp launches in every trial and chooses only
the final receiving phases. Its oriented collinearity determinant lies
in \([0.81,1.43]\) for a vacancy and \([538,560]\) for an excess.
The outgoing states and their propagation are identical before this
last measurement. The apparent change therefore includes a response
of the analyzer. In the two-ended instrument, averaging random phases
and inverting the independently calibrated two-port intensity matrices
recovers the sharp image and its derivatives. This returns the sharp
ordinary-to-collar discrepancy above. Neither a free clock correction
nor a fitted bridge area can identify all the raw images with one
curvature. These tests concern a finite diffractive instrument.
The full click records are in \path{jacobi_screen/coherent_ports/}.

\paragraph{Measuring the work of stretching one bridge.}
Two swap-symmetric strain protocols on the same source change the
isolated exchange gap to \(s\) and produce the same endpoint transfer
curve. Let \(P_-\) project onto the antisymmetric orbital, and \(D\)
onto contact occupation. One protocol scales
\(P_-+8D\) by \(s\); the other scales \(P_-\) alone.
A Ramsey comparison against an outside port independently measures
the symmetric level offset divided by the exchange gap. It is 8 on
unstrained material. At \(s=3/4\) the two 99 percent count intervals
are \([7.9953,8.0092]\) and \([10.6571,10.6727]\), respectively.
Preserving the measured material index selects the first actuator
uniquely in the head-independent, three-level swap-symmetric class,
up to a common scalar. Its work per unit logarithmic length is thereby
specified. A physical lapse and a uniform change of the contact
energies can produce identical contact probabilities. Identifying
longitudinal work per proper volume as regional normal pressure
requires a local calibration of spatial strain independent of lapse.
Three disjoint material oscillators give an independent check. A strain
on the central contact leaves the two adjacent dimers at unit rate.
A common change of elapsed time to three quarters slows all three.

For a bridge term \(H=N e^{-\epsilon}(P_-+8D)\), variation of
lapse \(N\) and minus variation of logarithmic length \(\epsilon\) at
\((1,0)\) both give \(P_-+8D\). If these terms supply the local
energy and longitudinal pressure in fixed flux and volume units, their
sum predicts the even part of null focusing. The held ratio after
ordinary calibration is \([0.98491,0.99490]\) for a vacancy and
\([0.77909,0.78841]\) for an excess. Both lie below one. The independent
odd current ratios lie above one. A common positive multiplier of the
collar response leaves at least a 14.28 percent relative discrepancy
among the four sharp-screen comparisons even if chosen after observing
all four. Indeed for any two measured ratios \(l>h>0\) the least
possible maximum of \(|l/a-1|,|h/a-1|\) over \(a>0\) is
\((l-h)/(l+h)\). These conclusions apply to the specified finite
lapse, work and focusing assignments. The same-source strain and
impulse records are in \path{directional_stress/traction/}.

\paragraph{Time-resolved measurements on the live bridge.}
On the 80-cell reciprocal-strain prototype the observers retain the
bridge strain and the two original seam heads after a four-tick pump.
Across 17,496 links of orders one through four, reciprocal leading
return coefficients measure six of the nine independent head correlations
at fixed marginals. Nine first-relay time bins resolve all nine directions.
The records then determine the regional modular response and the signed
Hamiltonian energy current at the pump appointment.
Two preparable lotteries agree on all the leading fronts and differ
in energy current on two nested cuts. A common additive shift of the
Hamiltonian cannot remove both differences.

A separate detector measures an interior census and the outward and inward
number currents on a 12-port region. Include its two heads, bridge strain
and all enclosed spent-mobile modes, with a local vacuum when the mobile
is outside. A preparation curve holds all three detector means fixed.
Per unit decorrelation probability the regional entropy slope lies in
$[0.21031,0.21060]$ for a vacancy probe and $[0.21372,0.21403]$ for an
excess probe. Thus assigning area and volume solely from those three
readings cannot give first-order generalized-entropy stationarity on
this source. Recovering null balance requires an independent geometric
response on the same proper regions. The detector, regional spectra and
current certificates accompany the finite calculations in
\path{rate_geometry/seam_strain/weak_ruler/}.

\paragraph{Equal-volume balls averaged over observer positions.}
At the same pump appointment fill the five-station reciprocal radar star
by four Euclidean tetrahedra. Choose centers uniformly in a fixed small
proper volume along the middle thirds of its interior edges. Each observer
adjusts the radius to enclose the volume $4\pi\ell^3/3$. Only a fraction
of order $\ell^2$ lie within reach of an edge, and the area loss of each
affected ball is of order $\ell^2$. Their mean has an exact fourth-power
term. Its coefficient depends on whether each observer fixes the volume
or only the ensemble fixes its mean volume.

For individually matched balls, calibration of the area-loss coefficient
on the ordinary rail and the twelve-port regional entropy predicts a
response between $2.898\times10^{-5}$ and $3.033\times10^{-5}$ per unit
of head re-pairing. Independent radar gives $0.0018187$ to $0.0019239$
in the same units. Two physical controls can instead keep both the mean
area and the enrolled number exactly fixed near the reference. The regional
entropy then increases at a rate between $0.01354$ and $0.04251$ nats per
unit re-pairing. The continuum distribution of observer positions is an
explicit extension of the station records. A regional algebra measured
on shrinking proper balls would have to specify which modes enter those
balls and how their entropy changes. The geometry, controls and enclosures
are in \path{weak_ruler/center_average/} within the same package.
Four graph-distance classes partition the enrolled ports. Two native
preparation lotteries have identical energy and number on every class
at every common admixture to the reference. Their individually matched
area-loss coefficients nevertheless have different first variations.
Hence even independent signed weights for the four shell energies and
the four shell counts cannot recover this geometric response.



\paragraph{Which part of conservation is supplied by the pump?}
For the 80-cell reciprocal-strain pump prototype let $P_i$ be the mobile's port projector
and $H$ its joint generator, including the heads and strain. Distribute
energy as $e_i=(P_iH+HP_i)/2$. The directed currents
$j_{ij}=i(P_iH^2P_j-P_jH^2P_i)/2$ are antisymmetric and obey
$\dot e_i=-\sum_j j_{ij}$ in the Heisenberg picture for every state.
For time-dependent regional membership $w_i$, the outward loss is
$\sum_{i<j}(w_i-w_j)j_{ij}-\sum_i\dot w_i e_i$. On the reference
the membership change contributes $+1.52744\times10^{-5}$ to the outer
energy inventory. All 24 endpoint and current comparisons include zero
residual with this contribution. This supplies material energy
conservation on these graph regions. Proper stress and the spatial
momentum equations require further measurements.
See \path{weak_ruler/port_ward/} in the matched Einstein package.

\paragraph{A regional entropy response that survives refinement.}
On active Interval Echo, protect two written words and clock each adjacent
station crossing in seven addressed appointments. On the declared midpoint
refinement, a corner annulus of relative radius $\ell$ admits one uniform
word with probability asymptotic to $(27/16)\ell^2$. Two distinct uniformly
placed words are both admitted with probability asymptotic to
$(729/256)\ell^4$. Counting five triangular sectors fixes these coefficients
before the word read. At the reversed-tag reference, the signed entropy
slope of the full addressed word memory divided by $\ell^4$ tends to
$0.0094118671$ nats. With independently tagged original faces the limit is
$0.0018823734$. All protected route readings agree under this variation.
Assigning area solely from these readings fails the fixed-volume
stationarity requirement at leading order.

Even a reader given both local words has a sensitivity bound. Write $p$
for the correlated two-word admission probability. The positive part
of the signed regional state variation has trace $p/72$. An area outcome
of range $L$ on $m$ independent preparations has mean slope bounded by
$mLp/72$. Cancellation to $o(\ell^4)$ requires
$\liminf\eta mL\ge 0.23796918$ nats for positive area coupling $\eta$.
This is a constraint on local measurement resources. Proper area and
stress require independent definitions. For the original fastest-swap
clock the annular coefficient has a positive limit on a cofinal subsequence;
the explicit coefficient uses completed crossings.
See \path{collar_scaling/station_clock/} in the Einstein station experiment.

At a uniformly selected station the same calibration gives coefficients
$27/10$ for one word in the closed ball and $81/40$ for its outer
annulus, after division by $\ell^2$. Almost every ball lies inside an
original face as the subdivision is refined. The mean entropy slope for
the addressed annulus is $0.0135530886\,\ell^4+o(\ell^4)$ nats for
both the global and the independent-face tag preparations. The route
area response vanishes at every center, so the discrepancy persists
after observer averaging. For fastest routes a comparison that keeps all
dual edges and changes only gateway labels has two different limiting
coefficients. Incidence alone cannot select the original clock's limit.

An independent comparison of the order of two measured seam energies at
each of two stations reads an explicitly nonlocal part of the modular
response. Keep two nonadjacent original face sectors fixed throughout
refinement. Change their tags from oppositely paired toward independent.
On the whole corner ball the signed word-entropy slope divided by
$\ell^4$ tends to $0.00133857665$ nats. A sum of word observables
of range $o(R)$ in a ball of $R$ station hops has zero limiting
coefficient whenever its summed operator strength per station is
uniformly bounded. Both results use the same preparations. Thus a
local stress assignment on these recorded words would miss a measured
part of the entropy. The comparison on a larger region requires its
material stress and geometric response.

\paragraph{What the separated sources and exterior clocks measure.}
On the active 20-cell board a freely moving mobile can visit two confined
neutral dipoles at noncontact separations. For dipoles on equal-degree
ports each source has sharp static energy 116.5 exchange units. Reversing
one dipole changes the sign of their connected thermal interaction.
A fair average over orientations restores attraction at four separations,
but three successive energy differences require mutually incompatible
inverse-radius strengths. This measures a mediated interaction with
no common Newton coefficient on the tested distances. In the dilute
one-mobile sector the connected thermal response is bounded in magnitude
by $C(6/35)^{2d}$ uniformly in inverse temperature, where $d$ is the
separation of the two coupling supports in mobile hops. With equally
likely charges and equilibrated orientations, the magnitude of the
zero-or-one mobile connected potential on the midpoint family is
$o(1/R)$ at fixed positive activity and increasing cell separation $R$.
This excludes a Newtonian tail in that channel in graph units.
In a separate
two-cell source, uniformly illuminating ten exterior sites gives a marked
gate probability of $0.1238075$ on an empty pole and $0.1392475$ on an
occupied pole. A source-independent mixture of lossless exterior
propagators would preserve the uniform probability $0.1$ at each site.
A held material-source prediction covers 800 site probabilities. These
active-law measurements and the assumptions of their force and clock
interpretations are in \path{row_26_newton/mobile_mediator/} and
\path{row_27_metric/exterior_channel/} in the Interval Echo OpenWave package.

\paragraph{An inverse-square current on measured guides.}
Three sharp markers move on disjoint guides, with one marker per coordinate.
The sign of each offered step is fair. Let $q_i$ denote the measured
probability of one specified sign on guide $i$.
A unit release at the eight central addresses and absorption at the outer
collector give a visit count $u$. The expected signed current on a link is
$J_i(x)=q_i[u(x)-u(x+e_i)]$. Its divergence is the release distribution
by one-step balance. In three address dimensions the transient lattice
Green function with covariance $2\operatorname{diag}(q_i)$ fixes the
far coefficient. For equal rates,
\[
  J_i(x)=\frac{x_i}{4\pi |x|^3}+o(|x|^{-2}).
\]
Here $|x|\to\infty$ and $|x|^2/L\to0$, with $L$ the distance scale
of the collector in address steps. The finite boundary error is
$O(1/L)$ by the maximum principle. Three disjoint paths of arbitrarily
large length exist along strips of subdivided original faces in the
midpoint construction. On the deposited 80-, 320- and 1,280-cell boards,
explicit routes give three guides of lengths 36, 84 and 180 respectively.
On three twelve-port guides, doubling the
step rate along one axis predicts $0.57893$ of all exits on that axis.
The count is $37{,}959$ of $65{,}536$ launches. Each marker has zero
mean step at every interior site, including where $J$ is nonzero.
A sourced flux in this chart therefore supplies no measurement of
attraction between masses.
The derivation and the finite records are in
\path{interval_echo_shell_flux/product_address/green_response/}
under the model experiments.

\paragraph{An entropy change on the same source.}
Let $\sigma_i=\pm1$ specify the central site on guide $i$ and draw
the released triple with probability
$(1+b\sigma_1+s\sigma_1\sigma_2\sigma_3)/8$.
At $b=0$, $s=-1/2$, varying $s$ leaves all one- and two-guide
marginals fixed. The entropy of the three released registers has slope
$\log(3)/2$ nats. The timing survey that takes the earliest arrival over
the eight corners keeps each address at its original range as $s$ varies.
Both the boundary count and the enclosed address count are fixed.
The finite modular response has an independent reader. On the occupied
corner support the reference modular Hamiltonian is a constant plus
$(\log(3)/2)\sigma_1\sigma_2\sigma_3$. The product of the three
centred coordinates is harmonic under the walk. Its stopped value,
with coordinates normalized to $\pm1$ on the source corners, reads the
input parity in expectation at the outer collector. Thus
$dS=d\langle K_0\rangle$ on this source while the aperture derivative
vanishes.
At each of the six ranges
the frequency-weighted mean time does respond on the eight body
diagonals, with alternating slopes of magnitude $1/8$ appointment.
The two timing protocols consequently yield different information
about the release. Direct pairwise parallel-arrival ranges among four active sites force
distortion at least $\sqrt{2}$ in any Euclidean embedding, at every
available scale. In particular, diffusion and parallel arrival give
different rulers on these addresses.
For equal rates the derivative of the occupation potential $q u$
has leading term $15x_1x_2x_3/(32\pi |x|^7)$ in the infinite guide.
Reflection cancels it on every complete shell even at finite size.
The entropy changes while the outward flux per release stays one.
Along addressed balls of $R$ layers with $L=R^3$ and $\ell=R/L$,
a perturbation $\delta s=\ell^4\delta\epsilon$ keeps a nonzero
$\ell^{-4}\partial_\epsilon S$ at fixed aperture. This family tests a
uniform statement over these source lotteries. A continuum application
requires identifying the perturbation and the regions in a common proper
metric. The signed null balance does not follow from counting boundary
states. The clock and energy measurements are in
\path{interval_echo_shell_flux/product_address/corner_source/}.

\paragraph{How much of the source a stress instrument must read.}
On a separate stationary two-head source on the 80-cell board, the
differentials of survey area and volume are operators on the same two
exchange-parity blocks. Restricting to fixed volume identifies the
target operator up to addition of a common multiple of the volume
differential and a trace constant. Remove the single-head terms. Each
remaining block is a real $3$-by-$3$ matrix of Pauli correlation
coefficients. A product of one spin direction at each head has rank
one within a chosen parity. For every real volume multiplier the
two blocks have rank at least two, and at least one has rank three.
These bounds include the certified uncertainty of the stationary state
and the native survey operators. A determinant changes sign in a
certified bracket, giving a representative with ranks two and three.
Thus five parity-resolved products suffice for the exact response,
and four cannot recover it for all source variations. A deposited
five-term approximation misses at most $3.11\times10^{-7}$ nats
per unit Hilbert--Schmidt tangent. Its coefficients were obtained
from the survey. Independently measuring five suitable correlations
and deriving their coefficients from propagating matter and collar
stress is a distinct physical test. See
\path{source_implication/full_algebra/correlation_rank/}
in the matched Einstein calculation.
The same equilibrium also admits a variation at fixed survey volume,
fixed single-head marginals in both parities, and fixed independently
defined translation signals. Both probe charges retain their phase
shift, domain-cut flux, image-cut flux and marked force. The Ward
identities then preserve the total boundary flux and momentum rate.
On a unit Hilbert--Schmidt tangent the head entropy derivative lies
between $0.00159319$ and $0.00159487$ nats, with a compensating
survey-area decrease. A certified right inverse for the nine constraint
derivatives on the eighteen correlation coordinates extends this
tangent to a local curve of equal readings. Consequently every
differentiable scalar function of these twelve translation readings
and the protected marginals misses the signed response somewhere on
this preparation. No choice of their common coupling repairs this
loss of information. Regional observables outside this translation
experiment require a separate test.

Resolving individual contacts changes the information available. Ten
directed four-tick contact-flux measurements together with eight
independent regional readings determine all eighteen correlation
coordinates at fixed single-head marginals and parity weights.
The smallest singular value of this material map lies between
$7.42\times10^{-13}$ and $8.34\times10^{-13}$ in the deposited units.
One can use the reference density's modular operator $-\log\rho_0$
and this material map to reconstruct the signed entropy derivative,
without using the survey area or volume for the weights. The uniform
error bound for the rounded reader is $1.59\times10^{-5}$ nats per
unit Hilbert--Schmidt tangent. On a held variation, the two-charge
material replay gives $0.0015940293$ nats per unit variation, compared
with $0.0015940511$ from the spectrum. Absolute errors in the individual
readings can be amplified by a factor of $2.27\times10^9$.
This is a statement about the expectations of the instruments.
It distinguishes informational completeness from a stress law.
In particular, keeping two prescribed quadratic aperture readings
at graph radius six, the regional readings and survey volume fixed
leaves a tangent with entropy slope between $0.0007221$ and $0.0007314$
nats per unit variation. Thus the contact-resolved information can
recover what this candidate physical weighting omits. The calculation
is in \path{correlation_rank/spatial_cuts/}.

\paragraph{Finite same-source balance on the complete survey algebra.}
Write $z$ for the thirteen measured marginal coordinates (the parity
weight and the two single-head Pauli triples in each parity), and $x$
for the eighteen two-head correlation coordinates. Together they
parametrize the trace-one part of $M_4\oplus M_4$. Each of the eighteen
material means $j$ is affine in this state. Thus
$\delta j=B\,\delta x+C\,\delta z$, with $B$ the invertible native
matrix above. For the faithful reference $\rho_*$ let
$\delta\langle-\log\rho_*\rangle=k_x\delta x+k_z\delta z$.
Set $w=k_xB^{-1}$ and $v=k_z-w C$. The finite first law gives
\[
    \delta S=w\,\delta j+v\,\delta z
\]
throughout the 31-dimensional trace-preserving tangent space. The reference was
selected by the constrained survey equilibrium. Once it is prepared,
the reference density and the material response operators fix these
weights independently of the geometric variation under test. Since
the selected source extremizes $S+\eta A$ at fixed surveyed volume,
the entire 30-dimensional fixed-volume tangent space satisfies
\[
     \eta\,\delta A+w\,\delta j+v\,\delta z=0 .
\]
The coefficient $\eta$ comes from the ordinary transfer calibration.
The rounded full reader has certified error below $1.783\times10^{-5}$
nats per unit Hilbert--Schmidt variation. In a held native replay the
two arms change both head marginals and the parity weight, with their
survey volumes matched to numerical precision. The material and
marginal terms give $+17.6617754$ and $-17.3273213$ nats per unit
transfer. Their sum is $0.3344540635$. Direct entropy differencing
gives $0.3344540613$, and the ordinary-calibrated area decrease gives
$0.3344540641$. These values come from expected records in the
two-arm calculation.
This is an operational balance at one four-station survey. A
stress-energy interpretation requires a physical generator for the
weights on proper balls across radii and frames.

\paragraph{Transporting the entropy reading between material instruments.}
Keep the marked source and four-tick flight fixed and mount three distinct
partial translations on domains of twelve, ten and nine cells. On each
mount 31 regional and local momentum readings have an invertible response
$B_i$ on the full trace-one source. Hence $B_jB_i^{-1}$ transfers every
finite source difference between mounts. Using 31 readings across all
three reduces the modular noise gain to $5.01\times10^6$ nats per unit
absolute error in a detector difference. A separate fair lottery over
head products and reversal parity supplies a tracial calibration stock.
Subtracting its material means determines the prepared density, hence
its logarithm, entirely from material records. On two equal-survey-volume
preparation pairs this gives entropy slopes $-0.069481704$ and
$+0.125540977$, compared with $-0.069481705$ and $+0.125540979$ from
the ordinary-calibrated area. Every pair of mounts also predicts all 31
changes on the omitted mount, with relative errors below
$5.72\times10^{-11}$ in the independent flights. Whole-region readings
suffice as well. Restrict each shift to its central cell and then to
its domain within one graph step. Any two mounts determine the whole
source from 31 of their 32 regional means and predict the full omitted
mount to relative error below $6.05\times10^{-11}$. Transport of
a geometric boost weight to shrinking proper balls is a separate requirement.%
\footnote{\href{https://github.com/FloatingPragma/observer-patch-holography/tree/main/code/matched_einstein_limit/source_implication/full_algebra/correlation_rank/spatial_cuts/chart_transport}{Native material response matrices, interval bounds and held flights.}}

\paragraph{Which local geometry does a finite clock survey determine?}
Six stations on the same prepared source give fifteen two-way ranges.
After projecting the directional readings to reciprocal lengths one can
realize every length exactly on a smooth spatial three-sphere. Construct
a surface from six round chambers with individual outlets and tubes, one
per measured pair. Their lengths account for the radial travel inside
each chamber. The shortest competing two-tube route exceeds the
longest direct route. Place the surface in a product collar in a
three-sphere, wide enough that exiting the collar cannot shorten any
measured route. Projection to the surface shortens paths inside it.
This construction concerns the projected lengths. For the raw directed
means the best common static clock calibration has a maximum
logarithmic error between $0.003729$ and $0.003734$.

Even among smooth realizations on this fixed topology, the measured
lengths leave precisely the coefficient needed in the Einstein inference
undetermined. In a normal ball about one measured station, put
\[
 g_e=g+e\chi(r)r^2(g-dr^2),\qquad e\geq0,
\]
where the cutoff is one near zero and the ball avoids all selected
minimizers that do not meet the station. This is smooth since
$r^2dr^2=d(r^2)\otimes d(r^2)/4$. It lengthens transverse directions
and preserves radial lengths. Each measured route retains its exact
length. In three spatial dimensions it multiplies the proper rim area
by $1+er^2$. At fixed proper volume the leading area change is
$12\pi e r^4/5$. The scalar curvature changes by $-18e$ at the station
and, in the ultrastatic extension, every unit-normalized null
contraction of the Ricci tensor changes by $-6e$.
Thus the ambiguity is of order $r^4$ even for exact range measurements.
At a round station one can also interchange two eigenvalues of a small
positive transverse deformation. A rotation then preserves every
centered ball's area and volume while the two metrics have opposite
null-curvature responses along a marked ray. Frame-resolved geometric
data on the same matter variations enter independently of a scalar
volume normalization.

The timelike area test can be run directly on these two interpolations.
Give the angular deformation in the round chamber the matrices
$B_\pm=bI\pm s\,\mathrm{diag}(1,-1,0)$ relative to the common reference
$bI$, with the first axis pointing along a tagged station ray.
The spatial Ricci slopes are $\pm3\,\mathrm{diag}(1,-1,0)$, so their
trace vanishes and the timelike Einstein slopes at
$u=(5/3,4/3,0,0)$ are $\pm16/3$. Independently integrate each tilted
geodesic ball and adjust its radius to give volume $4\pi\ell^3/3$.
At $\ell=1/128,1/256,1/512,1/1024$ in survey units, the respective
values of $(A_+-A_-)/(2s\ell^4)$ are $-4.54560$, $-4.48724$,
$-4.47283$ and $-4.46924$. The limit is $-64\pi/45$. Any estimate from
the shared station readings has worst-case leading area error at least
$64\pi/45$ per unit of this angular deformation. These areas are predictions
of two smooth interpretations of the same finite observations.%
\footnote{\href{https://github.com/FloatingPragma/observer-patch-holography/tree/main/code/matched_einstein_limit/source_implication/full_geometry/station_balls/timelike_areas}{Moving-ball quadratures, equal-volume roots and station lengths.}}

If the spacetime continuation may also vary with time, multiply its
metric by $\exp[2st\,(\mathbf a\cdot\mathbf x)\chi(r)]$ about that
station. All null paths as unparameterized curves and all six station
clocks are preserved. The entire spatial metric at $t=0$ agrees as well.
At the station event the conformal exponent and its gradient vanish,
but its mixed Hessian changes $R_{kk}$ by $-4s\,\mathbf a\cdot\mathbf n$
for $k=(1,\mathbf n)$. This accounts for the odd directional response
that rest-frame areas cannot measure. Both constructions are
interpolations of the finite records. A model-selected geometry must
supply an additional observable to distinguish them.
There is also a constructive direction-resolved test. For a smooth
affinely parametrized null wavefront with its tangent normalization
fixed, measure the proper screen area in solid-angle caps about six
axes and their antipodes. Small-radius focusing measures cap averages
of $R_{kk}$. The six axes through opposite vertices of an icosahedron
give a rotation-covariant reconstruction: antipodal sums recover the
even quadratic form and differences recover its three odd components. Caps of half-angle
$60^\circ$ have quadrupole coefficient $3/8$ and dipole coefficient
$3/4$, so the inversion is nonsingular. If each measured cap residual against
an independently calibrated stress has magnitude at most $\delta$, the
null residual in any direction is bounded by $\sqrt{377}\,\delta/3
<6.48\delta$, when the input bounds include normalization, stress
uncertainty and finite-radius bias. To take the curvature limit,
absolute screen-area errors must be $o(r^4)$. The six-station radar
survey supplies travel times. A thirteen-bin native angular acquisition
on the same 80-cell source resolves all nine directional combinations
using signed weights; the unweighted angular design has rank four.
These are occupation measurements. Measuring proper screen areas and
calibrated stress on these directions would test physical null balance.
\footnote{\href{https://github.com/FloatingPragma/observer-patch-holography/tree/main/code/matched_einstein_limit/source_implication/full_geometry/station_balls}{Six-station geometry, proof and wavefront observables.}}

\paragraph{Reading a common radial stress weight.}
On the full stationary survey, allow an arbitrary signed weight on each
of six graph-distance shells, an independent relative normalization of
the two probe charges, all eight regional material summaries and an
arbitrary survey-volume multiplier. For every relative normalization
there is a unit two-head correlation variation that those readings
miss, with a signed entropy response exceeding
$1.30\times10^{-6}$ nats. A directed interval cover includes the
limiting case where only the second probe contributes. Allowing a
separate six-shell profile for each charge recovers all eighteen
correlation coordinates when combined with the summaries. The
distinction concerns an actual finite preparation and a specified
material instrument. Physical transfer to proper balls requires a law
selecting the spatial weights in each frame and controlling the omitted
response on refinement.
There is a positive result at finer resolution. Allow the same price
for both charges at each of the twelve individual cells, retaining
the regional means. These readings distinguish all eighteen correlation
variations. Combining them with the reference state gives a signed
entropy reader with uniform bias below $1.01\times10^{-5}$ nats per
unit Hilbert--Schmidt variation for exact material means. An absolute
error $\epsilon$ in each input can add up to $3.46\times10^{22}\epsilon$
to this bound. An exact such reader requires prices
within at least one graph shell to differ by more than $1.05\times10^{18}$
in native transfer units. In independently propagated arms at the same
survey volume $2.7634718368$, the cell reader gives $0.000247296$, the
spectral entropy slope is $0.000247295$ and the ordinary-calibrated
negative area slope is $0.000247297$.
The cell record therefore carries the missing response on this survey.
Applying the physical theorem requires a transport law for these
weights across shrinking proper balls and frames.
\footnote{\href{https://github.com/FloatingPragma/observer-patch-holography/tree/main/code/matched_einstein_limit/source_implication/full_algebra/correlation_rank/spatial_cuts/radial_profiles}{Radial and cell-resolved material measurements.}}

\paragraph{A finite-density source in the Newton comparison.}
A separate preparation clamps two equal-energy neutral pairs and the
exterior loads beside a nine-port guide on the native board. At inverse temperature $1/16$ and
chemical potential $30$ the guide contains $4.11$ vacancies on average.
Turning one source reverses its contact field, changing the neighboring
vacancy probability from $0.4386$ to $0.3343$. Summing all 512
configurations and averaging the four connected free energies over
independent source orientations gives attraction at five guide
separations. The first three energies have strictly positive second
difference, whereas every attractive potential $u-C/(d+c)$ has
negative second difference for $d+c>0$. Removing the intervening
bond gives zero interaction at fixed fugacity. Fixing the total number
of vacancies instead yields a positive signal even after this cut.
A connected-cluster bound gives exponential decay throughout a
sufficiently high-temperature interval, uniformly in system size
and fugacity. The finite-density mechanism therefore supplies a local
response and a controlled range test. A physical Newton measurement
also requires freely moving sources, their proper separation and a
common coupling.
\footnote{\href{https://github.com/FloatingPragma/observer-patch-holography/tree/main/model/candidate_laws/interval_echo/openwave/row_26_newton/mobile_mediator/finite_density}{Finite-density source experiment and range theorem.}}

\paragraph{A second load responds to the three-guide current.}
A sharp source sends three marked loads down separately metered guides.
At each appointment a fourth load starts in a real, equal superposition
on two adjacent receiver ports. Three independently switched native
contacts interrogate the source coordinates in sequence. At the
ordinary receiver clock, a missed coordinate reverses the coherence
when its switch is enabled. Averaging the switches therefore leaves
coherence only on a joint address match. Every trial ends in a physical
right-or-left port reading. The two mirror arms of the receiver
interrogate adjacent addresses, including all trials in the comparison.

Let $u(x)$ count mean visits per source release and let
$J_i=[u(x)-u(x+e_i)]/8$ be the independently metered outward
current. At the registered free-flight time the signed displacement of
the receiver loads summed per release is $B_i=-(520/4229)J_i$.
Four successive outward faces give $-0.00515$, $-0.00212$,
$-0.00106$ and $-0.00065$ receiver links per release, each with
simultaneous 99\% half-width below $0.000186$. The reflected inner
face gives $+0.00516$. The guide Green theorem transfers an inward
inverse-square response to this receiver in the interior scaling
limit. The freely propagating one-load and two-load position
generators differ on the whole blank board by exactly $144I$, so their
bare transition probabilities agree at every time. Yet the two-load
test probe receives a complete phase turn per missed coordinate at the
original clock and has zero mean recoil at every release time.
The supplied face routing, phase clock, mirrored pad bias and source
activity determine this coupling. Its failure to transfer between the
two receivers limits its gravitational interpretation.
\footnote{\href{https://github.com/FloatingPragma/observer-patch-holography/tree/main/model/experiments/interval_echo_shell_flux/product_address/green_response/material_recoil}{Native recoil, source counts and receiver controls.}}

A second receiver uses a three-phase contact sequence and releases its
test load on a blank edge whose endpoints have different degrees.
At stiffness eight, both test loads acquire the same detuning on this
edge. The initial free acceleration per matched source release equals
$-J_i$ for either load. In independent trials the ordinary and held
loads give $-0.06671$ and $-0.06664$ receiver links per release at the
first face (forecast $-1/15$), then $-0.01399$ and $-0.01403$ at the
third (forecast $-0.01400$). The shared response also transfers to
a mixture of source loads with the species hidden. Changing the
stiffness removes both signals. This
\href{https://github.com/FloatingPragma/observer-patch-holography/tree/main/model/experiments/interval_echo_shell_flux/product_address/green_response/material_recoil/common_receiver}{common receiver}
supplies a material readout of the inverse-square current in the guide
addresses. To express this response per unit source mass at a given
proper distance, observers must measure how the emission rate depends
on source mass and independently range the source and receiver.

A four-link material circuit supplies an independent source count.
A standing wave across the ring measures exchange excitation above
the ground state. A uniformly selected link releases one guide source
when its antisymmetric detector clicks. For any preparation of either
mobile load, the release probability equals one quarter of its exchange
excitation in the measured units. The shared receiver therefore
accelerates inward in proportion to that excitation. Silencing one of
the four apertures separates preparations of the same energy. The
proportionality uses the complete
\href{https://github.com/FloatingPragma/observer-patch-holography/tree/main/model/experiments/interval_echo_shell_flux/product_address/green_response/material_recoil/energy_source}{four-link source instrument}.
A mass measurement also requires the rest energy, and comparison at
a proper separation requires a physical range measurement.

At fixed particle counts a separate experiment changes binding energy
by changing the separation within the source. Independent parked
Ramsey clocks read far-minus-near energy differences of $17$, $32.5$
and $65$ native units. A common lottery of 1024 tickets reads the
native diagonal terms and the antisymmetric exchange on the active
contact. Its click probability is exactly $\langle H\rangle/512$,
constant during free exchange. Each click triggers one guide release.
For the largest binding contrast, the two distal probes report
near-minus-far rightward displacements in $[0.007973,0.009094]$
and $[0.007981,0.009102]$ receiver links, against the independent
Ramsey forecast $[0.008462,0.008465]$. Thus both probes read less
inward displacement for the paired source. The
\href{https://github.com/FloatingPragma/observer-patch-holography/tree/main/model/experiments/interval_echo_shell_flux/product_address/green_response/material_recoil/energy_source/binding}{binding measurement}
tests a programmed energy-to-release conversion in the material
address chart used for OW-26.

\paragraph{Two stationary sources at matched survey geometry.}
On the native 80-cell board, let the two outgoing heads seed the next
radar cycle. A preparation instrument draws one of twelve directed
routes with positive probabilities and uses a fair probe charge.
Changing the route probabilities selects a different attracting
stationary ensemble at the same ordinary head energy, $0.3657$.
A separate four-station observer uses a fixed survey on fresh copies.
Two selected preparations give all six reconstructed lengths equal
to numerical precision, at tetrahedral area $14.2955956754$ and
volume $2.76347115815$. The exact native channels give an upper
bound of $7.73\times10^{-7}$ on the largest edge difference.
The independent directed timestamps retain
information beyond these six lengths. Between the two ensembles the
complete free-flight entropy rises by $5.95\times10^{-6}$ nats;
the four stations with their two heads gain $5.56\times10^{-6}$ nats.
The oriented bridge energy current changes from $-0.010567$ to
$-0.011100$. A parked local head detector also distinguishes the
ensembles in independent counts. The route lotteries are supplied,
and the geometric match has finite precision. A stationary source on
a chosen bath can therefore be distinguished from another by a
material instrument at a precision where this spatial survey cannot
separate their geometries. An entropy-balance interpretation requires
a physical bath selection or additional geometry on the same regions.
Switch both settled preparations to the same uniform route lottery.
After one common cycle their surveyed volumes differ by
$1.31\times10^{-7}$ and after two by $2.51\times10^{-7}$.
A central-current calibration from a separate pair predicts a second
volume difference 24 times the observed magnitude. These finite
changes use the intervention cycle as a clock. The route probabilities,
release calculations and enclosures are in
\path{source_implication/full_algebra/reuse/stationary_baths/}.

\paragraph{An attracting entropy equilibrium on the original heads.}
Observers can maintain the finite entropy--area equilibrium while
retaining both head memories after every radar cycle. They record
the chosen route and probe charge, the conserved reversal sector,
and whether the return preceded the deadline. For each record they
align the outgoing head eigenstates and apply a lottery of reversible
rotations. The partial sums of the aligned eigenvalues determine exactly
which reference spectra a lottery can recover. With the return bit, all
six inequalities on the two sectors have positive margins above
$0.00638$. Erasing the bit makes the second inequality fail in both
sectors, even for arbitrary two-head rotations. The controller includes
unread random Pauli rotations on one eighth of cycles. It attracts every
initial ensemble with the specified reversal weights to the reference,
with trace-distance contraction at most $7/8$ per cycle.

The strict spectral margins also permit an exact comparison on the
selected equilibrium. Every nearby target within $0.001$ in trace norm
has its own attracting controller. A small rotation about the observed
right coin's polarization holds the radar volume fixed during two
interventions. An unread left sign measurement increases parked energy
by $[0.29756288,0.29756345]$ and entropy by $[0.40942660,0.40948019]$
nats per unit intervention probability. Independently replacing partners
preserves the left head, the unconditional right marginal and the
parked energy, yet increases entropy by $[0.23107941,0.23113299]$.
At the selected reference, the same ordinary calibration gives signed
area derivatives $[-0.01772317,-0.01772084]$ and
$[-0.01000393,-0.01000160]$ in squared radar units. Both are exact
finite-survey bounds. The strict entropy--area maximum places a
retained point with a negative objective derivative on every sufficiently
short forward interval of the partner-replacement path. Sustaining a
source under repeated observation therefore leaves a physical choice of
which source realizes entropy equilibrium.

Changing the rotation lottery gives a distinct family of attracting
preparations. A small coherent phase adjustment keeps the surveyed
volume fixed. The entropy derivative at the numerical reference is
$+0.40622832$ nats per unit of lottery probability. Independent
interrogation of the twelve directed clocks gives an area slope of
$-0.01758242$ in squared range units, so the ordinary-matter calibration
predicts $+0.40622831$ nats. At the finite step $10^{-5}$,
independent synthetic head and arrival
\href{https://github.com/FloatingPragma/observer-patch-holography/tree/main/code/matched_einstein_limit/source_implication/full_algebra/reuse/retained_equilibrium/observer_banks}{counts}
give an entropy slope of $+0.41249$ $[0.4108,0.4142]$ and an area
slope of $-0.01840$ $[-0.01958,-0.01721]$. Their calibrated residual
lies in $[-0.04143,+0.01637]$ at simultaneous $99\%$ coverage.
Doubling the ordinary gain gives $[-0.49366,-0.38142]$. The
\href{https://github.com/FloatingPragma/observer-patch-holography/tree/main/code/matched_einstein_limit/source_implication/full_algebra/reuse/retained_equilibrium}{stored-head experiment}
requires calibrated feedback to maintain the chosen source.
Interpreting this balance on shrinking proper spacetime balls requires
independent curvature and regional stress measurements.

\paragraph{What transfers to a shorter radar deadline?}
Two observers score the same return timestamps with deadlines two and
four, while the feedback cycle and the original six-unit survey proceed
as before. At the exact reference, six independent changes of pairing
preserve the local head marginals, parity weights, and all twelve original
mean ranges. Within this family the two shorter tetrahedra's four
readings $(A_2,V_2,A_4,V_4)$ have differential rank four. An interval
right-inverse test gives residual below $0.00374$. Thus the signed collar
intervention can keep both earlier areas and both volumes fixed while
reproducing the original six-unit ranges pointwise: entropy increases
and $A_6$ decreases at the reference. In the independent twirl endpoint
banks both shorter areas increase, whereas the six-unit area decreases.
These
\href{https://github.com/FloatingPragma/observer-patch-holography/tree/main/code/matched_einstein_limit/source_implication/full_algebra/reuse/retained_equilibrium/nested_ranges}{nested deadlines}
interrogate the same four stations; choosing proper radii requires another
geometric measurement.

The six long-deadline lengths themselves resolve the shape change. Two
lengthen and four shorten. A common log-length dilation misses an edge
secant by at least $0.02016$ per unit source step at simultaneous $99\%$
coverage. Both endpoint Gram matrices admit Euclidean realizations, so a
flat tetrahedron undergoing a volume-preserving change of shape can give
an area response. Across twelve separately registered proper rotations,
averaging $\delta\log A-\tfrac23\delta\log V$ cancels any common affine
strain and preserves a rotation-independent contribution. This
\href{https://github.com/FloatingPragma/observer-patch-holography/tree/main/code/matched_einstein_limit/source_implication/full_algebra/reuse/retained_equilibrium/shape_readout}{rotation test}
requires twelve physical registrations.

\paragraph{Which bridge reading tracks the source?}
At the same retained reference, ordinary exchange and pairing controls
can hold both entropy and six-unit volume fixed. A fresh four-tick
flight interrogates work $W$ and energy current $J$ on the bridge
between ports 1 and 38. Along that path the certified slopes satisfy
$W'/J'\in[0.09853,0.11006]$ for a vacancy and
$[0.03286,0.03794]$ for an excess. A common nonzero linear combination
of work and current therefore cannot have the zero entropy response
for both probes. In a complementary intervention, vacancy phase counts
alone bracket the adjustment that fixes its current and the volume.
Throughout that bracket the held excess-current interval is
$[-0.01886,-0.00614]$ at simultaneous $99\%$ coverage. These
\href{https://github.com/FloatingPragma/observer-patch-holography/tree/main/code/matched_einstein_limit/source_implication/full_algebra/reuse/retained_equilibrium/bridge_stress}{bridge measurements}
give a cross-material prediction. Identifying a regional null stress
requires its response on the geometric region.

On the same source, the conserved total Hamiltonian distinguishes net
energy injection from regional redistribution. Each mobile contact
couples to at most one head. The orbital preparation, the local head
marginals entering the initial energy, and the parked reversal weights
are unchanged by the collar transfer and its compensating rotation.
The total energy slope therefore vanishes at every free-flight dwell
for both probe charges. At four units a regional calorimeter assigns
unit weight to both marked cells and independent weights in $[0,1]$
to the other 78. The absolute collar-to-ordinary energy slope ratio
is at most $0.005853$ for a vacancy and $0.002852$ for an excess.
The retained entropy response requires at least $0.564323$.
Independently holding the twelve-cell vacancy energy and the survey
volume fixes a path on which the excess regional energy falls
by $0.001015$ to $0.002154$ per unit transfer while the head entropy
rises by $0.229004$ to $0.229753$ nats. This
\href{https://github.com/FloatingPragma/observer-patch-holography/tree/main/code/matched_einstein_limit/source_implication/full_algebra/reuse/retained_equilibrium/regional_energy}{regional energy comparison}
constrains this family of calorimeters. Identifying null stress also
requires the appropriate directional stresses.

\paragraph{Predicting the next geometric reading.}
Commission a fixed survey using 62 two-head calibration standards,
then use separate counts from the two parked heads to forecast
future geometry. On twelve held route preparations the forecasts
cover all 720 directed ranges and all 60 independent volume
readings at cycle counts $0,1,2,4,8$. Each setting uses $2^{44}$
simulated launches. A separate pair agrees on all twelve present
ranges within their joint measurement resolution. Four common
cycles later, six ranges distinguish the pair, and their measured
volumes have disjoint simultaneous intervals
$[2.76266148,2.76267108]$ and $[2.76267344,2.76268304]$
to the displayed precision. Parked memory readings thus carry
predictive information beyond the instantaneous range survey.
The forecasts apply to one appointed route law and one tetrahedron.
A relativistic stress law and proper geometric refinement require
independent tests. The counts and held forecasts are in
\path{source_implication/full_algebra/reuse/stationary_baths/forecast/}.

An independent interval calculation gives a sharper population-level
result on the admitted two-head state family. There exist two faithful
states with exactly the same twelve initial mean ranges for which the
volume difference after four identical cycles lies in
$[2.2483,6.2748]\times10^{-6}$ cubed range units. The initial
constraints are linear in the state. A certified right inverse corrects
a proposed state variation to their exact kernel; both perturbed states
remain positive and the later volume derivative has a fixed sign along
the segment. These mathematically constructed states are distinct from
the pair prepared for the finite-count test. Thus a deterministic law
for future volume using only the twelve present means fails even at
unlimited range precision in this finite family.

\paragraph{Which part of the collar charge does a null observer measure?}
At the faithful stationary source there is a preparable curve that
preserves survey volume, parked energy and both marginal entropies.
Its tangent \(D\) commutes with the reference state \(\chi\).
Per unit curve parameter, the total entropy increases by
\([0.007674,0.007978]\) nats, entirely through loss of mutual
information, and the survey area changes by
\([-0.000352,-0.000326]\). The collar logarithm therefore has a
signed signal. For every candidate charge \(P\) on the full heads,
cyclicity gives
\[
  \operatorname{Tr}(D\chi^{is}P\chi^{-is})=\operatorname{Tr}(DP).
\]
If the signal is nonzero this modular action cannot double it at the
boost time for a factor-two null dilation. This exact conclusion
persists for products of the preparations and collective readouts.
Correlated regional algebras can have a different modular action.
The curve and enclosures are in \path{source_implication/modular_orbit/}
within the same package.

Rest-frame conservation also leaves a local pressure ambiguity.
A coincidence measurement of the two heads along their separately
measured marginal axes has centered mean \(w\). It is insensitive to
ordinary parity mixing and its volume adjustment. Its unpairing
response lies in \([0.20029,0.20035]\). On a supplied flat comparison
chart let \(F=(x^2-y^2)/2\) near the measuring event and cut it off
smoothly outside. The symmetric addition
\[
 \Delta T_{ab}=\xi w(\partial_a\partial_b-g_{ab}\Box)F
\]
is identically conserved for each fixed preparation. Its integrated
translation charges vanish. Locally its energy and current vanish
while its \(xx,yy\) pressures are \(+\xi w,-\xi w\).
A speed-one-half independent energy measurement in the \(x\)
direction distinguishes the choices by \(\xi w/3\). This is an
ambiguity of the stress attachment allowed by the flat records.
A specified response to strain, a metric coupling, or proper
moving-frame energy data can select an attachment.

\paragraph{A reference test at all routed radii.}
On a separately prepared protected clock and two-word source let
\(P_z=P_0+z\Delta\), \(-1\le z\le1\), describe the two jointly
admitted words. The registered preparations use opposite, independent
or shared tags. Both one-word marginals, ordinary word-energy
distribution and routing clock are fixed. Only the conditional table
for joint admission, which occurs with probability \(q_B\), changes.
For any reference \(r\) in this family,
\[
 S_B(z)-S_B(r)=q_B[(z-r)k(r)-D(P_z\Vert P_r)],\qquad
 k(r)=-\sum_{ij}\Delta_{ij}\log P_{r,ij}.
\]
The product marginals give \(k(0)=0\). Differentiation gives
\(-k'(r)=\sum\Delta_{ij}^2/P_{r,ij}\), lying between
\(229/86580\) and \(44/12285\) from the exact word tables.
The relative-entropy term is therefore bounded above and below by
positive constants times \((z-r)^2\). On these routed balls
\(q_B=\Theta(\ell^4)\) and the clock area has zero variation.
For any sequence of references \(r_\ell\), fixed-volume first-order
stationarity to \(o(\ell^4)\) holds along this variation exactly when
\(r_\ell\to0\). Its normalized linear collar response then tends to
zero. Any fixed nonzero intervention also retains a nonlinear
entropy cost of order \(\ell^4\). This is an all-radius result on this
clock and source. A nonzero gravitational response requires an
independently responsive proper geometry or a different source.

\paragraph{The angular part of regional modular energy.}
There is a positive finite result on a separately prepared cell with
a rotation-averaged reference. Restrict to its constant one-load mode
and first spatial triplet, with projector \(P\). For each of the twelve
poles \(n\), let \(K_n\) be the marginal modular Hamiltonian of the
pole and its five neighbors. Let \(H_s(n),H_r(n)\) be the five spoke
and five rim seam energies, and \(H\) the total internal energy.
On this subspace the finite operators satisfy
\[
 PK_nP=P[cI+\gamma H+uH_s(n)+vH_r(n)]P .
\]
The fivefold stabilizer reduces the symmetric dipole and quadrupole
to their two axial coefficients. The spoke and rim energy matrices
span these coefficients, and the total energy and scalar account for
the rotationally invariant part. Calibration at one pole gives
\(u=0.352820\), \(v=0.697918\), \(\gamma=-0.049106\) to the
precision shown; rotation fixes all twelve readings. Subtracting the
angular mean removes the common total-energy term. Thus all eight
nonconstant angular components of every supported first entropy
variation have an independently readable seam-energy representation.
An exactly time-stationary reference can also be prepared.
For each nonzero energy gap \(d\) of the isolated cell, mix zero
waiting time and a wait of \(\pi/|d|\) with equal probabilities. This
cancels the corresponding coherence. All wait channels commute. The
finite composition preserves the rotation symmetry and gives a
certified stationary rim excess \(v-u\in[0.345076,0.345121]\).
The larger rim weight is essential. An inward-increasing positive
ball weight on these seams gives the wrong sign on an equal-energy
axial-versus-transverse contrast. These clock-free regional readings
supply neither a geometric response nor a stress normalization on the
stationary 80-cell preparation.
The coefficient certificate is in \path{modular_orbit/angular_energy/}
under \path{code/matched_einstein_limit/source_implication/}.

\paragraph{What a sharp propagation distance can detect.}
Let \(F\) attach the same real number \(f_c\) to all probe positions in
cell \(c\). All terms internal to a cell commute with \(F\).
The blank external contacts form a matching, with off-diagonal hopping
\(-1/2\) on each edge. The independent two-endpoint blocks therefore give
\[
 \|i[H,F]\|=\tfrac12\max_{c\sim d}|f_d-f_c|.
\]
Either carrier charge attains the extreme derivative with the two
endpoint amplitudes in relative phase \(i\) or \(-i\).
The dual distance, the supremum of differences in \(f\) at commutator
norm at most one, is twice the shortest cell path. On the stationary
80-cell source a separate free dimer clock and receiving-cell counts
measure 0.50003 and 0.49976 per exchange time. Halving the external
exchange gives 0.25008 and 0.25007. This is a common measured local
speed and a distance with an exact finite characterization. Varying
either parked-head preparation leaves this distance unchanged.

There is a more general limit on any measurement of one copy of these
heads. Their reference density is strictly positive. The ordinary
and unpairing families, including the compensating head rotation,
satisfy \(0.00319968\,\rho_{\rm ref}\preceq\rho'
\preceq50.00251\,\rho_{\rm ref}\), with the lower constant rounded
down. For any source-independent experiment on this one copy the
complete event has a positive effect. Its two probabilities obey the
same inequalities. This persists under a common enlargement of the
apparatus and adaptive observation on that copy. If an estimator
converges in probability to one deterministic value at the reference,
it converges to that value on the perturbed source as well, by applying
the upper bound to each error event. Thus a change of deterministic
limiting geometry cannot be obtained solely by enlarging the apparatus
around this fixed finite source under this definition of readout.
Ensemble means and finite detection rates can change. A source with a
growing number of independently prepared matter degrees of freedom
requires its own analysis. A common random choice of basis for the right-hand two-level
register on two independently prepared copies gives a binary correlation
readout invariant under a common compensating right-hand rotation.
At one eighth of the full unpairing intervention its probability
falls by \(0.00522870\)--\(0.00522873\). Thus repetition can
distinguish the physical source. On any number \(M\) of independent
noninteracting copies, however, the generator is \(\sum_i H_i\)
and the average cell coordinate is \(\sum_i F_i/M\).
The current operators act on separate copies and their common extremal
product state attains the single-copy norm. The velocity distance is
unchanged for every \(M\). A different instrument reads the mean distance of a
mobile probe at dwell four, capped at four cell hops. On repeated
identical preparations the ordinary and collar variations both
reduce the distance traveled. Dividing the collar slope by the
ordinary slope gives \([0.40521,0.40894]\) for a vacancy and
\([0.54357,0.55131]\) for an excess. The intervals are disjoint.
A common scalar change of geometry observed with one fixed gain
per probe cannot give these responses. The probability of reaching
a cell and the length assigned to the path are distinct observables.

\paragraph{Two regional conservation equations.}
A chart on twelve cells, including both marked heads of the 80-cell
source, identifies a translation by two cell steps on its domain.
The antisymmetric part of this partial shift defines a directed
quasimomentum. Commuting each cell observable with the native
Hamiltonian gives an exact rate equation. Internal incidences cancel
between owners. The net quasimomentum rate is the sum of the domain
and image boundary currents and the force of the marked contacts.
Native energy on the same cut has its separate energy-current equation.
This supplies two independently testable continuity relations.
The shift charge has matrix elements between cells two steps apart,
whereas every native bridge energy current is supported within a cell
or across one step. Their operator inner product vanishes.
Two opposite phase preparations distinguish the directed shift
while all these energy-current expectations agree. For the registered
orientation, in each charge and head-parity sector the integer matrices
\(h=2H+cI,\ f=4F\) give
\(\operatorname{Tr}(h^j f)=0\) for \(0\le j<7\) and
\(\operatorname{Tr}(h^7 f)=64\). Hence
\(\operatorname{Tr}(H^7F)=1/8\), whereas cyclicity gives
\(\operatorname{Tr}(H^7 i[H,K])=0\) for every operator \(K\).
At fixed boundary flux the marked force therefore cannot be absorbed
by redefining the regional momentum. Connection terms, momentum of
additional supports and a coarse physical stress have independent
meanings. Identifying one
symmetric stress tensor from the two measurements thus needs a limit
on an independently selected physical sector. There is an exact positive transfer on a more restricted
preparation. Compress the probe to orbitals uniform on the twelve
ports in each cell. On 119 unmarked bridges the energy current equals
40 times the load current as a compressed operator, for all cell
amplitudes and head states. The factor is the sum of five local
agreement energies of eight at an unmarked gateway. Under any small
cell-dependent phase of the uniform wave, the routed energy flow also
matches the directed shift's derivative up to its calibrated sign and
energy unit. All 79 relative phase directions are covered.
Both source derivatives at fixed phase vanish for these readings.
Moreover every such orbital has a nonzero initial leakage rate out of
this sector under uninterrupted propagation. This instantaneous
identity supplies no transfer of the nonzero collar signal to the
original focused probes. The translation chart
and current meters are in
\path{source_implication/directional_stress/translation/}.


A separate test asks whether the marked force can be recovered from
the measured stress and the local screen geometry. After four units
of flight, two nonselective phase controls differ only on the
coherence joining ports 0 and 229. Those ports have native graph
distance nine. An order-$j$ time derivative of a port-local effect
contains terms $H^a E H^{j-a}$. Its matrix element across these ports
vanishes for $j<9$ by locality. The two preparations consequently agree
on every registered local stress and on both optical screens through
eight derivatives. The marked-force difference is positive for
both probe charges, about $4.0051\times10^{-7}$ for a vacancy and
$1.8430\times10^{-6}$ for an excess. No function of those common records
can return both forces. A later phase-sensitive interrogation at port 0
reads information about the same coherence. Eight such settings,
using two phase quadratures and head analysis at four local receiver
ports, reconstruct the entire marked force. All six simulated population
values (reference and two signed responses, for two probe charges)
fall in the independently certified regional intervals. The largest
inverse weights exceed $10^7$, so this reconstruction carries no
verified finite-shot precision. The force measurements
and optical comparison are in \path{source_implication/directional_stress/connection/}.

A remote interference experiment tests whether a scalar delay supplies
a common clock response. Coherent launch quadratures on ports 0 and 5
use the same click histories at receivers 38 and 39. Reweighting
those histories by different frequencies gives different ratios of
the pairing and ordinary delay responses. For a vacancy at port 38
the ratio lies between $0.3002$ and $0.3050$ at zero frequency advance,
and between $0.5212$ and $0.5734$ at quarter-turn advance.
With variable time origin, clock rate and a frequency-convention term,
a common scalar response requires a five-column determinant to vanish.
On five settings its certified interval is wholly positive for the
vacancy and wholly negative for the excess. The registered material
fringes respond to the collar, with frequency-dependent delays. The
calculation is in \path{source_implication/common_front/fringes/}.

The independently reconstructed marked force also gives a held
calibration test on the original four-tick source. Calibrate its signed
conversion to sharp optical focusing on the ordinary change alone,
then predict the response to changing head pairing at fixed surveyed
volume. Both charges and all four readouts (forward, reverse, and
their odd and even combinations) give ratios of measured response to
prediction strictly different from one. The closest interval is
$[0.99888,0.99910]$ for forward excess. These are certified differences
between finite instruments. The marked force measures the rate of a
directed translation; its identification with local null stress would
require another physical result.

The same source gives a common coherent pilot signal. Eliminating
the spatial ports outside the four marked endpoints by a Schur
complement leaves an exact source-dependent coherent self-energy
supported on those endpoints in the registered one-probe sector.
Scattering from these endpoints propagates to exterior receivers.
For a pilot interrogating both marked heads, the modulus of the
leading connected delayed hopping has a pairing-to-ordinary response
ratio in $[0.29682,0.29744]$. For both probe charges a reciprocal
dark-port probability has a leading variance with ratio
$[0.53140,0.53583]$. The corresponding signed head-entropy ratio
is in $[0.56432,0.56454]$. These separate observables distinguish
a common source-sensitive scattering response from an identification
of that response with entropy at one coupling. An exterior check at the
same blank port and unit linewidth gives a positive ordinary dispersive
return for each carrier. The pairing-to-ordinary ratio for the real
return lies in $[0.03709,0.04015]$ for vacancy and
$[-0.000197,-0.000178]$ for excess. This is a certified calculation
of the finite-frequency response. The construction is
in \path{source_implication/common_front/influence/}.

\paragraph{Null curvature from bisected clock intervals.}
\label{par:bisected-null-curvature}
There is a way to separate focusing from a variation of record density
without using beam area. Center three timelike geodesic clocks at the
same event. For each clock \(u\), count markers in its causal diamond
of proper duration \(h\) and in its two successive half-diamonds.
Write \(P_u\) for the full count divided by \(\pi h^4/24\) and
\(S_u\) for the mean of the half counts, each divided by
\(\pi(h/2)^4/24\). Assume a smooth four-geometry and a positive
twice differentiable scalar marker intensity \(m\) per unit proper
four-volume. For the mean counts put \(H=\nabla^2m\) and
\(b=-mR/180+\Box m/60\), evaluated at the central event. The
small-diamond volume expansion~\cite{pwgibbons2007geometry} gives
\begin{align*}
 q_u &= mR_{uu}/30+b+H_{uu}/40,\\
 P_u &= m+h^2 q_u+o(h^2),\\
 S_u &= m+h^2(q_u/4+H_{uu}/32)+o(h^2).
\end{align*}
The second moments of a flat diamond are \(h^2/60\) along time and
\(h^2/30\) along each spatial coordinate. The extra term in \(S_u\)
comes from the two half-diamond centers at proper offsets
\(\pm h/4\). Forming \(P_u-2S_u/3\) cancels \(H_{uu}\). For a unit
spatial vector \(n\), take
\(u=(1,0),(5/3,4n/3),(5/3,-4n/3)\) with weights
\(w=(-9/16,81/160,9/160)\). They sum to zero and reproduce the
contraction of any symmetric tensor with \(k=(1,n)\) twice. Thus
\[
 R_{kk}=\lim_{h\to0}\frac{36}{m h^2}
            \sum_u w_u(P_u-2S_u/3).
\]
The leading rest count supplies \(m\). An error \(\epsilon\) per
normalized count contributes at most \(67.5\epsilon/(mh^2)\) for
exact central normalization. The curvature measurement requires
absolute four-volume errors \(o(h^6)\) on this clock family. The
algebra is checked in \texttt{BisectedDiamonds}. If the same three
frames independently measure regional energy densities \(\rho_u\),
the directly observable residual is
\[
 \sum_u w_u\left[\frac{36(P_u-2S_u/3)}{mh^2}-\kappa\rho_u\right].
\]
When \(\rho_u\) estimates \(T_{uu}\) within \(\delta\) and
\(\epsilon\) includes the geometric expansion remainders, its error
against \(R_{kk}-\kappa T_{kk}\) is at most
\(67.5\epsilon/(mh^2)+(9/8)|\kappa|\delta\).
The two terms can be bounded independently. An ordinary-matter
calibration freezes \(\kappa\) before testing the collar.

For a controlled example take \(U=t+x\), metric
\((1-U^2/4)\eta_{ab}\) and intensity \(1+U^2/2\) near the origin.
The geometric value along \(+x\) is \(R_{kk}=2\).
At \(h=1/32\) the full-count estimate in expectation is 5.00241.
Bisection gives 2.00160 in expectation and 2.00130 with standard
error 0.00150 in a synthetic Poisson census. In flat geometry with
the same intensity the two expectations are exactly 3 and 0.
Replacing the conformal factor by \(1+U^2/4\) reverses the physical
curvature to \(-2\). The full-count mean is positive at 1.00065,
while the bisected mean is \(-1.99883\).
Uneven recording can change even the inferred sign of null curvature.

On the stationary 80-cell source a sharp launch has nonzero probability
of reaching any of the 960 ports at arbitrarily short exposure.
In each charge and parity sector the off-site hopping blocks are
nonnegative in a common basis. Apart from identity blocks there are
only two undirected marked edges, acting on distinct head factors.
Every shortest-path product is nonzero and all have a common phase.
The leading amplitude between any two ports is therefore nonzero on
the faithful preparation. A uniform port-time census using mere
positive-probability reachability scales linearly with duration.
For a second census take the certified reciprocal lengths of
three-hop links, allow waiting and put both tips at port zero.
Each port \(x\) contributes \(\max(h-2d(0,x),0)\). The full-to-half
ratios at \(h=2,4,8,16\) are respectively within 0.001 of
6.224, 10.458, 9.500 and 6.662. A regular four-volume with positive
central density approaches a ratio of sixteen. These finite
measurements cannot supply a native null Ricci value. A joint limit
with many cells per shrinking interval, the clock transformations
above and an independently normalized regional stress is a distinct
physical statement. The counted controls and relay bounds are in
\path{source_implication/bisected_diamonds/}.

\paragraph{Obtaining the ball weight from clocks.}
Two opposite straight rays from the same interior point reach a flat
ball's rim after proper distances $d_+,d_-$. The roots of the
line--sphere intersection give
$d_+d_-/(2\ell)=(\ell^2-r^2)/(2\ell)$ for every direction. On a
convex normal ball of sectional curvatures bounded by $C$, the Hessian
of half the squared central distance along a unit geodesic is
$1+O(C\ell^2)$. Interpolating between its two rim values controls
the product with the same relative error. With first-exit clock
resolution $h$, the integrated kernel error for bounded density is
$O(C\ell^6+h\ell^3)$. Thus $h=o(\ell)$ resolves the leading source
coefficient. On independently supplied curved charts, transverse exit
times also resolve signed sectional curvature when $h=o(\ell^3)$.
This checks how proper clocks could supply the weight without
installing a radial table.

A calibrated three-outcome material return reads the resulting
weighted occupation on a supplied cubic guide. Sharp positions and
six disjoint hopping measurements also recover its symmetrized local
energy. On a guide prepared in its spatially constant one-load mode,
an unobserved lottery varies the occupancy and hence the regional
energy. A second, independently routed flat ruler has identical area
and volume in both preparations. The measured entropy derivative
divided by $\ell^4$ tends to $48(8\pi^2/15)$ in native energy units.
Its area derivative is zero at every resolution. Constructing the
source and the ruler leaves their physical backreaction to be
established. The clock construction and its signed curvature controls
are in \path{code/matched_einstein_limit/fixed_volume/chord_kernel/}.

\paragraph{A moving volume and an equal-volume area comparison.}
For an orthogonal, nonrotating, freely falling diagonal cohort with
axis lengths $a_i$, put $V=a_1a_2a_3$ and $H_i=\dot a_i/a_i$.
Timelike geodesic deviation gives
\[
 R_{uu}=-\sum_i \frac{\ddot a_i}{a_i}
       =-\frac{\ddot V}{V}+2\sum_{i<j}H_iH_j .
\]
The product rule and the directional stress-continuity identity on this
source have formal checks in \texttt{QuantumScalarStress}. The largest
held difference between the radar volume result and
$\kappa(\rho+\sum_i p_i)/2-\Lambda$ was $4.99\times10^{-6}$.
Three approaching inertial mirrors in Minkowski space give a falling
volume and zero timelike Ricci. Initial convergence of an observer
cohort alone therefore cannot assign curvature.

The fixed-volume entropy argument uses a spatial ball in a specified
rest frame. At proper time $0.60$ construct its local slice by the
spacetime exponential map on the central observer's spacelike tangent
vectors. Its second fundamental form vanishes at the centre. Three
proper-length variations on a radial and angular grid measure interior
volume. A separate angular survey measures the rim. The ideal rulers
use the supplied geometry. Adjust the rim radius until the measured
volume agrees with $4\pi\ell^3/3$ on every preparation, and infer
\[
 \widehat G_{uu}=\frac{15}{\ell^2}
                   \left(1-\frac{A|_V}{4\pi\ell^2}\right).
\]
At $\ell=0.1$ the ground and a $y$-oriented source yield rim areas
$0.1256522654$ and $0.1256513732$ at the same volume $0.0041887902$.
The area-derived increase in $G_{uu}$ is $0.010651$. Independently
measured matter stress predicts $0.010646$ and the radar survey gives
$0.010644$. Across four sources the largest area error against stress
falls from $0.000333$ to $0.0000840$ to $0.0000221$ on radii
$0.4,0.2,0.1$. Geodesic balls on constant coordinate-time slices of
the same metric give the flat intrinsic result. The observer's choice
of rest slice is part of the small-ball premise.

\paragraph{Native routes, charged probes and prescribed optics.}
On four blank Interval Echo boards, a homogeneous positive-curvature
three-space assigned to the measured volume-doubling ratios conflicts
with independently clocked landmark ranges. In the finest survey the
doubling counts $1158,4630$ would imply an equal-volume area deficit of
$21.65\%$. The ranges on five stations require more than $6.4\%$
relative distortion to realize that very curvature. The apparent
deficit is a consequence of the rejected geometric interpretation.
