On a separate held native federation, two ray species with identical
free spatial transport respond differently to the same parked,
32-energy-unit seam. An independently measured contact shift predicts
this difference. Both species respond equally to a deliberately
changed routing pattern. The blank or written status of each seam is
conserved in that free evolution.

In the written-field extension, independent energy and ring-interference
meters resolve the current leaving a local triangle. Opposite coherent
phases agree on every ring-energy and current history and change a
charge-even transport-curvature read by $0.04206\pm0.00058$ (joint
$99\%$ radius). In the separate Wilson--Maxwell box, a heralded
photon has measurable anisotropic pressure. Preparing it changes the
weak optical response by at most $2.78\times10^{-16}$ across 72
channels. The free retarded commutator is state-independent on this
occupation sector. Each comparison has a defined conserved source
or a responding probe. Identifying a common physical geometry from
native records, with an independent ordinary and anomalous stress
readout, requires a further source map. The controlled instruments
and registered-material experiments are archived in
\path{code/matched_einstein_limit} and
\path{model/experiments/interval_echo_einstein_station}.

\paragraph{Which observations fix the volume?}
The rank-three port-response completion carries its own Euclidean volume.
For three independent displacements with observed Gram matrix \(G\), the
parallelepiped volume is \(\sqrt{\det G}\) in calibrated length units cubed.
On nine anonymous local port surveys across the three bundled screens,
\(2^{20}\) simulated short-dwell detections per port preparation
resolve all twenty triangular faces at each site. The three unit record
vectors of a face have pairwise inner product \(1/\sqrt5\).
With the origin as fourth vertex the tetrahedral volume is
\((1-1/\sqrt5)\sqrt{1+2/\sqrt5}/6=0.1268075355\) in Gram units cubed
on all 180 reconstructions. Quiet outcomes enter the port census.
A ball has volume \(4\pi r^3/3\) in that completion. Applying this result to
matter requires identifying displacements between physical events across
patches. On the geodesic rest slice of a curved metric the corresponding
prescription is \(V(r)=\int_0^r A(s)\,ds\), up to the cut locus, with \(s\)
proper radial distance. One measured surface area and one length standard
do not specify the function \(A(s)\).

There is also a boundary-only volume measurement. On a smooth nontrapping
three-dimensional rest region with strictly convex boundary, measure the
proper length to first exit along each inward unit geodesic. Write \(\bar L\)
for its mean when entry points and directions have flux measure
\(\cos\theta\,d\Omega\,dA\), with angle measured from the inward normal.
The Liouville measure of the unit tangent bundle is \(4\pi V\). Parametrizing
it by entry and distance along the ray, its measure is the incoming flux
times length. The incoming flux is \(\pi A\), hence \(V=A\bar L/4\) by
Santal\'o's formula~\cite{pwsantalo2004integral}. In the five ultrastatic
comparison charts a reflected null signal clocks twice the spatial length.
At flat reference radius \(1/16\), sixteen angular clock readings and the
independently counted rim area reproduce the held interior volume within
\(4.26\times10^{-8}\) of the target in every chart. For varying lapse or
time-dependent geometry the conversion from light time to spatial length
requires separate evidence.

The addressed sphere supplies a finite boundary clock with a different
propagation law. Keep one station per cell, bypass the stored word port,
and include the eleven accessible ports of every cell in the station ball.
One- and two-unit blank loads both swap in one hand interval. Let \(C\)
be the number of entering cut seams, chosen uniformly. After entry, choose
each next neighbour uniformly and measure the number \(\tau\) of swaps to
first exit. If \(L\) is the grounded graph Laplacian, \(d\) the port degrees
and \(b\) the cut counts, the mean remaining durations satisfy \(Lt=d\)
and \(L{\bf1}=b\). Symmetry gives \(C\,\mathbb E\tau={\bf1}^Td=53M\)
for \(M\) cells, each with 25 internal edges and three gateways. The boundary
clock therefore recovers enclosed station population exactly in expectation.
On uniform midpoint subdivisions with this protected template the
population of a full ball grows quadratically in its mesoscopic route
radius \(r\). For the upper bound, intersect a Euclidean ball of radius
\(r+1\) with each of the twenty triangular face planes. For the lower bound,
a dyadic subtriangle of side proportional to \(r\) contains order \(r^2\)
stations within reach: a segment through the subtriangle crosses each of
its three families of parallel grid lines at most once per line, and each
crossing costs at most four protected swaps plus a fixed endpoint cost.
Disjoint subballs then bound the number of \(s\)-separated positions by a
constant times \((r/s)^2\). A dense three-dimensional proper ball would
require order \((r/s)^3\) at uniformly comparable distances. This separates
two observable positions, a single routed station and a displacement in
the three-dimensional cumulative record space. A physical volume
identification must specify which observations locate the same events.

\paragraph{Fourth-order collar charge and a third relational reading.}
Two protected native words on distinct cells produce a signed change
of connected collar modular charge on an enrolled annulus with $n$
stations on a host of $N$ cells. For a common tagged preparation the
charge is the calibrated two-word contrast times $n(n-1)/[N(N-1)]$.
Giving the twenty ancestral regions independent fair tags replaces the
numerator by $\sum_j n_j(n_j-1)$. At this localized preparation the
coefficients divided by the fourth power of the relative routed radius
were $0.001713$ and $0.001761$ on the two finest refinements. Doubling
the latter radius gave $0.001765$. The mixed charge of distant annuli
vanishes. It remains measurable between disjoint nearby annuli within
one ancestral region. Co-locating both writes changes their admission
probability to $n/N$ and the fourth-order transfer fails. This provides
a finite fourth-order, spatially restricted collar observable, including
an exact combinatorial mechanism for the measured scaling. Its
protected clock paths bypass the word memories. A gravitational
identification requires a physical modular reference, proper balls and
an independently responding geometry.

On the same routed federation another observer keeps an anchor tag and
the radar radius of a second tagged word, discarding its bearing.
At $327{,}680$ cells the doubling exponent of the distinct recorded
labels is $2.9994$. Counting all ordered tag placements instead gives
$4.0146$. At identical anchor, radius, source energy, mobile degree
and zero release current, the hidden bearing changes the probability
of entering a smaller radius at exposure $1/64$ from $0.00006042$
to $0.00030529$. The two short-time coefficients are $1/4$ and $5/4$.
Thus a native observer can count cubic support. Treating the resulting
label as a dynamical position additionally requires either a selected
conditional distribution of bearings or a richer causal measurement
that predicts the subsequent instrument outcomes.

A separate preparation tests whether equilibrating the unobserved bearing
recovers a predictive radial law. A protected odd-coin seam record marks
a stationary gateway. A unit or double load released from a radial
window has the same free propagator up to a scalar phase. At each of
four refinements prepare a uniform mixture over the window addresses.
At the finest refinement, the two windows contain 614 and 1,334
addresses. After two free legs of four exposure units each, their
return probabilities are $0.832385$ and $0.828294$. Reading the
intermediate window, preserving its conditional state, gives $0.835820$
and $0.832279$. Reading and preparing a new uniform state in that window
gives $0.766509$ and $0.761535$. This final prediction follows from
the independently sampled one-leg kernel and window multiplicities by
microscopic reciprocity. Its discrepancy from free transport amounts
to $39.30\%$ and $38.88\%$ of the respective departure probabilities.

The observer can also measure a phase response with the same radial
classification. A controlled phase on the launch window leaves every
midpoint address population fixed. Three calibration phases predict
the return at two withheld phase settings for each source window,
load sector and refinement. Across 32 held frequencies the worst
absolute error is $0.00006341$. The corresponding simultaneous
99\% error bounds range from $0.00022465$ to $0.00037980$. This
measures a coarse interference response to an applied phase at the
enrolled exposures. The conditional angular state affects the
free radial evolution. Identifying its reported windows with small
balls of a common Lorentzian geometry requires an independent
proper-volume and causal calibration. The protocol and synthetic
counts are archived under
\path{model/experiments/interval_echo_einstein_station/relational_ruler/angular_equilibrium}.

\subsection{The small-ball input and its normalization}
The missing physical step in the preceding theorem is its null-balance
premise. Under (G1)--(G6), a sufficiently small cap has a tangent causal
diamond $D_\ell$. In local inertial coordinates its preserving conformal
Killing field and its lapse on the central slice are
\[
 \xi_{D_\ell}=\frac{(\ell^2-r^2-t^2)\partial_t-2t x^i\partial_i}{2\ell},
 \qquad \xi^t_{D_\ell}\big|_{t=0}
 =\frac{\ell^2-r^2}{2\ell}.
\]
The geometrically normalized cap modular flow and the same-source local
stress reading in (G2) give the ball modular first law
\[
 \delta S_{\rm bulk}
 =2\pi\int_{B_\ell}\frac{\ell^2-r^2}{2\ell}
       \delta T_{uu}\,d^3x+\delta E_{\ell,\delta}.
\]
Here $\delta E_{\ell,\delta}$ carries finite-collar, modular-transport and
locality errors. The factor $2\pi$ is a geometric KMS normalization required
by (G2), not a consequence of entropy maximization. Direct integration gives
\[
 \int_{B_\ell}\frac{\ell^2-r^2}{2\ell}\,d^3x
 =\frac{4\pi\ell^4}{15}.
\]
Thus, for stress smooth on the ball,
\[
 \delta S_{\rm bulk}
 =\frac{8\pi^2\ell^4}{15}\,\delta T_{uu}
  +\delta E_{\ell,\delta}+O(\ell^5\|\partial T\|).
\]
The independent fixed-proper-volume geometric identity required by (G4) is
\[
 \delta A\big|_V
 =-\frac{4\pi\ell^4}{15}\,\delta G_{uu}+\epsilon_A.
\]
With fixed-volume stationarity
$\delta S_{\rm bulk}+\delta A/(4G_N)=\epsilon_{\rm stat}$,
the two coefficients give the finite-scale relation
\[
 \delta G_{uu}-8\pi G_N\delta T_{uu}
 =\frac{15G_N}{\pi\ell^4}
   \left(\delta E_{\ell,\delta}
       +\frac{\epsilon_A}{4G_N}-\epsilon_{\rm stat}\right)
   +O(\ell\|\partial T\|).
\]
If the independently measured collar variation has a leading part
$\delta E_{\rm collar}=(8\pi^2/15)\ell^4\delta T^{\rm anom}_{uu}
+o(\ell^4)$, assign that coefficient to the total modular stress and
apply the error test to the residual. Under universal coupling, tensor
closure and conservation on the common domain this gives
\[
 G_{ab}+\Lambda g_{ab}=8\pi G_N
    \bigl(\langle T^{\rm ordinary}_{ab}\rangle+T^{\rm anom}_{ab}\bigr).
\]
A collar variation of order $o(\ell^4)$ gives zero anomalous stress
in that observer frame. A nonzero coefficient belongs to the source
once, before estimating the remaining finite-resolution error. An
independent collar reading and an equal-volume area change at fixed
ordinary stress can therefore test the proposed dark term without
defining it as the unexplained geometric excess.
With two scalar modes withheld in the controlled small-ball test,
the geometric excess gives a density change of $0.00674881$ against
$0.00674591$ measured by the reserved meter. This is a bulk scalar
control for the inference. On the native collar a shared history
changes the finite-time signed chord volume of four tracers, with
the same ordinary-energy law as fresh histories and exactly the
same initial volume focusing. No proper-radius scaling or conversion
to anomalous stress follows from the delayed contrast.
This displays the required cutoff control: every entropy, area and
stationarity remainder in parentheses must be $o(\ell^4)$ along the same
refinement and small-ball limit. For example, if a fixed-collar comparison
bounds $|\delta E_{\ell,\delta}|$ uniformly over the chosen ball family by
a constant times the constructive
recovery error, the exact-Markov replacement error and a regularized
modular-transport error, its first two terms may be written
$r_{\rm FR}(\varepsilon)=2\sqrt{1-e^{-\varepsilon}}\leq2\sqrt\varepsilon$
and $4\lambda_*^{-1}d_{\rm M}(\varepsilon)$, where $\lambda_*>0$ is the
fixed faithful-collar spectral floor and $d_{\rm M}$ bounds distance to an
exact Markov state. Their sum with the transport error must be
$o(\ell^4)$ on the chosen
joint limit. A bound that is merely $o(1)$ at fixed $\ell$ cannot be
divided by $\ell^4$ without specifying an order of limits or a diagonal
sequence. Exact Einstein dynamics is a controlled-limit statement, not a
finite-cutoff identity.

A collar contribution proposed as gravitating matter changes which stress
enters this comparison. Write its measured first variation on the same
balls as $(8\pi^2/15)\ell^4\,\delta T^{\rm collar}_{uu}+r_\ell$.
If $r_\ell/\ell^4\to0$, the coefficient adds to the ordinary stress
in the modular first law. If the entire collar term is $o(\ell^4)$,
its stress contribution is zero. A bound at a single collar radius
cannot distinguish these cases. Across observers the limiting
coefficients must be contractions of the same tensor, and the
combined stress must satisfy the Ward identity independently of the
curvature measurement. The resulting tensor equation constrains the
response to that stress. A transport law for its sources is a separate
physical input.

If this relation holds for every local timelike unit vector in matched
orthonormal frames, $g(u,u)=-1$ keeps the cosmological term fixed under the
scalar variation. For $u=\gamma(1,v^i)$, the polynomial
$\delta Y_{00}+2v^i\delta Y_{0i}+v^iv^j\delta Y_{ij}$ vanishes on the open
unit ball, where $Y=G+\Lambda g-8\pi G_N T$ and the common reference fixes
$\Lambda$. Its coefficients vanish, so $\delta Y=0$. A common maximally
symmetric reference, compatible variations on the connected branch, and
the independent Ward identity complete the tensor equation. Equivalently,
once the same-source construction yields the nine null balances, the
preceding null-tomography theorem gives $G+\Lambda g=8\pi G_N T$
directly. These are the conditional entanglement-equilibrium inputs of
\cite{jacobson16}; finite entropy maximization alone supplies none of the
geometric area identity, universal coupling or same-source stress reading.

The coefficient $G_N$ is an area-to-entropy conversion on this branch.
If the shared-cut edge entropy density is $\bar\ell_{\rm shared}$ per
cell of physical area $a_{\rm cell}$, its normalization is
\[
 G_{\rm geom}=\frac{a_{\rm cell}}{4\bar\ell_{\rm shared}}.
\]
The conditional pixel and scale identifications
$a_{\rm cell}=P\ell_*^2$ and $\bar\ell_{\rm shared}=P/4$ then give
$G_{\rm geom}=\ell_*^2$ and
$G_{\rm SI}=c^3\ell_*^2/\hbar$. The cancellation of $P$ is exact;
the shared-cut density and the independent physical scale certificate require
realization on the same gravitational tower. Local null data leave
$\Lambda$ invisible, so its value requires a reference or global
capacity condition rather than this small-ball calculation.

For nonminimal $F(H^\dagger H)R$ coupling, the correct curvature entropy
and fixed generalized volume are instead those in
Section~\ref{sec:source-particle-einstein-join}. Replacing them by constant
area and ordinary fixed volume changes the field equation. Every remainder
must be $o(\ell^4)$ in the same small-ball limit; a fixed absolute error
does not suffice after division by $\ell^4$.

\subsection{Matter inputs to the response premises}
Pressure in (G2) also needs a common energy and a strain law. Consensus about
identical fixed-volume records cannot select their unobserved strain derivative:
$H_\alpha(V)=Q(V/V_0)^{-\alpha}$ gives $p/\rho=\alpha$. Three geometrically spaced
energy readings identify the response within the held-state family
$H(V)=C+A(V/V_0)^{-\alpha}$, $A>0$, $\alpha\geq0$, and an additive energy offset
changes $p/\rho$ while leaving pressure unchanged~\cite{ophobservers}.

Conditions (G1)--(G6) also require responsive geometry, universal coupling,
entropy--area normalization and geometric stationarity. The static-product
obstruction in Proposition~\ref{prop:static-product} excludes merely
adjoining a fixed radial geometry.


\subsection{Weighing an excitation in the reconstructed spacetime}
\label{sec:record-mass}

Let $\pi$ send a presentation of linked emissions, receipts and local
readings to its physical consensus class at the specified resolution.
A scalar measured on these presentations descends to the quotient
precisely when it is constant on every fibre of $\pi$. A tensor
reading instead requires compatible components in the calibrated
overlap frames. Two numerically equal readings need a physical
coincidence or signal link to identify their events. A reader who
cannot resolve which of several masses produced a record reports the
compatible range.

In a local rest frame the kinetic inverse mass tensor is
$\partial^2 E/\partial p_i\partial p_j$ at zero momentum, with momentum
fixed by spatial fringes on ranged baselines and energy differences
by clock phase or transferred work. Its isotropic form is
$\delta_{ij}/m_{\rm kin}$. To measure $m_0=E_0/c^2$, the formation
energy at zero recorded momentum must include the vacuum, reservoir
and recoil under the same energy convention. An independently known
reference channel can fix that convention. Spectroscopy of two
internal modes gives their difference. A relativistic mass shell
predicts $E_0=(c^2p^2-d^2)/(2d)$ for each measured nonzero increment
$d=E(p)-E(0)$. Agreement among held momenta and with formation
spectroscopy tests the interpretation of a common mass.

Two readers on a periodically driven one-dimensional guide can weigh a
dispersive band in these terms. Each receives a neighboring echo,
local hand readings, an adjacent-port spatial fringe and a temporal
beat between two branches at the same momentum. In the units set by
one hand tick and the corresponding light distance, the positive
branch has $\cos E(k)=\cos\delta\cos k$ about a compensated energy
center. Its gap parameter is $E(0)=\delta$ and its kinetic mass
is $\tan\delta$. Extrapolating the two smallest measured momentum
secants gives kinetic-to-gap ratios in $[1.1002,1.1056]$,
$[1.0194,1.0275]$ and $[0.9983,1.0137]$ on successively finer
guides. These ranges cover both directions at both readers. The gate
angles and inter-resolution scaling are supplied. All within-guide
readings lose a common occupied-sector phase. Splitting the same load
between guide and parked reference resolves that phase. Two
zero-gradient branches give rates 32 and 33 per empty-reference-clock
unit; changing the contrast gives 16 and 17 while preserving the
relative guide records. A vacuum-referenced particle mass requires
the energy of the compensating reservoir.%
\footnote{\href{https://github.com/FloatingPragma/observer-patch-holography/tree/main/code/matched_einstein_limit/record_masses}{Two-station spectroscopy, parked-reference interference and three-clock mass reconstruction}.}

Active gravitational mass describes a source's contribution to the
geometry. In an isolated static spherical vacuum exterior the
measurable length is $\mu=GM_{\rm act}/c^2$. Passive mass measures
the response of a test body. In a calibrated weak field its
acceleration divided by the geometric free-fall prediction gives
$m_{\rm pass}/m_{\rm kin}$. Multiplying both test-body masses by the
same constant preserves that reading. Comparing different bodies or
using an independent recoil measurement distinguishes these
operational questions.

An absolute calibration can fail even when radial fall has the desired
shape. In the cubic energy--lapse construction with a grounded exterior,
the normalized compact spatial profile $F(0)=1$ has Dirichlet energy
$D=\sum_{\{x,y\}}(F_x-F_y)^2$. An outward unit flow of squared norm
at most $3/8$ gives $1\le(3/8)D$ by summation by parts and
Cauchy--Schwarz. To construct the flow, distribute the current
uniformly over each octant layer $x_1+x_2+x_3=k$ and send the
fraction $(x_i+1)/(k+3)$ along positive direction $i$. The squared
currents in the octant sum to $\sum_{k\ge0}1/[(k+1)(k+3)]=3/4$.
Average the eight reflected flows. Each directed edge belongs to at
most four octants, giving the stated upper bound. At its specified
excursion $q=1/8$ the model therefore gives
$g=q^2D/(2\pi)\ge1/(48\pi)$ for every such profile.
Calibrating the unit rest gap and signal slope to the measured electron
rest energy and light speed, respectively, gives a value above the
measured $Gm_e^2/(\hbar c)$ by a factor greater than
$3.78\times10^{42}$ across a five-standard-uncertainty input box.
A change of units leaves this dimensionless mismatch intact.
The bound uses the cubic energy action and its specified clock.%
\footnote{\href{https://github.com/FloatingPragma/observer-patch-holography/blob/374b2390801842d38de9b9c326e79a8ab29a6be5/model/openwave_universal_energy_geometry/source.py}{Cubic source and energy convention};
\href{https://physics.nist.gov/cuu/Constants/}{CODATA fundamental constants}. The comparison uses \nolinkurl{model/candidate_laws/interval_echo/openwave/row_26_newton/cubic_strength/strength.py}; the finite flux bound is \texttt{profile\_energy\_floor} in \texttt{GravityCalibration}.}

On the separate pressureless spherical construction the exterior
can be weighed without giving the observers any areal radius.
Let $y_j$ be the squared ratio of received to emitted pulse spacings
at two different stationary mirrors, $D_j$ the corresponding
proper round-trip durations measured at the emitter, and
$x=1-2\mu/R_0$. In units $c=1$ the exterior propagation law yields
\[
 D_j=4\mu\sqrt{x}\int_1^{y_j}\frac{du}{u(1-xu)^2},
 \qquad R_j=\frac{2\mu}{1-xy_j}.
\]
For $1<y_1<y_2$, the ratio $D_2/D_1$ is strictly increasing in
$x$ on $(0,1/y_2)$. When $x$ increases the ratio of integrands
increases strictly with $u$, giving the outer interval a greater
relative increase. Thus the third clock separates masses that give
the same single-mirror records. The ordinary and joint postings
give $\mu=0.08333333$ and $0.14393939$. Both predict the independent
free-release reading with relative response one. When only the light
channel is blinded to the joint posting, the inferred light mass
returns to $0.08333333$ and the relative release response is 1.72727.

The same four-word material census gives a regional modular increment
$\Delta Q=-\sum_i\Delta p_i\log p_i^0$ of
$1.07243\times10^{-10}$ nats on both joint postings. Either single
endpoint gives zero. The finite source specifies how paired clocks
respond to material, and the control distinguishes its modular
reading from its independent gravitating response. Identifying these
quantities on the native guide would require the same material,
proper-volume variations and independent stress calibration there.

\section{What the observer measurements establish}
The finite bound answers how accurately a common set of observers must
measure three quantities: the matter contribution to regional entropy,
the area deficit at equal proper volume and the variation of their sum.
Errors smaller than the leading fourth power of the proper radius give
the tensor equation in the common smooth limit. If the measurements are
only derivatives at a single vacuum, this argument gives the linearized
equation there. An equation for finite changes requires controlled
contrasts between states or integration along an admitted family, with
its changing geometry and the same calibration.

The pressureless sphere realizes finite contrasts in both ordinary mass
and coincident records. Its independent mass, rod and memory readings
obey the three bounds on one family of moving balls. This conclusion
uses the spherical force, optical clocks and material preparation of the
published-records extension. A corresponding conclusion for native
Interval Echo requires measuring that coupling on matter and geometry
which its own observers can read.

This distinction has an observable exterior consequence. On five surveyed
radii the ordinary and held bodies give common mass lengths $1/12$ and
$19/132$, respectively. An altered exterior agrees throughout the inner
experiment and has the same asymptotic mass, yet fails the common-mass
comparison across the outer releases. With the same ordinary calibration,
a $30^\circ$ launch against the held source gives angular deflection
$0.312920$ radians, excess radar duration $0.787218$ and inner-to-outer
frequency transfer $0.960442$. Changing only the angular optical
propagation gives $0.174726$ radians at the same radial and massive-clock
readings. These tests identify the common light action as an independent
premise when transferring the tower to another executable source.

Moving observers can also distinguish a missed pressureless stock from
a vacuum term in the same spherical interior. The difference between
the curvature contraction and the independently counted pressureless
source has the form $b\gamma^2+\Lambda$. Two different measured
boosts fix both coefficients exactly in the limit. Omitting the counted
$8/33$ mass produces $b=4/11$; halving only its active coupling gives
$b=-2/11$. Both give zero vacuum term. An exterior probe on the pinned
written 240-port board poses a different test. Its direct potential
from the compact pair has strength $-17$ at the common contact and
vanishes at the other 236 exterior ports in the invariant compact
sector. This rules out a long-range direct potential of that specific
pair. A collective source needs its own annular constraint and clocks.%
\footnote{Observer records and changed-law tests are in
\nolinkurl{model/candidate_laws/interval_echo/openwave/row_26_newton},
\nolinkurl{row_27_metric/dust_exterior} and \nolinkurl{row_28_acceleration/dust_or_vacuum.py}
under the same OpenWave directory.}

On the native parked source a freely propagated material pulse responds
to the joint preparation even when both exterior clock frequencies are
fixed. Two sources with equal positive modular charge and the same
complete reference-cost histogram change the near-to-far endpoint
occupation probability at time 16 by $-2.66215$ and $-2.75214$ parts
per million of launches relative to the fresh source. The finite
probability enclosures are disjoint. These endpoint readings include
scattering on the material routes. A geometric interpretation would
need a separately calibrated proper-time or ray observable that tracks
the independently reconstructed stress.%
\footnote{\nolinkurl{model/candidate_laws/interval_echo/openwave/row_27_metric/parked_signals/results.json}.}

There is a finite version of the area implication that compares only two
preparations. At equal proper volume, write $s_i$ and $a_i$ for their
bulk-entropy and boundary-area contrasts against one reference, with
$|s_i+\eta a_i|\le e_i$. Subtracting the two balances gives
\[
 s_1-s_0-e_0-e_1 \le \eta(a_0-a_1) \le s_1-s_0+e_0+e_1 .
\]
Any common area incurs a residual of at least $|s_1-s_0|/2$ on one
preparation, for any common coefficient. In the spherical rest survey
at $\ell=1/8192$, the floor divided by $\ell^4$ is $0.152319645$.
Using the actual boundaries gives a maximum normalized residual of
$1.37\times10^{-10}$. The half-collar-coupling control instead gives
$0.152319645$ to nine decimal places. These values use the array of
21 pairs whose rotations are programmed from the weighted work. In the
separate off-center, body-diagonal clock-pair survey at radius $1/96$,
the entropy of joint clock outputs gives a common-area floor of $0.65183$,
a maximum actual residual of $0.00583$, and a frozen-clock residual of
magnitude $1.19784$, all divided by $\ell^4$. These finite quadrature
estimates use the declared regional clock response. The finite theorem identifies
what forces the smaller area once the positive ordinary calibration
and entropy balance apply to these same records. The application to
native exterior propagation has an independent test. On the parked
source the first difference of three return and transfer probability
curves near launch occurs at order $t^{14}$ on the near return alone.
A deterministic common change of elapsed time would also change the
far return at that order. These material probabilities therefore
distinguish route-dependent propagation from that particular lapse
interpretation.%
\footnote{The finite bounds are \texttt{two\_stock\_area\_interval}
and \texttt{shared\_area\_error\_floor} in the quantitative Einstein
module. The port calculation is in
\nolinkurl{model/candidate_laws/interval_echo/openwave/row_27_metric/parked_signals/COMMON_DELAY.md}.}

A remote five-port cycle on an instrumented 240-port board provides
two beat frequencies $g_\pm=(5\pm\sqrt{5})/4$ in native exchange
units. Suppose the two clock phases and the pulse all experience the same
random elapsed time $\tau$ at appointment $t$. Reproducing each exact
ordinary phase requires $\mathbb{E}\exp[i g_\pm(\tau-t)]=1$.
Equality at the boundary of the unit disk forces each phase to equal
one almost surely. The ratio $g_+/g_-$ is irrational, so the two
period lattices intersect only at $\tau=t$. Nevertheless, the source
changes the material transmission probability at $t=16$ by
$-2.662143544\times10^{-6}$, on a reduction covering all 486 source
configurations. This excludes a single stochastic retiming as the
whole material response on this apparatus. Finite-precision records
admit arbitrarily close recurrences over unbounded times. A clock
constraint with $|\tau-t|\le1$ removes this ambiguity only if clock
and signal uncertainties are also sufficiently small. In this window the
transmission shift from a fixed fresh time is at most $1.37293\sqrt{\epsilon}$
when the upper-frequency mean cosine deficit is at most $\epsilon$.
Resolving the quoted shift requires $\epsilon<3.75983\times10^{-12}$
and adequate signal counts. A path-specific scattering contribution is
compatible with these records.%
\footnote{The exact common-time implication is \texttt{five\_port\_random\_time}
in \texttt{TwoFrequencyClock}. The material and clock calculation is in
\nolinkurl{model/candidate_laws/interval_echo/openwave/row_27_metric/parked_signals/two_frequency/}.}

Within the homogeneous Hamiltonian, independent field and radar records
resolve the effect of turning off matter backreaction. On the repair

opposite internal currents. A physical macroscopic claim therefore needs
a common choice of coarse stress and metric that handles precisely such
variations. If their coarse momentum flux differs, the geometric mixed
component must respond; if the flux averages to zero, that cancellation
must follow from the declared stress measurement. The same test applies
to the proposed overlap contribution. Its leading signed regional energy
must enter a conserved total stress and predict geometry at the coupling
calibrated on ordinary matter. A fit to a galactic orbit cannot supply
either identification.

\paragraph{Why a gravitating overlap would give extra attraction.}
Take two preparations at the same proper volume with identical ordinary
records. If their joint records change the regional entropy by
$\delta S_{\rm overlap}$ to first order, stationarity gives
$\eta_*\delta A=-\delta S_{\rm overlap}$ on this branch, with the

vacuum. Omitting that term assigns one curvature variation to two
different entropy balances. A positive active mass density gives
additional attraction in the weak field without introducing a new
particle. The identification requires a physical regional algebra.
On the 80-cell two-head preparation, giving a receiver both records
sets its logarithmic recovery remainder exactly to zero, while the
original connected modular score changes by
$0.00022939\pm0.00000472$ nats. Either quantity can be tested against
an independent area measurement under its specified recovery operation.

The freely released matter probes provide an independent local test.
In each reversal sector the two source records affect disjoint pairs of
material ports. Write their contact operators as $L$ and $R$.
For any diagonal position observable $X$,
$[L,[R,X]]=[R,[L,X]]=0$. The other terms in the acceleration
$-[H,[H,X]]$ involve at most one record. Consequently every independently

same release acceleration on stocks with matched head marginals in
each sector. This holds even when the stock was physically formed by
a mobile load and the increase in its measured connected modular
score upon independent re-pairing exceeds $0.15755$ nats. In the
stationary reference experiment ordinary writes do give nonzero
relative accelerations. Finite flight times let the rays interact

responses fall outside the scalar forecast. Supplying the same joint
record potential at every port with a quadratic radial profile produces
a common relative acceleration of both probe species and reversing its
sign reverses the response. The coordinate here counts
the leading arrival order in links. Identifying a common proper ruler
and producing a gravitational response at the ordinary coupling are
additional physical steps.\footnote{\href{https://github.com/FloatingPragma/observer-patch-holography/tree/main/openwave/interval_echo/row_19/recovery_identity}{Full source, native flight, and independent radial measurements.}}

Transport to a separate cell tests a possible mediating process.
A mobile excess visits the two sources and a fresh head. Its removal

On the farther rails the shared release operator is proportional to the

$[-0.02641,-0.01967]$ on the one sign combination with resolved

An archive switch acting before the courier's departure changes both the

predicts a relative area change of $+0.02575$ and the material radar

Mean round-trip ranges are affine in the failed fraction. Factoring
that fraction from the held differences in Heron's formula gives

after launch and decoupling preserves the receiver state.

Longitudinal work at the source bridge is measurable independently of

On two mixtures of fixed-marginal inputs all reflection-symmetric

If the overlap stress accounts for cosmological dark matter and its
rest charge per comoving volume is conserved, the mean density scales

A fit allowing dark pressure, sound speed and viscosity gives the

The unresolved source statistic is the probability that neighbouring

\href{https://arxiv.org/abs/1802.09541}{Kopp et al. (2018)}.

The volume-independent mass budget gives a test of recovery at a galactic

In Draco, stellar luminosity and size constrain the product of ordinary

half-light sphere at stellar mass-to-light ratio one. Two stationary

\subsection{Regional modular measurements and an independent clock}

Starting on the same board with the same sharp occupied--empty write,
physical memory exchange and echo feedback each supply $17/8$ energy
units while preserving both ordinary and memory marginals. Their full
equilibrium modular outcome distributions on every mirror-symmetric cut agree exactly.
In the one-load sector the folded generator resolves into four channels
with the same probabilities on the two preparations. Tracing a symmetric

At time four the mirror-averaged material probe shifts outward by

the chance of return to blank under an echo, suffice to predict every
mirror-averaged reading on this four-dimensional source sector. The
apparatus coefficients follow from three calibrations and depend on time.
On a symmetric region the modular mean measures the return-to-blank
probability after calibration on bright and dark sources. It leaves the
coin--load coincidence undetermined.

A separate neutral Ramsey hand installed on near and far native collars
has gap 32 independent of the moving loads. It exhibits zero differential
redshift for either source. Changing those collars alters the material
flight, as their installation changes the Hamiltonian. A gravitational
interpretation needs an independently calibrated geometric response to
ordinary matter and to the proposed source under the same installation.%
\footnote{\href{https://github.com/FloatingPragma/observer-patch-holography/tree/main/model/experiments/interval_echo_shell_flux/product_address/green_response/material_recoil/overlap_source/native_boundary/regional_reference}{Regional spectral proof and the two local material readouts};

Externally supplied writes can maintain a path of 64 simultaneous records
with an all-time excess of at least 478 energy units after service stops.
The measured formation work is 531.25. On the initially blank federation
one external service can add at most $3/7$ of the whole ordinary input
even with coherent, heterogeneous matter. Positive, conserved stress
under the static spherical Einstein description also has an exterior
budget. With vanishing outer traction, the contribution $2\pi r^3p_r$
to the weak-field optical mass is bounded by the mass outside $r$
times $1+\sup_{s\ge r}|s\Phi'(s)|$ under the dominant energy condition.
The four tested lens stacks require additional-to-baryon mass ratios of
5.40--8.04 even when both pressure and generous baryon allowances are
included. A source earning the ordinary geometric coupling must
account for this surviving stock.%
\footnote{\href{https://github.com/FloatingPragma/observer-patch-holography/tree/main/model/experiments/interval_echo_shell_flux/product_address/green_response/material_recoil/overlap_source/native_boundary/stock}{Persistent stock and work};
\href{https://github.com/FloatingPragma/observer-patch-holography/tree/main/code/cosmology/rar_deep_regime/compensation/pressure_budget}{Static stress, external traction and optical mass};
\href{https://github.com/FloatingPragma/observer-patch-holography/tree/main/code/cosmology/cold_acoustic_polarization}{Early-universe polarization comparison}.}

The distinction between a supplied stock and an unavoidable source can
be tested quantitatively. Three powered paths give measured excess-to-ordinary
energy ratios of 2.104, 5.134 and 10.520. With the standard cold-fluid
response the middle path agrees with the Planck polarization abundance
intervals, while the other two give large held residuals. Assigning the
middle ratio to each galaxy underestimates the optical mass even with
pressure support and doubled baryonic ceilings in the four tested stacks
under the stated two-error shift. The local and cosmic mass ratios can
differ through formation and transport. This comparison requires a
gravitating readout, a physical supply of work and a prediction for where
that work remains in the material.

\subsection{Fixed recovery loss with opposite clock and flight responses}

Place two sharp-difference neutral clocks on distinct physical bonds of the
two-owner assembly. An ordinary unit interacts with a clock contact and
changes its sign-return probability. Calibrate a tick by its successive
half-probability crossings relative to the far clock. Raising ordinary
energy by $17/8$ gives first and second near--far shifts $+0.01465$ and
$-0.00244$. A real memory exchange with the same energy gives $-0.03956$
and $-0.16646$. The changes of successive tick durations differ on the
same hand. A universal static rate multiplier fails on these material
clock records.

The information lost under reduced overlap access can also be held fixed.
Start with a native bright branch of weight $1/4$ and a dark branch of
weight $3/4$. On only the latter apply a partial exchange of its endpoint
loads via a 17-edge bypass in the enclosing federation, restore the
borrowed rails and release the same assembly. Varying the exchange angle
preserves both separate marginals and the full source spectrum. The
resulting source rotation is a symmetry of the interacting regional
Gibbs reference and of the common memory replacement state. Under
memory erasure the input and output relative entropies are separately
constant along the entire family. This covers every erasure probability
with a recorded flag or an unflagged equilibrium replacement.
The linear connected modular score is also constant.

Across this family the connected time-four material displacement ranges
continuously from $-0.134267$ to $+0.170790$ graph hops. Integer
error bounds certify both endpoint signs. The sensitive clocks on a
separately instrumented board give second near--far shifts of opposite
sign, $+0.00880$ and $-0.25508$. These outcomes distinguish physical
sources that the specified modular charge and loss identify. Replacing
either scalar by a universal metric source therefore requires additional
physical information on these constructions.%
\footnote{\href{https://github.com/FloatingPragma/observer-patch-holography/tree/main/model/experiments/interval_echo_shell_flux/product_address/green_response/material_recoil/overlap_source/native_boundary/regional_reference/recovery}{Physical preparation, channel symmetries, integer bounds and clock records};
\href{https://github.com/FloatingPragma/observer-patch-holography/tree/main/model/experiments/interval_echo_shell_flux/product_address/green_response/material_recoil/overlap_source/native_boundary/matched_probe/signal_time}{Clock construction and ordinary calibration}.}

One sharp source reading does transfer between instruments within this
family. Let $u$ be the measured probability of agreement between memory
coin and outgoing load. On source blocks conserved by the native
evolution, the change of density equals $2u-1$ times that of ordinary
orbital dephasing plus $2(1-u)$ times that of ordinary endpoint
randomization. Consequently the same two independently measured
ordinary waveform differences predict both hand waveforms and the
radial readout before seeing their held records. Two further routing
angles satisfy all simultaneous count bounds. The relation transfers
material response functions between preparations. Each instrument
has its own time dependence and physical units.

A common-background test gives the same readers the near and far clock
signs together with the occupied port addresses in each trial. Let $C$
be the change in clock disagreement on releases without a tracer, and
$B$ the change in the second mirror launch's connected radial record,
in units of six hops. Each difference is relative to the same
half-empty, half-paired ensemble. The two shutter exposures are mixed
with equal weights and concealed from the decoder. The statistic
$(C+10B)/11$ has a positive sign for an additional mobile source unit
under its calibrated admission lottery. It has a negative sign for
both routed endpoints, with simultaneous sampling bounds. Every
enrolled joint outcome preserves the bridge charge. Its mean is affine
between the endpoints with weight $(4u-1)/3$. Thus no nonnegative
amplitude multiplying the fixed load response reproduces these
joint records anywhere in this source family.

For $0.67\le u\le0.69$ both launches on both boards nevertheless
shift inward under their own radius protocols. The bare signs follow
from finite propagation bounds, and the equipped signs use the measured
endpoint bounds. A fresh preparation at $u=0.68$ resolved all four
shifts and the opposite signs of the load and overlap statistics.
Independent fringes on the blank contact map give the mobile unit
a kinetic coefficient $0.50002$ in native units. Transferring its
inertia to the clock-filled background fails for this spatial
instrument. Eleven of twelve fringe frequencies miss the blank
calibration, and the two fringe components together exclude a lossless
single-phase description at all six wavelength and appointment pairs.
Identifying a gravitational mass requires a separate calibration.%
\footnote{\href{https://github.com/FloatingPragma/observer-patch-holography/tree/main/model/experiments/interval_echo_shell_flux/product_address/green_response/material_recoil/overlap_source/native_boundary/regional_reference/recovery}{Complete joint readouts, dispersion measurement and held preparation}.}

On the 80-cell equilibrium a source experiment closes a finite
geometric implication. Two simulated observers measure independent
reference and endpoint cohorts using nine pairs of local axes. Their
parity-tagged coincidences determine the connected modular charge
$q=\operatorname{Tr}[D(\log P-\log X)]$, where $X$ is the measured
reference, $P$ its conditional product and $D$ the full endpoint
displacement. Fixed one-head marginals imply $\operatorname{Tr}(D\log P)=0$.
Thus $q=dS_X(D)$. An ordinary intervention fixes $\eta$ between
23.1042361 and 23.1042363 nats per squared range unit. With the
volume derivative below $3\times10^{-15}$ in magnitude, the finite
stationarity certificate gives $|q+\eta\,dA(D)|<1.26\times10^{-9}$ nats.
The count interval $[0.00014802,0.00031100]$ nats therefore implies
$-1.347\times10^{-5}<dA(D)<-6.406\times10^{-6}$ at simultaneous
99\% coverage. A separate finite calculation of the endpoint area
change gives $-9.93158\times10^{-6}$ to the displayed precision.

Two more stations on this preparation give 30 directed timing
histograms. Of the fifteen choices of a tetrahedron, seven areas
decrease, five increase and three signs are unresolved. To allow for
volume changes, use an ordinary perturbation $o$ and an
entropy-preserving head rotation $z$ to calibrate each survey before
predicting the held displacement $d$. If one head entropy balances
$-\eta\,dA+\lambda\,dV$ on all three directions, elimination gives
$q(A_oV_z-A_zV_o)=S_o(A_dV_z-A_zV_d)$. Ten surveys exclude the
counted charge interval even with separately chosen $\eta,\lambda$.
The original quartet agrees. This tests the reuse of a single head
entropy on different reconstructed regions. The proper-ball theorem
instead specifies the entropy and stress of each region.

One candidate local term can be read on each quartet by comparing
the emitter's route label with the observer's echo or timeout. For
reference echo probability $p_r$ on route $r$ and mean $\bar p$,
the connected modular score weights each route's echo-probability
increment by $\operatorname{logit}(\bar p)-\operatorname{logit}(p_r)$
and averages over routes. Nine quartets give a positive increment and
six a negative one. After separate ordinary and rotation calibrations,
ten of the fifteen quartets exclude this local statistic as their bulk
term. On the original four stations the counted interval is
$[3.8809,3.9283]\times10^{-6}$ nats and the geometric forecast is
$[9.7632,10.6834]\times10^{-6}$. The independently measured two-head
charge and this binary echo statistic have different consequences.

What the observers can infer about free fall depends on which records
enter this implication. Each set of six symmetric ranges embeds as a
Euclidean tetrahedron. In optical units the metrics
$e^{2\phi(x)}(-dt^2+dx^2)$ give all four supported stations the same
round-trip times whenever $\phi$ vanishes at those stations. Straight
light paths retain unit coordinate speed. A freely released body at
rest has initial acceleration $-\nabla\phi$, which those endpoint
conditions leave free. In explicit completions of both measured
tetrahedra, two additional clocks bracketing one station distinguish
the gradients that give inward, zero and outward initial motion.
This comparison identifies the missing premise. The finite source
implies the four-station area response, while a deduction of
gravitational attraction also needs a law fixing the relevant clock
gradient or free trajectories from the same source.%
\footnote{\href{https://github.com/FloatingPragma/observer-patch-holography/tree/main/openwave/interval_echo/row_19/selected_collar}{Source count banks, stationarity transfer and free-release comparison}.}

Allowing the comparison load any incoherent distribution over the 24 ports
gives a stronger finite test using the two clocks alone. At exposure
twelve the near and far readers can form
$S=(8357\,\Delta P(++)-91643\,\Delta P(-+))/100000$ from their joint signs.
An integer enclosure gives $S\ge0.0245310$ at both routed source endpoints
and $S\le-2.4879\times10^{-6}$ for a mobile unit with independent fair
coin loaded at each individual address on the empty half of the baseline.
The routed score is affine in the measured source agreement. Every
nonnegative combination of the port responses has score at most zero.
This establishes an operational difference between the sources for
these material clocks with their timing settings supplied. Deducing an
attractive metric for macroscopic observers requires a source of proper
stress and agreement of independent probes in the coarse geometry.

A paid outward trail provides a contrasting persistent source. Every
$j$ enclosed heads carry between $8.5j-16.25$ and $8.5j$ units above
the matched ordinary energy throughout free evolution. When the load
is removed, with extraction work included, their actual canonical
regional modular charge becomes exactly $8.5\beta j$. On this empty
board all memory Hamiltonians act on separate factors. Disjoint
vacuum receivers acquire no signal at any source age. After an ordinary
unit is launched, disturbance by the recorded trail is bounded by the
unit's ability to reach its vertices. The 35-edge launch on the
320-owner carrier changes every ordinary outcome probability by at
most $9.28\times10^{-24}$ through time four. Nearby ordinary units
do scatter in the full contact dynamics. This tests the distinction
between persistent modular energy and a field carried to a distant
observer before release.%
\footnote{\href{https://github.com/FloatingPragma/observer-patch-holography/tree/main/model/experiments/interval_echo_shell_flux/product_address/green_response/material_recoil/overlap_source/native_boundary/stock/aperture}{Native aperture bounds, extraction, observation cadence and distant probes}.}

The same thermal spectroscopy determines the map of written contacts.
For the uniform zero-current double-load preparation, only the boundary
of their support contributes to the initial acceleration of the mean hop
distance to the source. Each live edge leaving a written port contributes
$-\kappa q^2 m_x/N$, where $m_x$ counts its incident writes. The outward
and folded 960-port trails give $-7.9333$ and $-4.9000$ in native units,
and the initial current derivative through each more distant shell
vanishes. The measured map thus suffices to prove local inward motion.
The probe's independently recorded positions check the implication.
Multiplying the recorded modular energy nearly fourfold at fixed marks
leaves the double-load motion identical. A mobile ordinary unit on a
240-port comparison board gives the opposite sign from a written seam
at the adjacent detector. When both sources are present, every initial
shell acceleration is equal to its ordinary-only value for that
preparation. An effective gravitational coupling would have to explain
which common macroscopic clock and trajectory response survives these
source and probe comparisons.

A mobile ordinary unit in thermal equilibrium transmits a measurable
change across an exterior cut. Denote the ordinary probability at port
$x$ by $p_x$ and the sum over its live neighbors by $s_x$.
On an exterior edge the initial probe current derivative into $u$ from
$v$, multiplied by the number of allowed starting ports, is
$4\kappa(s_u+p_u-s_v-p_v)-\partial_t\operatorname{Im}\rho_{uv}$.
The last term is measured by a two-port interference comparison on
separate copies of the ordinary preparation. It vanishes at equilibrium.
On the 240-port board the source readings force increased inward
transfer at the first exterior cut and the opposite change at the next
cut. A charge-conserving insertion clock also slows at the first
exterior station. After a sharp ordinary address read its frequency
profile with the ordinary motion calibration misses the first exterior
acceleration by about 0.46\%. The force inferred from these records
depends on ordinary position frequencies and exchange current as well
as the written contact. The common large-scale metric would have to
recover the observed radial range and the lensing response. A supplied
ordinary stream transmits a passive contact change four to six hops
on this chart. At fixed injected current its change in source has equal
and opposite endpoint contributions. Reversing the feed reverses the
remote occupation change.

An isolated ordinary preparation also transmits a response. Both charts
receive the same uniform in-phase launch and evolve freely for an age
drawn uniformly between zero and $T$. At $T=65{,}536$, separate source
readers resolve an inward change in initial probe transfer at the first
exterior cut and an outward change at the second. Integer contraction
of the full two-load generator gives exactly the source observable on
all twelve exterior cuts for every one-unit preparation. A rational
spectral enclosure yields $[0.0109717,0.0110785]$ and
$[-0.0059931,-0.0059251]$ in the infinite uniform-age limit. It
also proves the two signs for every $T\ge22{,}900{,}210$ native time
units. This statement concerns ensembles of releases with a specified
age distribution. For the uniform random-port launch both charts
give the same reading at all ages. Two preparations whose equal
mixture is that invariant launch have opposite changes by linearity,
including when all initial nearest-neighbor comparisons agree.

For stationary material the integrated release field is
$4\kappa\sum_x(1+d_x)p_x$, since each closed-neighborhood reading
counts the occupied port and its live neighbors. A degree-six chart
at $\kappa=8$ supplies at most 224 native units per ordinary source.
This puts an absolute ceiling on the local potential for any specified
site volume, inertial calibration and number of sources. The isolated
rest profile gives 23 of 369 held orbital speeds within twice their
quoted errors when used as a local potential on resolved baryonic
maps. The native source implication and the transfer to a common
astronomical metric can thus be tested independently.%
\footnote{\href{https://github.com/FloatingPragma/observer-patch-holography/tree/main/code/cosmology/paid_trail_bao}{Independent baryon maps, orbital predictions and source-strength bounds}.}

A weak written contact and a clamped ordinary source also admit a
common finite spatial response. If $H_0=D-a$ has positive blank mode
$\phi$ and energy $E_0$, set $c_{xy}=a_{xy}\phi_x\phi_y$. For a real
eigenmode $\psi$ of $H_0+\epsilon V$ with energy $E$, the finite row
equations give
\[
 L_c(\psi/\phi)_x=-\epsilon\phi_x(V\psi)_x+(E-E_0)\phi_x\psi_x .
\]
Linearization yields a Poisson source with its normalized mean
subtracted. On the completely open blank chart the response obeys
$L(N\,\partial_\epsilon p)=4\kappa(m-\bar m)$, where $m$ is the
independently read incidence of the written edge. This fixes the flux
on every enclosing cut. Physical inverse-square acceleration further
requires a three-dimensional area law and calibrated trajectories. On the
3,840-port native swap-distance survey, the mean crossing area at
radii 16--31 is 1.747 times that at 8--15. Separate positive area
weights for the two kinds of contact allow a factor of at most 1.753.
A squared-radius area law requires at least 3.551 on those intervals,
allowing one hop of uncertainty in the source origin.

With the ordinary source alternated on a reserved port, the same
spatial coefficients and ordinary coupling predict the 239-site
occupation response of a different written seam. Four source-only
intervals force an inward change of the released double, and all four
independent precision position comparisons resolve it. This is a
conditional finite geometric response of measured ordinary material.
Allowing a different reversible exchange coefficient on every link in
the earlier thermal chart leaves a resolved discrepancy with
inclusive motion on ten of twelve exterior cuts. At a native swap
pulse every successfully placed probe transfers, but the first exterior
route retains coherent visibility of only 0.99624 on the written chart:
the accompanying material can distinguish the two routes. These
results identify which probe records the common finite description
predicts, and which retain material information.%
\footnote{\href{https://github.com/FloatingPragma/observer-patch-holography/tree/main/model/experiments/interval_echo_shell_flux/product_address/green_response/material_recoil/overlap_source/native_boundary/stock/aperture}{Source measurements, coherent transfer, independent release and finite proofs}.}

The time for which the source acts is also a record. For a binary
contact $b(t)$, set $F(t)=\int_0^t(b(s)-p)\,ds$ and
$\bar H=H_0+pV$. Writing the exact propagator for $H_0+b(t)V$ as
$U=e^{-iFV}W$ gives the generator $e^{iFV}\bar H e^{-iFV}$ for
$W$. Its derivative in $F$ has norm $\|[H_0,V]\|$. Unitary variation
of constants therefore gives
\[
 \|U(t)-e^{-it\bar H}\|\le |F(t)|\,\|V\|
       +\|[H_0,V]\|\int_0^t |F(s)|\,ds .
\]
Even at equal integrated exposure the timing can affect the readout.
On the native chart, shortening each recorded pulse and exposing rare
copies to the full pulse produce opposite two-hop release changes
at equal expected dose after every completed tick. In four fresh runs
a four-owner repair process supplies the active times, with the pulse
width calibrated to a granted mean exposure of $10^{-4}$. In each
run ordinary position and interference records imply an increased
initial inward release at two, three, four and six hops for ages
sampled uniformly up to 8,192 and 32,768 ticks. Keeping the same
contact connected for the whole active tick gives the opposite
four- and six-hop changes in all eight ensembles. This establishes the
finite motion implication for those measured preparations. How a
macroscopic distribution of overlaps selects its pulse widths and
proper areas remains a separate physical premise.

A second local source removes the external timing setting. Two ordinary
units occupying the opposite endpoints of a stored magnitude-two seam
read the additional energy $-4\kappa$, $-(4\kappa+1)$ or zero according
to the head parities. A single unit reads zero for each. For two sharp
seams on the prepared 2200 repair source the measured connected
logarithm is $q=\log(11/3)/14$ nats upon independently pairing the
records at fixed marginals. Its magnitude resolution retains the full
likelihood ratio on the admitted 36-dimensional support. Since the
difference of the two four-cell tables is a scalar times
$(1,-1,-1,1)$, the response of every calibrated receiver is its
mixed conditional difference times $-q/\log(3/11)$. A local
ordinary-pair interferometer resolves the predicted probability shift.

For a separate double-probe release, let $W$ insert the double
uniformly at the $N-2$ free sites before normalization. The blank
exchange generators on the three- and two-load spaces obey
$T_3W=WT_2$, since each transposition also permutes the available
insertion sites. If $N_R$ counts ordinary singles in region $R$ and
$P_R$ counts the double there, $W^*P_RW=|R|I-N_R$. The kinetic
contribution to $(N-2)$ times the initial enclosed probability
acceleration is therefore $[T_2,[T_2,N_R]]$. The remaining entries
are differences of the native contrast potential
$-2\kappa E(S)-4\kappa s_x(S)$, where $E(S)$ counts occupied
ordinary edges and $s_x(S)$ counts ordinary neighbors of the double.
Ordinary position and pair-interference records determine its
expectation. At age 64 the complete connected source and conditional
ordinary calibration give $[-4.732,-4.674]\times10^{-7}$ at two
hops and $[9.085,9.742]\times10^{-8}$ at six. Separate readings on
the actual two source preparations resolve both changes. This finite
example gives opposite radial signs for a single positive modular
increment and specifies the material conditions under which each
is perceived.

The reference can be tested while varying the amount of material.
Prepare each of four owners with two units with probability $p$
and zero otherwise. Among copies with the same witness, the two-head
masses $a,b,c,d$ change in the doubly positive cell by
$(bc-ad)/(a+b+c+d)$ under independent re-pairing. Sum this over the
accessible witness values to get the per-attempt change $D$.
The measured reference log odds $\ell$ then give $q=-D\ell$ on
the receiver's two heads. At $p=1/16$ a blind re-pairing gives
$+2.728\times10^{-6}$ nats and decreases the ordinary fringe
by about six parts per million. With the third head available the
same material preparation gives $-2.315\times10^{-4}$ nats and
increases the fringe by about 513 parts per million. Independent
simulated readings recover both signs. Simply omitting the third
record from the report leaves the fringe unchanged.

There is also a pair of preparations with the same positive $q$.
At $p=1/4$ thin the blind re-pairing by the ratio of the two charge
increments. The reference odds cross unity between the two fillings.
The same signed charge gives opposite changes in the same fringe
and in the two-hop release. Carrying the reference odds with the
charge predicts both responses. Inferring the extra macroscopic
attraction requires a physical reference and a common geometric
response under the specified coarse graining.

\paragraph{A measured thermal source and a stationarity residual.}
For a frozen two-head preparation let $Z_{ab}$ be the ordinary
thermal trace at each conserved parity. The material is normalized
separately in each such sector. The four magnitude preparations
average over the conserved parities belonging to their sharp heads.
Write $L$ for the resulting mixed difference of $\log Z_{ab}$.
For fixed one-head marginals let $D$ change the joint probability
table in direction $(1,-1,-1,1)$. The derivative of the isothermal
mixture free energy with respect to $D$ is
\[
 \frac{\ell-L}{\beta}+J,\qquad
 \ell=\log\frac{P_{00}P_{11}}{P_{01}P_{10}},
\]
where $J$ is the mixed energy of any extra source interaction.
The term $\ell$ follows by differentiating the measured mixing
entropy. Additive one-head energies and single-head parity entropies
cancel. At $\beta=\log(8/7)$ the ordinary trace gives
$L=-0.0000779163$ to the precision shown, while the source counts
give $\ell$ between $-1.29950$ and $-1.29905$. Stationarity at fixed
geometry would require $J$ between 9.7278 and 9.7312 native energy
units per unit of $D$. The four evacuated sharp head energy readings
are 0, 32.5, 32.5 and 65, with zero mixed difference. A proposed
geometric entropy term would need its own measured area response
and ordinary calibration on this variation.

The same connected charge predicts a positive change of
$[0.00049352,0.00049438]$ in the ordinary internal energy per
accepted re-pairing, consistent with an independent energy read.
On a common chart, hold a nearby ordinary port empty for both
overlap arms, then use one unit at that port as a calibration.
The overlap-to-ordinary ratio for zero-to-one hop positional work
is $[-6.420,-6.408]\times10^{-4}$; for two-hop release of a separate
double it is $[4.156,4.633]\times10^{-6}$. The instruments read
different relative signs on these preparations. Universal
macroscopic coupling of the measured energy remains a separate
physical premise.

\paragraph{Measuring the area at fixed marker volume.}
For each thermal position reading reconstruct the ball using the fastest
ordinary round trip with temporary parking of material on its path. Retain
exactly the prescribed number of stations and count its live boundary
links $A$. Write $B$ for the membership record and $M$ for the head
magnitudes and ordinary positions visible in the ball. Use
$\delta S=-\sum\delta p(B,M)\log p(M\mid B)$ at the native reference.
The source and ordinary calibrations share their reference, cuts and
volume. For 165 stations and increasing address in range ties, the
regional entropy increases by $0.092806$--$0.092831$ nats to first order.
The ordinary calibration predicts $-0.10569$ to $-0.10560$ links if
$\delta S+\eta\,\delta A=0$. Independent simulated counts resolve
$+0.0000127$ to $+0.0000170$ links. Both address orders exclude the
calibrated identity in each of the three nontrivial volumes that always
contain the source. Separate constant coefficients for the two cut
classes also fail on these volumes.

A position-only geometry loses identifiable source information. For
head class $h$ and ordinary position law $p_h(x)$, select each reading
with probability $\min_j p_j(x)/p_h(x)$. This preserves all head
weights and makes the accepted position law independent of $h$.
The acceptance is about $98.24\%$ on this chart. With both heads visible,
the regional entropy tangent under re-pairing retains the head increment
$\log(11/3)/14$, while every position-only area has zero change in the
exactly matched control. A route that interrogates both written heads
directly has a measurable alternative. Its mean earliest receipt shifts
by the change in joint success probability times the smaller available
time saving, with the joint change fixed by source records and ordinary
spectroscopy. A free single at either load level has the same evacuated
generator in the two source arms. The timing implication closes on this apparatus. In a second survey
both phase receipts enter the protected ranges. The 192-station ball
always encloses both contacts. For increasing address in ties, the
source predicts a gain of 0.00426 boundary links, confirmed by a separate
count. Ordinary calibration requires a loss of 0.105. Even with that
calibration repeated in each ball, an extra weight on the two local flags'
conditional entropy must lie in $[-15.33,-15.12]$ at 192 stations and
$[-70.03,-66.46]$ at 219. A macroscopic source law must predict the
trajectories and spatial geometry on the same regions.%
\footnote{\href{https://github.com/FloatingPragma/observer-patch-holography/blob/main/model/experiments/interval_echo_shell_flux/product_address/green_response/material_recoil/overlap_source/native_boundary/stock/aperture/THERMAL_RESULTS.md}{Area survey, matching proof and timed-contact experiment}.}

\paragraph{Free motion inferred from ordinary interference.}
On the continuously occupied 240-port chart a vacancy or excess load
moves on the live edges with hopping coefficient $-1/2$. A defect at
either endpoint of a written contact removes the joint single-occupancy
energy. Write $P_e$ for the indicator of those endpoints. In a frozen
sector $h$ the material generator is $H_h=K+\sum_e a_{h,e}P_e$.
For any diagonal regional reading $M$, put $C=[K,M]$. Its acceleration
operator is
\[
 CK-KC+\sum_e a_{h,e}(CP_e-P_eC).
\]
This commutator identity extends to arbitrary fillings with their
respective occupation projectors. Thus a common initial material
preparation has the same instantaneous acceleration at fixed one-head
marginals. At a later age the head-conditioned material fringes can
differ. Reassigning whole head pairs across independent material
copies removes exactly their covariances with the operators
$CP_e-P_eC$. Outside the source these operators vanish locally.
Earlier interactions change the exterior material readings.

For the real, uniform launch there is no initial flux. Average the
ordinary boundary acceleration at the absolute difference of two
independent uniform times in $[0,T]$. Multiplying by $T^2/2$ gives
the change in terminal occupation. The finite spectral identity is
$1-\cos x=x^2\operatorname{sinc}^2(x/2)/2$, with
$\operatorname{sinc} y=\sin(y)/y$ continued at zero.
The re-paired source and local material calibration predict an excess-load
increase of 129.62--131.94 parts per million within one hop at $T=64$,
confirmed by independent terminal counts.

The edge residual after the observed ordinary kinetic term is subtracted
equals $\sum_h p_h(V_{h,u}-V_{h,v})\operatorname{Re}\rho_{h,uv}$.
Divide by the measured mean real fringe and subtract the native-arm drop
to test an additional scalar potential. The resulting increments exclude
zero circulation on ten of 400 intact triangles for each probe.
Independent ordinary current slopes confirm the excess-load loop on
ports 14, 18 and 22, giving $-4.187$ to $-4.096$ in native energy units.
An extended metric shared by clocks and trajectories requires additional
structure beyond this transient scalar interpretation.%
\footnote{\href{https://github.com/FloatingPragma/observer-patch-holography/blob/main/model/experiments/interval_echo_shell_flux/product_address/green_response/material_recoil/overlap_source/native_boundary/stock/aperture/OCCUPIED_RESULTS.md}{Occupied-source measurements and finite current identities}.}

\paragraph{Measuring the regional entropy premise.}
An interference-preserving volume survey parks the mobile material,
measures its echo ranges and restores it before acquiring address and
two-port interference records on fresh copies. For a local record $u$
with probability $w_u$ and registered ball $B(u)$ of probability $p_B$,
the entropy derivative along a reweighting is the mean change of the
reference score $-\log(w_u/p_B)+S(R_{u,B})$. The one-defect material
restriction $R_{u,B}$ consists of the inside block and a single
homogeneous outcome for an exterior defect. On the ascending 192-marker
survey the overlap gives a positive derivative for both probes, while
the crossing count $52-8c$ depends only on the clamp reading $c$.
Its change vanishes exactly at fixed clamp marginal. A separate observer
receiving only membership and material readings resolves a positive
excess-load entropy derivative. Concavity and independent interference
measurements bound it between $0.0005333$ and $0.0012680$ nats. Thus this
fixed-area result persists after the source labels are withheld.

An independently diluted local presence reading compares a deep interior
and a deep exterior port at matched mean heat. A balanced interior

intervention. The exterior intervention preserves the interior marginal.
For the diluted vacancy comparison the regional difference exceeds
$2.36\times10^{-5}$ nats and the allowed full entropy difference is
below $3.18\times10^{-9}$ nats. Boundary material fringes distinguish
the propagated interventions at 64 ticks. The counted area remains fixed

\paragraph{An exterior clock consequence from a local premise.}
For an ordinary material generator \(K\) on a connected finite chart,
suppose the hopping is nonpositive and the two conserved contact
energies add nonnegative diagonal marks \(A,B\). For frequency below
the inactive band, write \(R_0,R_A,R_B,R_{AB}\) for the four ordinary
resolvents with and without those marks. The finite identity
\[
 R_0-R_A-R_B+R_{AB}
 = R_0 B R_B A R_A+R_B A R_A B R_{AB}
\]
has strictly positive entries when both marks have nonempty support.
Indeed each resolvent is a sum of nonnegative walk matrices with a
positive route between each pair of stations. The local covariance
measurement sets the positive coefficient of the mixed difference.
It therefore fixes the sign of a remote dispersive clock response.
At one hop on the vacancy chart the finite-linewidth quadrature
changes by \(1.60\)--\(2.02\) parts per million, consistent with the
source prediction of \(1.814\)--\(1.818\). The phase reference has a
detuning of \(1/16\) below the rounded band edge and linewidth \(1/32\).

For a separately coupled rail, eliminating the material gives the exact
level equation \(s=g^2 R(\omega-s)_{xx}\) for its downward shift.
A gap \(d>0\) bounds the error in \(g^2R(\omega)_{xx}\) by \(g^4/d^3\).
This converts a resolved shell difference into a signed finite clock
work. The ordinary-clamp frequency shifts have opposite signs for the
two defects, whereas the overlap increases both dispersive responses.
A universal metric interpretation requires a common calibration for
clocks and motion.%

\paragraph{Which part of a local source measurement reaches the stress equation?}
For a two-contact preparation, the connected modular increment is
\(-D\log R\), where \(D\) is the measured change of the doubly positive
cell at fixed one-contact frequencies and \(R\) is the reference
cross-product odds. A local price on either contact cancels from \(R\).
One can therefore test the proposed conversion to ordinary energy
using a separate energy filter. On the occupied chart, observers find
\(R\simeq0.273\) for the written records. Even arbitrarily many
independent thermal vacancy and excess copies sharing the two head lines
give \(R>0.5946\) at the ordinary temperature. Turning the difference
into a required interaction free energy presupposes a Gibbs preparation.
The measured odds close the source premise of the connected-charge

\paragraph{Measuring the source in an ordinary port.}
In the closed 2200 preparation the two selected heads both read zero
exactly when an ordinary middle port has nonunit occupancy. At fixed
head marginals and mean load the measured modular displacement \(D\)
therefore adds \(31D/4\) native energy to each of five adjacent ordinary
bonds. It shortens a protected half round trip to that port by \(D/2\)
hops. Both predictions use the same local record and have independent
simulated readings. On the same site the cost of inserting an ordinary
single changes by \(3D/4\) and the double's change is zero. Both
ordinary calibration gains are nonzero. The total energy on the returned chart can change
with either sign at this \(D\). For the measured graph of transparent
echoes, a radius-two weighted ball transform of a signed port density
has matrix proportional to \(4I+3A\), where \(A\) is the live
adjacency. It is invertible since its determinant is one modulo three.
Radius-two entropy readings thus predict every radius-four reading if
such a source exists. All 240 centers reject that transfer for the two
tested closed repairs. The result concerns the sharp regional algebra
and its measured hop radii. A different small-ball stress identification
must supply its own readings and refinement.%

A local blank-memory read also closes a finite implication for distant
clocks. On the parked survey rails an ordinary double exchanges on
blank contacts and remains in place on written ones. Let \(L\) be the
closed-contact graph Laplacian and \(Lz=e_u-e_v\) with mean-zero \(z\).
For \(N\) ports and six equally likely contact slots per tick, opening
\(uv\) shortens the mean first-passage round trip between \(i,j\) by
\(6N(z_i-z_j)^2/(1+z_u-z_v)\). This follows by solving the first-step
equations and updating the inverse on the mean-zero subspace.
Multiplication by the measured increase in the fraction of open contacts
predicts the ensemble change. For the two exterior receivers it gives
19.04 fewer ticks. Both charts preserve the same uniform tracer density
after every appointment. Thus a measured delay is compatible with an
unchanged stationary spatial population.

The mean delay to a uniformly sampled partner is proportional to
\(z_x^2\) up to a common offset. Outside the changed endpoints one has
\((Lz^2)_x=-\sum_{y\sim x}(z_x-z_y)^2\). If the corresponding well
measures a gravitational potential, its enclosed flux must decrease
outside the source. Positive mixtures of localized contacts retain
this exterior sign wherever every voltage is harmonic. The spatial
identification fails on 91 of 108 eligible outer SPARC curves for
sources confined inside their first tested radii at the fixed baryonic
reduction. A direct timing record can therefore discharge its finite

\href{https://github.com/FloatingPragma/observer-patch-holography/blob/main/code/cosmology/paid_trail_bao/PASSAGE_MASS.md}{Outer orbital flux and distributed-source comparisons}.}

A native write against an ordinary reference of one reads the middle

and its variance increases by the modular displacement \(1/14\).
For an ordinary probe comparing first transfer with first return on
a 46-port ring, this variance predicts the change in arrival rate
from 16.350\% to 21.127\%. Independent simulated trials give
16.341\% and 21.023\%. Both figures use independently sampled source
contacts during each walk. The four home records also expose the
anticorrelation which supplies the whole middle variance. Their
signed sum vanishes on every word, and their regional variance is

One station sees all four at equal echo distance. Its nested
apertures have zero signed-load variance. Another station sees the
same homes at four distinct distances. The sum of RMS per hop there
increases by 0.73669 under the repair. Both final apertures enclose
the identical board. This rejects an observer-independent mass
assignment by summation of these cut amplitudes on this finite chart.
Internal pair readings suffice to restore additivity if one keeps the
one-port variances. Adding \(-2\sum_{i<j\in B}C_{ij}\) to the variance
of the signed regional total gives their sum, with a whole-source change

Dividing by \(t\) yields the same signed contact potential for both
instruments. At the two correlated homes its squared difference has
mean \(256(v+3f)\), where \(v,f\) are the two measured variances.
This follows word by word from the opposite partner on nonunit middle
events. The exact canonical increment, \(4608/77\), lies in both
independent spectroscopic intervals. On the original uncompressed
chart, all 232 surveyed clean ports retain their mean potential.
Local fluctuation growth and a mean gravitational lapse change are
distinct experimental implications.

Even two lower insertion levels can give one apparent ordinary
source. An admissible stationary preparation yields the common
increment 0.01023184 in ordinary-donor units. In the corresponding

static potential independent of the original records gives upper

donor of inter-probe agreement have lower limits of 32.59\% and
32.51\%. A shared metric interpretation must account for how its

A first-flag clock gives a sustained time change from the same source.
Its waiting time when the home load persists to a flag is the mean
of the three inverse calibrated flag probabilities. This is affine

\(1/14\) increase forces a positive elapsed delay for both ordinary
test loads. Renewing the source at each attempted flag reverses the

Tick-triggered advancement biases random-time samples by inverse flag
probability, and both initial preparations approach the same stationary

Unparked release supplies a geometric check independent of the clock.
Across 587 adjacent pairs on the clean chart, the repair changes the

even standing wave changes its initial acceleration oppositely to the

zero change in initial acceleration on every edge. On the original
13--12 pair the double's fourth-order response also has a negative

\paragraph{Which records distinguish nested rest balls?}
At a fixed center on the transparent returned chart, enlarge a proper
ball from radius $r$ to $s=r+1$ while its readable sharp homes and
written seams remain identical. The entropy tangent of this record
algebra stays fixed for any given reference and repair. A signed

lie in the null space of the row $rK_s-sK_r$, where
$K_r(d)=\max(0,r^2-d^2)$. Across 240 centers the 432 such comparisons
at radii two, three and four contain a square minor with determinant

within a single observer's repeated surveys and concerns this particular

load counts give the same positive entropy tangent across these nested

An ordinary bond spectrometer gives an independent regional work
readout. Two uniform integer deadlines and a random endpoint assign
