a negative increment to conditional entropy given the sharp middle
load. For the radius-two observation at the middle home the corresponding
changes are $31/64$ in weighted energy and $-\log(2)/32$ in nats.
A microscopic force measurement is unnecessary for either implication.

that enters the fixed-volume entropy balance with the ordinary

\paragraph{A fixed-volume relation for time-ordered records.}
Use a full native exchange or a wait as the sender's two symbols.
The receiver observes the target and shared head. The normalized
binary distinguishability $p$ is the probability of a changed word
on the full pulse. For 87 of the 99 calibrated inputs the
conditional half-pulse entropy equals $p$ bits. On the other twelve
the full pulse gives four equiprobable outcomes, and the half pulse
gives probabilities $(5,1,1,1)/8$. Two independent full pulses on

Write $A$ for the sum of crossing message distinguishabilities and
$V$ for their incidences at the chosen markers. Let $S$ be the
increase in joint history entropy per internal sweep. Each internal
edge is counted twice in $V$, so
$S+A/2-V/2=(9/4-(5/8)\log_2 5) F$,
where $F$ is the summed four-way frequency inside the region.
Independent ordinary aperture equations fix both half coefficients.
On the prepared repair, the third seam's paired readings predict
a residual of $0.0001803$--$0.0002711$ bits. Separate history and
return counts give $0.0001761$--$0.0002720$ bits.

An ordinary addition to a fraction of the comparison copies matches
the expected communication volume at a radius of eight hops.
The measured branching and history then predict the observed loss
of 0.001346 channel units on the held boundary of the first ball
enclosing the third seam. Matching both volume and history with
ordinary single and double controls isolates the four-way contribution. The local paired read predicts an area gain of 0.0007777--0.0007976,
and the held bound is 0.0006676--0.0008778. Other balls with a measured positive
branch excess give boundary gains at matched volume. This finite
relation measures a contribution to the entropy of motion. Deriving

\footnote{\href{https://github.com/FloatingPragma/observer-patch-holography/blob/main/model/experiments/interval_echo_shell_flux/product_address/green_response/material_recoil/overlap_source/native_boundary/stock/aperture/MOVING_RESULTS.md}{Native sweeps, ordinary calibration and held boundary counts}.}

reading. Let \(x\) be the vector of mean recorded loads, \(b\) the
signed incidence vector of the third written contact, \(J_i\) the
mean observed transfer at its \(i\)-th visit, and \(B_i\) the
measured blank-contact transfer during that round. Native pair
conservation and the blank half-exchange give the finite identity
\[
x_2=B_2 B_1 x_0+B_2 B_1 b J_1+B_2 b J_2 .
\]
Two opposite releases from the endpoints give opposite excesses in
both currents for each of two probe loads on the matched preparations.
The direction-averaged excess therefore vanishes in every terminal
mean. The local records also predict perceived transverse ranges.
A pair of full pulses transmits an unambiguous bit with probability
\(p_t=2u_t+C_t\), where \(u_t\) is the transferred fraction of a
load released onto an empty target on a half pulse. The correction
\(C_t\) is measured by counting target occupations together with
the resident head. Independent renewals give cost \(1/p_t\).
The contact joins stations at the same radius from the central
observer. The radial ranges stay fixed, while the mean distances
between stations on that shell decrease for the repair and increase
for the history- and volume-matched branching excess on both probe
alphabets. This is a finite implication from a locally accessible
event to a shared distance record. Giving these records the physical

\paragraph{Exterior relative acceleration from a measured overlap.}
The full native contact supplies a local precursor for coherent
release. For any sharp input let \(q\) be its probability of exchanging
the two distinct material loads in a full pulse and \(D\) the
probability that two copies disagree about that exchange. In units of
the short-exposure swap probability for a single on an ordinary
blank contact, the coherent initial swap coefficient is \(q+144D\).
All 99 native input columns verify the identity.
Both material alphabets have unit blank coefficient.

Release sharply outside all written endpoints at equal-radius
stations \(U,V\). Let \(d\) be the calibrated shortest relay time,
\(L=d(U,V)\), and use the coordinate
\(\phi(x)=[d(U,x)^2-d(V,x)^2]/(2L^2)\).
The observable \(K\) is the initial second derivative of the projected
separation \(\langle\phi\rangle_V-\langle\phi\rangle_U\),
with the empty launches subtracted. With a unit clock set by the
blank crossing probability, the exact finite identity is
\[
L^2 K=\sum_{i\sim U}d(V,i)^2+\sum_{j\sim V}d(U,j)^2
       -(\deg U+\deg V)(1+L^2).
\]
Each incident contact is blank. When the two preparations agree on
the initial length, a shortened cross-range with every other range
weakly shorter forces a more inward \(K\). The repaired local head
frequencies enforce this condition on 25 of 991 registered chords
with equal initial lengths and two hops of clearance. The remaining
966 are unchanged. The four-hop witness shifts by \(-0.016602\) on
both probe loads. A control with the same modular-cell distribution
and ordinary energies reverses the effect. This proves an operational
exterior tide on the finite construction and specifies the additional
measurements needed beyond the scalar defect. Matching its radial
distribution to observed gravity remains a separate premise.%

The transfer of this measured effect to rotation-curve observers has a

annuli, use the ordinary single's tidal response and the declared
energy-to-mass conversion, and grant each annulus an arbitrary mixture
of physical patch lengths at its imaged mass. A sum of accelerations at
several orbital radii cannot exceed the sum over annuli of their largest
weighted responses. This condition, together with individual radius
bounds, excludes all ten complete SPARC curves in the sample across the

\((\mathrm{km/s})^2/\mathrm{kpc}\), while the two-error lower limit
exceeds \(368.90\). In the executable model the local precursor
discharges the premise for selected exterior tides. Explaining a

distribution and ordinary gravitational normalization.%

A four-relay circuit on the 80-owner carrier isolates a collective
change of the observer's ruler. A 65-unit blank path competes with 52
blank steps and four native transmitting contacts. Their joint cost
is bounded between 12.953 and 12.976 by the fully repaired local
measurements. Every individual repair leaves the four-link cost above
13. With the common 64-unit launch chord the shortened cross-range
forces an inward change of 0.00228134 in magnitude. All native
contacts at both freely released material probes are retained. The
same launch is silent on the unrestricted ruler. Preparing an average

attempts yields exactly the same four probabilities and exterior tide
as the repaired source. The calibrated ordinary count for a single
chain changes from 8.09 to 126.54 between the 240- and 3840-port
material pools. Treating that count as a galactic source mass requires
a physical identification of the mixing volume. The independently

\paragraph{Landmark ranges and common material transport.}
A random two-way message using every available route gives effective

the port count. Write \(z\) for the bypass unit-current voltage and
\(r=z_u-z_v\) for the resistance across a transmitting seam. Increasing
its local success from \(p\) to \(q\) changes \(R(A,x)\) by
\(-t(z_A-z_x)^2\), with \(t=(q-p)/[(1+pr)(1+qr)]\).
At a clean material launch \(x\), harmonicity of \(z\) makes the
change in initial range acceleration
\(-2t\sum_{y\sim x}(z_x-z_y)^2\). Every landmark sees this same inward
change. The change in the difference of two landmark ranges vanishes.

Actual material arrivals give an independent directional test. With
ordinary energy readings and all selectively transmitting words held

frequency by \(0.01565555\). Throughout a fractional displacement
\(0<s\le1\) toward the independent two-head table, the certified
increase exceeds \(s/512\). Both native probe loads therefore acquire
the same change in every material trajectory event, equal to that
increase times the open-minus-blocked transfer. Independent local
and receiver counts certify a lower probability of reaching station
36 before 25 for either load launched at 104. The canonical repair
also changes the selective-word frequencies and gives unequal
changes. The energy-matched experiment identifies a shared propagation

In an ordinary-matched register of 896 returns, a peer's comparison of
the joint tally with a random re-pairing supplies a directly measurable
precursor. Among 4,096 independently prepared registers the measured
increase in contacts that pass both loads is \(0.00819\)--\(0.01562\).
The ordinary bearing \(b(x)\) is the probability that an ordinary
prepared double reaches station 36 before 25 from \(x\). Use this
same position chart to read both loads under both preparations. Stop
at either beacon or at first encounter with the transmitting contact.
Every exterior native ticket then has the same destination in the open
and blocked arms, and the stopped first-step equation gives \(Pb=b\).
Consequently \(P^nb=b\) at every finite deadline, including coarse
observations. Permitting contact crossings gives a different mean
reading from launch 104 at the fourth tick and an eventual probability
change of \(-0.00073242\) for both loads. This gives an operational
distinction between a local source that changes passage and a field
that accelerates material throughout the exterior. A microscopic
force measurement is unnecessary when the measured antecedent entails
the specified observable. Here the finite implication establishes
passage through the contact. The underlying counts and fixed-chart
test are in \texttt{peer\_register\_lab.json} and
\texttt{peer\_exterior.json}.

\paragraph{Ordinary spectroscopy of a retained overlap contribution.}
A proposed source can be tested without resolving its microscopic
gravitational pull. On the same 448 returned words, freeze the
first-return probabilities \(\pi_g=d_g/448\) of the 59 components
connected by ordinary exchange. At inverse temperature
\(\beta=\log(8/7)\), the modular reading on a component is
\(\beta H+\log(Z_g/\pi_g)\). Here \(H\) is the ordinary exchange
Hamiltonian and \(Z_g\) is its thermal trace on that component.
For component-uniform records with matched ordinary, marginal and
selective readings, exact finite spectral elimination identifies
\(\Delta\langle K\rangle=c_\beta\Delta\langle V_g\rangle\), where
\(V_g\) is half the mean squared difference of independent ordinary
energy readings in component \(g\). The coefficient is positive at
all positive temperatures and is \(0.00563733\) at this thermometer.
The two-clock calibration and independent prepared-register counts yield
\([-0.001042,-0.000253]\) nats for its modular increment. If the
thermal reference exchanges the components, the same matched heat
gives zero. The reference therefore has observable consequences.

After native writing resumes, an observer reporting each subsequent
return independently with probability \(p\) infers a component
excess of at least \(0.01065356p\) nats after subtraction of ordinary
heat, for every \(0<p\leq1\). Ordinary probes resolve the excess at
\(p=1/8\) and \(1/64\) in the executable. On the initial preparation,
energy and stiffness work calibrated independently on ordinary
perturbations give zero for all ten contiguous regions, while the
four measured single-home modular increments have signs \(+,+,-,-\).
Every exterior ticket gives the same stopped position update for
both loads and all eleven contact heads. Induction therefore gives
the same entire position transcript, and the same comparison of such
transcripts, up to first contact or beacon arrival at any finite
deadline. Thus the new measurement
discharges the premise for a modular-energy increment. To apply
the small-ball relation to it, the same source must supply the
lapse-weighted regional stress and fixed-volume stationarity at
ordinary normalization on a common family of reconstructed balls.
The finite spectra, bounds and raw counts are in
\texttt{sector\_calorimetry.json} and \texttt{sector\_retention.json}.

\paragraph{A measured precursor closes one fixed-volume implication.}
Leave the registered loads in their four homes. During ordinary
ranging, park any resident on the route in an adjacent empty station
by a native swap, acknowledge
the target, retrace the route and restore the resident. From
station 13, rank six markers by round-trip time with ascending address
order at a tie. One binary outcome records an empty home at station 20
and ten boundary links. The other records an occupied home, a
different marker membership and fourteen boundary links. Write
\(\Delta f\) for the change in the reported empty-home frequency.
Then \(\Delta A=-4\Delta f\) for every perturbation of this record.
The finite first-return chain on the prepared source gives
\(\Delta f\ge 0.01339400p\) at every positive independent reporting
probability \(p\). The regional thermal reference difference gives the
measurable source \(\delta Q_B=c\Delta f\), with
\(c\in[0.44435,0.44850]\) nats. This closes the implication from
a local source measurement to a reduction of the observed mean area
at fixed marker count.

The retained ordinary reference has empty probability \(3/56\).
Its entropy tangent is \(\log(53/3)\Delta f\). Moving an existing
ordinary double into the home independently gives an area increase of
four and the opposite unit entropy tangent. These two measurements
fix the coefficient \(\log(53/3)/4\) in the stationary combination
at six markers. Seven markers give twice that coefficient. At eleven
and thirteen markers both marker membership and total cut-link rate
are fixed, yet the ordinary-calibrated regional entropy tangent
exceeds \(0.0752131p\) nats. Thus this local contraction fails the
same-stress, common-normalization condition on the tested nested
regions. It also has the sign opposite to the inward ordinary
transfer. A pair of native records with the same three contact
energies and full component spectrum changes a two-load acknowledged
trip from 32 ticks to 6 by opening the final contact. The interaction
of the material probe with the records is part of the geometric
premise. The two local controls, trajectories and calibration bounds
are in \texttt{sector\_balls.json}.

\paragraph{Rest energy from the measured regional sources.}
The binary source \(q_6=cx\) determines the change \(x\) in the
empty-home frequency. Resolving the double frequency as well gives a
second thermal reading \(q_h=ax+by\), where \(y=\Delta P(2)\).
The two independently calibrated reference coefficients \(b,c\) are
strictly positive. The ordinary rest inventory at that home changes by
\[
  \Delta R=48(-x+3y)=48\,\frac{3c q_h-(b+3a)q_6}{bc}.
\]
Ordinary spectroscopy and timing on the clean bond provide an
independent measurement of the right-hand side. They satisfy
\(\Delta E=-8.5x+24y\) and \(\Delta T=-2x\), giving
\(\Delta R=6\Delta E-\tfrac32\Delta T\). On the prepared source,
\(\Delta R\ge1.28424p\) for every \(0<p\le1\). At \(p=1/8\)
the two modular readings give \([0.6924,1.5079]\) native units.
Independent spectrum and timing observations give \(+1.01172\)
units and a 99\% interval \([0.37736,1.64609]\).

Delivering a single or a double from station 38 to the empty home
places 48 or 192 ordinary rest units inside the eleven-marker survey.
The marker membership and boundary count stay fixed with either
tie convention. The ordinary deposit lengthens the protected round
trip by two ticks. The overlap preparation shortens that trip because
empty homes become more frequent while double reports also increase.
The finite inference establishes a local ordinary rest inventory.
Its identification with an exterior gravitational source requires a
regional stress law at the ordinary coupling. The delivery routes,
spectral readings and return-kernel bounds are in
\texttt{sector\_home.json}.

\paragraph{Which exterior clocks can recover a joint modular reading?}
On the protected survey, a sender chooses one of six contact slots.
Waiting for every acknowledgement gives the mean one-way time
\(H_{ij}=(Gl)_i-(Gl)_j+S(G_{jj}-G_{ij})\), where \(G\) is a
grounded inverse of the unweighted Laplacian, \(l_i\) sums the six
local service times and \(S=\sum_i l_i\). This solves the ordinary
first-step equation with \(H_{jj}=0\). On a fixed connected chart,
changing the service times multiplies all mean commutes by the same
factor. The remaining directional term is a difference of station
offsets and cancels around every tour.

Holding the appointment duration fixed and sampling an independent
service phase changes the embedded walk. The measured conductance
is \(1/(1+o_i+o_j)\). Four homes supply three blocks of the ordinary
chart, connected through articulation vertices. Its mean commute is
\(6N\langle R\rangle\), where \(R=R_0+\sum_i A_i o_i+DXY\) and
\(X,Y\) are occupations at the two homes sharing a block. The
paired-minus-independent regional modular variation is
\(q_{\rm conn}=\lambda\,\Delta\operatorname{Cov}(X,Y)\);
the independently measured retained and exchanged references give
\(\lambda\in[-3.06161,-3.02258]\) nats. The corresponding excess in
\(\langle R\rangle\) is exactly \(Dq_{\rm conn}/\lambda\).
Five ordinarily calibrated exterior baselines reconstruct an imposed
joint change of \(1/2\) as \(0.4860\) with a simultaneous 99.6\%
interval of \([0.3577,0.6143]\), while every single-home marginal
is held fixed.

The probability of reaching beacon 36 before 25 from station 104
changes by \(-3.050\times10^{-5}\) in the peer arm at \(p=1/8\).
A held source and independent receiver acquisition bound it between
\(-3.93\times10^{-5}\) and \(-2.60\times10^{-5}\). The beacon
coordinate is harmonic at every clean exterior launch. The native
second derivative in that coordinate vanishes for both ordinary
loads; the fourth derivative is signed across the 135 distant
launches.

For two independent probes released from rest, the observers can
instead measure the separation of their terminal positions on
their common ruler \(d\). The native hopping amplitude at a clean
launch is \(-1/2\). Its transition probabilities give the exact
initial separation acceleration
\[
 a_d(i,j)=-\tfrac12\big[(L_0d)_{ij}+(L_0d)_{ji}\big],
\]
where \(L_0\) is the ordinary unweighted Laplacian. On \(d=\langle
R\rangle\), the locally measured covariance at \(p=1/8\) implies an
inward contribution at stations 48 and 82, between
\(6.29\times10^{-9}\) and \(3.27\times10^{-8}\) in normalized
commute units per native time squared. For the matched intervention,
both ordinary probe loads also resolve an inward change on
\(\sqrt{\langle R\rangle}\) at stations 50 and 82 after a quarter tick.

At unchanged launches the Green difference is harmonic in each
column. Hence \(\Delta a_R(i,j)\) equals half the sum of all the
incident resistance changes at \(i\) and \(j\). Positive ordinary
deposits away from those contacts have nonnegative resistance changes
on every edge. This proves an instrument-specific distinction between
the inward overlap response and the ordinarily calibrated response.
The common-metric test requires orbital and light trajectories using
the ordinary coupling. See \texttt{sector\_passage.json}
and \texttt{sector\_passage\_tide.json}.

\paragraph{Earliest arrivals, image intensities and clock synchronization.}
Every exterior pair on the occupied chart admits an ordinary
shortest route that avoids all homes. The shortest travel time and
the incoming ports tied for first are the same on every returned
preparation. At stations 72 and 97, two of the eight ordinary
shortest routes visit both homes 8 and 44. The route delay on this
fraction \(f=1/4\) is \(2(X+Y)\) in empty-link acknowledgement
periods. Re-pairing the occupancy reports at fixed marginals changes
the three time bins. The paired-minus-independent probability
contribution is \(fC(1,-2,1)\), with \(C=\operatorname{Cov}(X,Y)\).
Its first moment vanishes and its variance contribution is \(8fC\).

If the sender selects among the fastest routes on each repeatable
preparation, the receiver can also detect a change in the incoming
port fractions. Three calibrated fractions reconstruct a prepared
covariance change of \(1/2\) in the interval \([0.4348,0.5390]\)
with simultaneous 99.5\% coverage for the ordinary single.
These exterior records predict an inward contribution of
\(3.380\) to \(4.213\) in units of \(10^{-6}\) on the separate
commute acceleration ruler at stations 48 and 82.
Under a fixed route lottery the time-integrated fractions are
unchanged because every signal retains its terminal port.
These two predictions concern the recorded guided messages.

Two routes visiting one home each yield total times \(12N+2S_X\)
and \(12N+2S_Y\) over \(N\) distinct members. Relative to independently
re-paired totals, their mean squared time disagreement differs by
\(-8\operatorname{Cov}(S_X,S_Y)\). At \(N=896\) the native peer's
gain per member over the ordinary covariance is at least \(0.02884p\)
for every positive reporting probability \(p\). This forces a change
in a comparison made entirely at the receiver. The mean time on
a series of links is additive for every dependence between them.
It gives no corresponding amplification simply by increasing the
number of records. See \texttt{sector\_fronts.json} and
\texttt{sector\_fronts\_collective.json}.

\paragraph{Which measured entropy enters the fixed-volume balance?}
Two distinct members of the same peer register can be compared with
independent members having the identical single-member law. For any
fixed region the ordered record has additive price under a product
reference. Its entropy first variation vanishes because the signed
joint intervention has zero row and column sums. If the observer
retains only the sum at each home, each outcome instead has the
price of its pooled probability. At reporting probability \(1/8\)
the native pooled four-home entropy variation is \(-0.000361216\)
nats at the ordinary sharp reference. At the two calibrated thermal
references the variations differ by \(+0.0001036\) to
\(+0.0001254\) nats. A balanced control directly measures this
instrument using four ordinary links. Re-pairing their records gives
a relative modular increase of \(0.02250\)--\(0.05111\) nats.

Center the ball at marker 231 and hold its cardinality at 197.
Order equal travel times by marker address. Over all mean link costs,
the largest inside upper time/address is \((12,44)\) and the smallest
outside lower pair is \((12,46)\). The same members always lie inside
and none of the 36 crossing links touches a home. Their times are
constant. Across the regional survey 26 balls admit a resolved positive
area coefficient from an ordinary deposit. All 26 miss the
distinct-member entropy balance at that coefficient for each of the
two choices of record. This checks the proposed entropy and area on
one finite chart with volume in markers. Taking the measured regional
charge to be the geometrically normalized modular stress of (G2)--(G4)
requires a physical identification on the same refinement family.
For an ordinary slot walk with acceptance set by the measured link
period, the local chart gives a propagation consequence. From a
common baseline at fixed one-member histograms, two mixtures with
exactly the same increase in the four-home modular charge give
opposite changes in the probability of escape from this ball by
256 ticks. For exterior launches the killed transition matrix up
to first entry is identical in both preparations. The same charge
therefore permits distinct interior propagation responses. See
\texttt{sector\_register\_balls.json} and
\texttt{sector\_register\_escape.json}.

\paragraph{A finite implication from clock fluctuations to relative acceleration.}
Use the protected walk on \(n=239\) markers with edge conductance
equal to inverse mean link period. Write \(P=I-L_w/6\) and \(G=L_w^+\).
Every station has mean inclusive return interval \(n\) in every
preparation. If \(V_i\) is the variance of its return interval, the
renewal identity and the diagonal resolvent give
\(V_i=12n^2G_{ii}+n-n^2\). For two clean ordinary launches on the
normalized commute ruler \(R_{ij}=G_{ii}+G_{jj}-2G_{ij}\), the Green
column changes are harmonic in either exterior coordinate. The
native initial hopping acceleration for either load therefore gives
\[
 \Delta\ddot R_{ij}
 =-\frac{(L_0\Delta V)_i+(L_0\Delta V)_j}{24n^2}
\]
with \(L_0\) the empty-link Laplacian. Thus the variance field of the
local clocks predicts a common perceived relative acceleration.
The stationary frequency of every inclusive station clock is
unchanged. A counter requiring a successful departure slows at the
occupied homes and their neighbors. Both counters can use the same
station log. Their frequency ratio is the departure probability
given a visit, so its source-dependent change survives every common
conversion of ticks to proper time.

At the fixed 48/82 launch pair, two controlled mixtures can have the
same positive four-home thermal modular increment and identical
single-home timing distributions. One reduces separation
acceleration and one increases it. To predict this ruler, the four
home histograms together with the joint distribution of the totals at
8 and 44 suffice. The three source-sensitive articulation blocks
contain respectively these two homes and each of the two remaining
homes. Their resistances add along each terminal path. Thirteen
calibrations determine the response on all 81 ordered configurations.
This realizes the precursor-to-geometry implication on the specified
ruler. Universal gravitating stress would also have to predict
stationary clock gradients at the ordinary coupling. See
\texttt{sector\_return\_clocks.json}.

\paragraph{Can two observers see ordinary mass enter a region and its boundary contract?}
An operational rest energy is the spectral gap of a standing material
preparation relative to a reference rail carrying the same conserved
load. In the blank six-slot network the ordinary single and double
give gaps 48 and 192 in native units. On the calibrated graph
Laplacian both energy branches satisfy \(E=E_0+\lambda/2\) exactly.
Thus both have kinetic mass one when \(p^2=\lambda\) and \(\hbar=1\).
A common relativistic identification of both rest gaps with the
kinetic masses times one limiting speed squared requires additional
dynamics. Neither gap uses a gravitational trajectory for calibration.

On the 240-marker apparatus an ordinary exchange actually carries the
load from port 38 through the boundary link \(68\)--\(22\) to
port 20. Two local readers identify the arriving load by separate
energy readings. After deposition the same eleven markers and all six
boundary links return the same timings as before delivery, even
though the enclosed rest inventory has increased by 48 or 192. During
transit one resolution of tied ranges temporarily changes the cut to
five links. Increasing the probability of sending a double from
one quarter to one half increases the Shannon entropy of the shared
arrival label by \(0.1293\)--\(0.1346\) nats and increases local
rest inventory by \(47.54\)--\(49.51\) units at simultaneous 99\%
counting coverage. The readers agree on every ticket. This directly
separates agreement on an ordinary entropy increase from the
additional dynamical requirement of entropy stationarity at fixed
proper volume.

A second exchange keeps both endpoints occupied. The eleven-marker
rule puts the receiver on different sides for the two orderings of
tied ranges. Including its complete radar shell gives twelve markers,
with the receiver at range three and the donor at five. Swapping a
single with a double across their common boundary moves 144 units
of rest inventory inward. Independent bond-energy counts give
\(119.9965\) for the regional energy gain, with a 99\% interval of
\([119.9541,120.0389]\). The exact value including interactions is
120 and the exterior loses 120. Both ordinary probes record the same
periods on all 627 links before and after this exchange. Raising its
probability from one quarter to one half increases the entropy of the
regional material register by 0.130812 nats with the outside ticket
withheld. The protected periods depend only on which endpoints need
parking. Every ruler computed from them therefore gives identical
geometry for both sharp outcomes. See \texttt{occupied\_mass\_exchange.json}.

There is a positive finite result on the independently prepared
80-cell stationary survey. A read of parked parity energy (levels
16 and 17) and full head tomography gives
\(dS/dE=1.37593\)--\(1.37612\) along its ordinary intervention,
including the equilibrium enclosure. A right-head rotation
compensates the survey volume with zero entropy and energy slope.
The ordinary-matter area coefficient \(\eta=23.10424\)
implies \(dA|_V/dE\) between \(-0.059562\) and \(-0.059553\)
squared range units per unit parked energy, rounded outwards.
Independent vacancy and excess radar measurements both resolve the
negative sign. This proof concerns stored energy on one constrained
survey. For the same energy write, 24 of 36 fixed-proper-volume
balls on six allowed triangulated fillings contract and 12 expand.
On each vertex cone the area slope scales as \(\ell^2\). Dividing by
\(\ell^4\), as required for a smooth bounded three-dimensional
stress density, gives a coefficient four times larger on every
radius halving. Establishing the ordinary Einstein response requires
a common physical metric and independently conserved ordinary stress
on regions that realize the smooth small-ball scaling. See
\texttt{ordinary\_mass\_crossing.json} and
\texttt{ordinary\_stationarity.json}.

\paragraph{A finite area prediction from paired-head readings.}
Let \(X\) be the prepared survey reference, \(P\) its independent
pairing conditional on parity, and \(Y\) a mixture preserving the
individual head records and parked energy. Subtracting the two
relative entropies to \(P\) gives
\[
 \Delta S=D(X\Vert P)-D(Y\Vert P)
 =\mathrm{Tr}[(Y-X)(\log P-\log X)]-D(Y\Vert X).
\]
The incoming head measurements on a finite held intervention give
\(7.0204\)--\(7.1043\) micro-nats for the reference modular charge
increment and \(5.2603\)--\(5.7010\) micro-nats for the entropy
increase. A lottery with probability \(1/65536\) of independent pairing
and an additional two-head write brackets the measured survey volume.
The write is chosen using entropy and volume alone. At an exact match,
concavity and the certified finite maximum force an area decrease of
at least \(8.15\times10^{-8}\) squared range units under the ordinary
calibration. The allowance for the measured reference's distance from
the exact maximum is \(1.03\times10^{-12}\) nats in the Lagrangian.
Independent timing counts enclose the contraction at matched volume
between \(1.6768\times10^{-7}\) and \(4.4257\times10^{-7}\). This
proves a finite geometric response on that four-station projected
survey. The physical identification of a bulk boost and conserved
stress on proper balls enters the Einstein implication separately.
See \texttt{finite\_pairing\_dose.json}.

\paragraph{Closing the finite entropy premise with an internal link.}
For a held lottery over three calibrated preparations, each measured

link counts bound a polygon in that simplex. Minimizing the linear
concavity bound for the entropy gain over the polygon gives
\(\Delta S\ge1.8089\times10^{-7}\) nats from one signed internal
transfer. The decoder withholds the held head readings and the area.
Let \(\delta\) bound the reference deficit in \(S+\eta A-\lambda V\).
The same global curvature bound on the geometric objective and the
relative-entropy remainder give
\[
 \eta(A_X-A_Y)\ge\Delta S-\lambda\,\Delta V-\delta .
\]
Fresh clock records enclose \(\Delta V\) between
\(-3.001\times10^{-10}\) and \(5.591\times10^{-10}\). With
ordinary calibration this forces at least \(6.5922\times10^{-9}\)
squared range units of contraction. Direct inference of area from
those clock records gives a decrease between \(3.0410\times10^{-7}\)
and \(3.0660\times10^{-7}\). See
\texttt{link\_source\_contraction.json}.

\paragraph{A source measurement before the geometric reading.}
Terminal probe positions at six senders suffice to bound the entropy
and volume changes without a recipe for the source, a parity label or a
held clock. On the paired intervention these bounds in the preceding
inequality force \(A_X-A_Y\ge 2.3446\times10^{-7}\) squared range
units. A direct range forecast from the same source records overlaps
the independently measured area. The reconstruction concerns the
31-coordinate parity-resolved record algebra. See
\texttt{position\_source.json}.

For a physical midpoint interrogation, write \(C,M\) for the click
and miss amplitudes and \(\gamma\) for the pointer overlap. The source
change relative to orthogonal pointer records is the sum over terminal
outcomes of \(\gamma C X M^\dagger+\bar\gamma M X C^\dagger\).
Four phase preparations measure the two components of its modular
price \(q\). Write \(G=\lambda V-\eta A\), let \(h\) bound the
prepared reference's Hilbert--Schmidt displacement from equilibrium,
let \(f>0\) bound the minimum eigenvalue from below there and along the
displacement, and let \(r\) bound the trace distances of both endpoints
from the prepared reference. Integrating the independently calibrated
geometric Hessian along the chord \(D=Y_\gamma-Y_0\) gives
\[
 |\Delta G-q|\le (h/f+0.72\sqrt{8}\,h+0.72r)\|D\|_1 .
\]
The source readings predict all nine nonzero held area signs, including
both contraction and expansion. In a six-station test, thirty independent
route readings gave fixed-volume area changes of six vertex balls for
each of six fillings and three flight windows. In every filling, adding
a common nonnegative multiple of the terminal site's connected charge
to its marginal modular price or parked energy fails the ordinary
calibration. The missing input for the field equation is a regional stress
on the same proper balls. See \texttt{source\_interference.json}
and \texttt{regional\_density.json}.

\paragraph{Comparing contraction with accumulated material impulse.}
For the omission that contracts the calibrated area, take the two raw

An arbitrary contribution \(b\) that changes sign between them drops
out of \((s_++s_-)/2\). Suppose \(c_{+,h},c_{-,h}\) are the ordinary
impulses measured for service history \(h\). Any common ordinary source
with nonnegative weights \(w_h\), even in the presence of \(b\), has
average \(\sum_h w_h(c_{+,h}+c_{-,h})/2\). All twelve measured
history averages exceed \(5.6493\times10^{-10}\), while the observed
average on the contracting preparation is below \(-6.8106\times10^{-12}\)
in the same oriented units. Independent overlap records also resolve
the adverse sign. This tests the inference from a fixed-volume
stationarity calculation to the motion of the same material probes.
Clock contraction on this chart establishes one geometric consequence.
The stress that governs trajectories in the Einstein limit
requires calibration on the matching proper regions. See
\texttt{ordinary\_service\_distribution.json} and
\texttt{charge\_even\_material.json}.

\paragraph{Do clocks and material probes assign the same ordinary source?}
Calibrate the two charge signs of a contact force, a total momentum
rate and a four-tick impulse against an ordinary parity write. The

weighted flight times has an interval-certified inverse. At an ordinary

dose predicts \(3.53365\)--\(3.53461\). This is a controlled

the measured contractions are \(1.33791\)--\(1.36833\) and
\(3.53414\)--\(3.53415\), respectively.

All fourteen clock-gain-independent edge ratios differ at those
programmed settings. Across six spatial fillings, fourteen of the

volume and twenty-two are larger. These areas share the underlying
clock records. Over the wider uncertainty of the exact six-meter
settings the individual ball intervals include equality. A joint
interval test of the edge ratios excludes equal shape for the whole

ordinary fractions in \((0,1]\). This leaves the physical stress
assignment to predict how both kinds of observer respond to a

\texttt{six\_meter\_clocks.json} and \texttt{pointer\_shape.json}.

The angular derivative at the ordinary endpoint is resolved on every
one of the thirty-six balls. Inside its incident tetrahedra a vertex
with solid angle \(\Omega\) gives
\(dA|_V=r^2\,d\Omega/3\). A central score and a point-supported
boost have combined form \(C+Dr\). At radii \(r,r/2,r/4\) a contrast
with coefficients \((1,-3,2)\) cancels both terms and retains
\(3\,dA(r)/8\). Even with separate coefficients for each vertex and
filling the measured area slopes exclude that source assignment. A

\paragraph{How much regional source information does ordinary matter require?}
A terminal observer records excess energy, connected modular increment
and marginal modular increment at six stations and one complement.
Twenty-one of the 36 ordinary histories form an interval-invertible
calibration of these scores. For every other history a signed combination
of the standards matches all scores and gives a different response on
at least thirteen of the twenty clock and material meters. Its two
signs define two realizable changes of ordinary-write probabilities.
On the equal-score directions a differentiable response through \(k\)
additional readings factors through \(\mathbb R^k\). Certified minors
require at least four more independent readings for clocks, four for
material, and six for both together, counted over the whole survey.
Five additional launcher energies determine the complete net inflow
at every station because the measured total energy is conserved.
Holding all these integrated flows fixed leaves joint rank at
least four on the resulting ordinary variations. See
\texttt{regional\_transport.json}.

At the centre of one preparation pair, intervals for all twenty-one
score differences contain zero. Thirty of the thirty-two parity-resolved
head moments differ. Their forecast for the held slope of the log edge
ratio $(1,2)/(0,1)$ and the independent clock interval are, respectively,
\[
 [4.06298,4.08986]\times10^{-9},\qquad
 [4.05684,4.09708]\times10^{-9}.
\]
The parity records predict a geometric response that the integrated
source scores discard. Identifying physical stress at the ordinary
coupling requires a common proper region and boost calibration.%
\footnote{Measurements and rank certificates in
\nolinkurl{regional_source_calibration.json},
\nolinkurl{regional_rank.json} and

\paragraph{A measured contraction at fixed volume and zero mean work.}
The observation pointer changes the native propagation energy by
\(aW-bJ\), where \(a+ib\) is its overlap and \(W,J\) are the two
incident hopping quadratures. A terminal observer independently reads
the energy from the slope of a controlled short flight against a bypass.
The sum of the directed parity currents entering each of six stations
also predicts the local parked-energy rate read by separate position
snapshots. At each enrolled flight duration an orientation perpendicular
to the work row gives zero mean work. Observer records at three such
orientations give positive modular increments and reduced radar areas.

Equal proportions of two of these duration ensembles admit a continuous
pointer adjustment whose endpoints bracket the original volume. For
some setting the enclosed volume and mean flight energy therefore both
agree exactly with the distinguishing-pointer baseline. On the bracket
the measured full modular increment \(q\), ordinary gain \(\eta\), and
the finite equilibrium estimate give
\[
  |q+\eta\,\Delta A|\leq 9.374\,10^{-9},\qquad
  1.1385\,10^{-6} < q < 1.1752\,10^{-6}
\]
at equal volume. This forces a contraction of \(4.887\)--\(5.127\)
in units of \(10^{-8}\) squared range units. Independent clocks at the
programmed center resolve the decrease. The two material impulse signs
give disjoint ordinary-energy equivalents. This closes the source
