to Einstein dynamics requires agreement between regional stress and
motion on the same reconstructed geometry.
See \texttt{flight\_energy.json} and \texttt{fixed\_volume\_flight.json}.

Each station can also read the entropy derivative of its two local hit
blocks and their common miss outcome. Subtracting the centered-ball
boost of the measured propagation energy gives a positive remainder
at all six sites for radar radius \(1/2\) in the bypass convention.
No positive local entropy-to-area gain covers all ordinary histories
on any of the 36 registered vertex balls. On a shrinking ball with
the same single interior receiver, the intercept of the measured
remainder remains nonzero at every station, which excludes a finite
rest-frame anomalous stress in that implementation. See
\texttt{local\_cap.json} and \texttt{cap\_stress\_radii.json}.
On the separately specified refining protected-word screen, closed-ball
records admit positive radial shell weights at each whole hop on all four
tested resolutions. The inferred enclosed energy scales as the cube of
relative radius in the many-hop limit under the flat rest-ball weight.
The same protected clocks are insensitive to the word intervention.
This separates experimentally realizing the radial source condition
from establishing its coupling to the clock geometry on that branch.
Independent phase standards can interrogate the recorded seam energies.
With unequal exposures the measured modular increment predicts both
components of their paired fringe on the same source. The two
components give incompatible time shifts under an independently
calibrated change in exposure duration. See
\texttt{resolved\_shells.json} and \texttt{word\_phase\_read.json}.

\paragraph{Measuring rest energy, propagation and a distributed source on one screen.}
Let the written contacts form a perfect matching in each cell and use its
identity to store the local word. On a calibrated blank contact, a sharp
load \(j=1,2\) transfers in one swap appointment. Written contacts in
the one-mobile-load sector contribute the same energy before and after
the load passes. Measured connectivity gives the graph Laplacian \(L\).
The native port standard and the written matching give the position
generator \(L/2+40j^2 I\), apart from the stored energies. On the
all-absence screen a bypass phase comparison resolves rest energies
\(40\) and \(160\) in native inverse-time units. Relative spectroscopy
of the lowest two modes gives a gap divided by the first nonzero
Laplacian eigenvalue within \(6\,10^{-7}\) of \(1/2\) for each load.
A unit placed at the calibration contact gives energy \(42\) above
vacuum. These are operational energy and propagation standards for
this measured graph.

For a freely moving probe, a local positive ordinary-load control and
the distributed charge give distinct initial responses on the same
inside-occupation meter. Launch real superpositions on the two sides
of a boundary contact and take half the difference between the
accelerations for the two relative signs. Subtract the empty control.
One ordinary unit on the neighboring inside contact gives \(8.5\) and
\(16.25\) for the two probes, both resolved by short-time count
records. For the correlated word intervention, write the one-probe
generator as \(H_0+A_i+B_j\) up to a scalar. The two changes have
disjoint cell support. For a diagonal counter \(X\), the mixed terms
of \(-[H,[H,X]]\) vanish. Every remaining one-word term cancels at
fixed marginals. This proves a zero initial response for any initial
one-probe packet in this experiment. At finite time there are
detectable changes of both free transport and shortest clock ranges.
See \texttt{initial\_acceleration.json} and \texttt{word\_routes.json}.

On four refining screens the same regional word price predicts the
variance of the summed material survey times. With identical local
time standards a shared writer forces the square of a timing contrast
times a positive admission factor. Both loads resolve its positive sign.
Reversing the second physical encoding reverses it. Independent clocks
also record whether the survey finishes before a random deadline. Every
strictly intermediate survival rate predicts more on-time returns under
the same encoding. At two such rates the observers resolve unequal
shortenings on each of the two coarsest boards. At the finest resolution
the direct deadline counts permit zero. The measured source and the
finite clock implication determine the sign. The baseline
quarter-survival fraction also exceeds the bound that would allow both
clocks to report one duration displacement at any positive aperture.
Their leading shortenings differ by a factor of \(32/9\) on the
shrinking many-hop continuation. Subtracting the survival excess fixed
by the measured source leaves an inferred common travel delay consistent
with zero. An extra center round trip on a calibrated blank contact is
recovered with the same correction. A second comparison
tests whether the regional source readings can be simultaneous
rest-ball moments of a single positive measure. Summing the two radius
kernels over centers gives a bound at each possible mass position.
Positivity carries it to every combination of such positions.
Station-supported mass fails that bound at all four resolutions of
the homogeneous source on the protected clock distances. These
finite implications identify which observer comparisons survive
changing the aperture, the probe and the word encoding.
See \texttt{regional\_travel\_times.json} and
\texttt{overlapping\_shells.json}.

\paragraph{An ordinary rest source and an inferred interior on one screen.}
With every intercell contact open and the absence matching written, take the twelve gateway maps \(G L^k\) of
the home cell for \(0\le k<12\), where \(L\) is its one-load
Laplacian block and \(G\) reads the external portals. Their common
kernel is invariant and has spectrum \(4,6,6\). Its lowest mode has
opposite amplitudes on the two pairs of internal ports and vanishes
at the portals. It is an eigenmode of the full graph. A one-load
preparation there has independently measured energy
\(41.99962\)--\(42.00037\) above vacuum. Complete occupancy records
through time \(64\) give unit residence in the home cell, whereas
a localized port of the same mean energy has lost about \(34.5\%\)
of its occupancy there. This supplies a stationary ordinary source
for a probe that is released after the source has settled.

Two surface addresses also determine a spatial source candidate.
In the round chord reconstruction their separation \(d\) puts the
midpoint at radius \(\sqrt{1-d^2/4}\). Isotropic pairs give a
positive \(1/r\) volume density that reproduces the boundary
modular reads at every center for apertures up to the screen radius.
The interior region chosen by the two endpoints' midpoint and the one
chosen by their direct membership have different records. The protected
native station times also disagree with direct chord distances under
every constant speed calibration.

Three regions give a finite obstruction to interpreting the raw
charge as a local positive rest source. Fix \(n\) on the screen.
At \(n,n/2,0\) take \(B=\sqrt{11}/4,\ R=3/4,\ S=\sqrt{15}/4\).
The squared-distance identity
\(2(R^2-|x-n/2|^2)=(B^2-|x-n|^2)+(S^2-|x|^2)\)
and subadditivity of the positive part require
\(2R Q(n/2,R)\le BQ(n,B)+S Q(0,S)\) for any common nonnegative
stress integrated with the flat rest-ball kernel, wherever it lies.
For raw endpoint words the independently measured residual has the
opposite sign at all four resolutions. The midpoint-weighted
reading has the required sign throughout. Whether this second
reading actually enters the physical fixed-volume entropy is a
further premise. The half-depth source forecasts
\(\eta\,dA|_V\in[-2.337,-2.336]\,10^{-4}\) nats if it supplies
the entire entropy variation. Surface ranges alone leave even the
central area sign undetermined. For example, put
\(U(r)=r^5(1-r^2)^4(r^2-S^2)\) and set the squared angular radius
of a radial proper-distance filling to \(r^2+\epsilon U'(r)\).
For either sign of sufficiently small \(\epsilon\), the screen
metric, the central proper radius and the volumes enclosed at \(S\)
and 1 agree. The boundary area at \(S\) changes by
\(8\pi\epsilon S^6(1-S^2)^4\). An interior transverse range can
distinguish the fillings. There is a separate obstruction even to a
signed local stress if the raw instrument is used through the screen
radius. All the words enter the central read as its aperture crosses
one. For a locally finite signed source the rest-ball kernel instead
bounds the change from \(1-h\) to \(1+h\) by \(4\pi h\) times the
total variation inside the larger ball. At \(h=1/4096\) the measured
jump requires more than 2,184 normalized midpoint masses.
This bound needs the thin-screen radial identification. See
\texttt{three\_regions.json}.

In the localized twenty-cell trial, the two charts give a testable
force comparison. The measured source locations give a strictly
positive potential rise across the selected exterior seam if they
supply a scalar Newtonian potential in the chord chart. A diagonal
potential on the native probe generator changes the initial
near--far acceleration by half that rise. The exterior increments
vanish for both the stationary home load and the shared-minus-fresh
overlap source. Moving an ordinary unit to an adjacent contact
recovers both nonzero native interaction readings. Stationarity here
follows from the native generator before release, so an arbitrarily
long source wait gives the same predictions. The ordinary
macroscopic coupling that enters the Einstein branch is a further
physical identification. See \texttt{midpoint\_bulk.json} and
\texttt{settled\_midpoint\_force.json}.

At each exterior owner a superposition of the compact modes of
Laplacian eigenvalues 4 and 6 yields a clock whose port occupation
oscillates at unit angular frequency. Both modes are verified in
each of the 486 sharp local word configurations. With the ordinary
compact spectator they are exact two-load eigenmodes with the same
gap. Their compared local port histories therefore have zero rate
change at every delay for either source. Counts through time 64
put the change of the frequency difference within \(2.23\,10^{-4}\)
of zero in native units. A word applied directly at the near clock
changes one port probability by \(0.48053\pm0.00112\). This tests
the redshift of an actual material transition independently of the
packet's diagonal potential. See \texttt{midpoint\_clocks.json}.

\paragraph{An exterior acceleration and a test of its ordinary coupling.}
On a twenty-cell carrier a material messenger tests two local word contacts
in succession. The two stations keep the same single-station records.
A shared tag increases the joint pass probability by $1/144$. A
uniform-port courier relays the success record, continuing at each visit
with probability $31/32$. Each accepted visit toggles a second matching of
vacuum contact memories in the visited cell. A local observer reads whether
the number of accepted visits was odd. For the walk Green matrix $G$,
the resulting increment in the occupied-memory probability at $v$ is
$G_{0v}/[144(2G_{vv}-1)]$. This quantity stays fixed after service ceases.
Different rules for receiving the two reports can reverse its sign.

Consider an exterior open edge with near and far ports $a,b$, and let $d$
be the increment in their difference of written internal coordination.
The native hopping matrix element is $-1/2$. Removing an active internal
contact lowers the diagonal return energy at either end by $1/2+8q^2$
for probe load $q$. For the near-cell occupancy $X$, the $ab$ entry of
$-[H,[H,X]]$ equals $H_{ab}(H_{aa}-H_{bb})$, since the endpoints have
no common neighbor. The diagonal entries cancel in the equal in-phase
launch because each gateway has one crossing. Hence the extra acceleration
is $(1/4+4q^2)d$ and the two-load relative clock shift is $-24d$.
On the enrolled two-to-three-hop edge independent source arrivals predict
the two signed flight readings and relative clock at two durations.
At release the responses are $+0.00150691$, $+0.00576171$ and $-0.00850961$
in the respective native units. For a terminal ordinary deposit, the two
acceleration vectors after a common positive normalization are
$(17/2,65/4)$ and $(65/4,65/2)$. Their positive span obeys $A_2\le2A_1$.
The overlap correction violates this inequality at every positive $d$.
A common gravitational coupling to those ordinary standards cannot account
for the three readings.

A coherent wave spread uniformly over every gateway of a hop-shell cut
gives the same coefficient times the mean written-degree difference.
Indeed the crossing commutator maps the gateway launch into the gateway
subspace, and the receiver's internal matching connects each gateway
only to a private port. Its Hamiltonian difference restricted to the
gateways is diagonal. Direct native action verifies 400 sharp ordinary
two-load comparisons on the twenty-cell carrier. Across 78 shell and
stopping-time combinations on the three carriers, all record interventions
enhance inward release. In 77 the ordinary accelerations are inward with
$A_2\le2A_1$. On one outer shell both ordinary controls are outward.
The positive ordinary mass calibration fails on every one of these cuts.

\paragraph{First variation of an exterior receiver at fixed mean volume.}
Time all intracell material delivery routes on both sharp receiver settings.
A repeatedly localized two-load clock supplies a relative frequency of 1
on the baseline and $4/5$ on the written setting. The calibrated ranges
admit 26 strict four-station tetrahedra on both settings. For two
tetrahedra sharing a face let $V_i(p)$ and $A_i(p)$ be their mean volumes
and areas at written probability $p$. A public random selection with
probability $\beta$ for tetrahedron two compensates the changing mean
volume. From $\beta=1/2$ the derivative is
\[
 \dot\beta=-\frac{\dot V_1+\dot V_2}{2(V_2-V_1)}.
\]
The entropy derivative of the local receiver and the independent selection
is $\dot p\log[(1-p)/p]$ there. For tetrahedra $(0,1,4,6)$ and
$(0,1,4,10)$ in the nearer surveyed cell, clock and latch counts give
$\dot A\in[0.00067677,0.00067728]$ and
$\dot S\in[0.00215015,0.00215087]$ in squared range units and nats
per intervention. Thus $\eta\dot A+\dot S>0$ for every $\eta\ge0$.
The measured clock gradient and inward motion coexist with a failure
of stationarity for this particular bulk entropy and choice of region.
The Einstein implication requires an ordinary-calibrated stress and a
stationary intrinsic small-ball construction on the same observer records.

\paragraph{Measuring a persistent ordinary source and testing its entropy.}
An ordinary load initially spread uniformly over 240 ports is exposed
to the overlap-dependent courier report at the two exterior receiving cells.
Every bridge permits exchange during exposure and restoration. Following
erasure, a terminal position read scores the available neighbors next to the
near and far gateways. Across the independent-to-shared intervention
let $b$ be the change of the near-minus-far score. In the restored
two-load dynamics each occupied ordinary neighbor changes the on-site
probe energy by the independently calibrated ordinary exchange term.
The native release law then gives
\[
   (\Delta A_1,\Delta A_2,\Delta\omega)=(17b/2,65b/4,-31b/2).
\]
The count intervals for $b$ are $[0.600,1.243]\times10^{-6}$ and
$[-2.122,-1.479]\times10^{-6}$ for the two dispatch directions.
Subsequent finite probe flights and the clock resolve all three signs
at $t=1/8$ when the guarded reader interrogates the same source used on the open board.
Predictions using only the observed position histograms and separately
sampled ordinary probe calibrations overlap all twelve held intervals
across both read times, both dispatch directions and three instruments.
Energy-block averaging under the restored Hamiltonian bounds the drift
from finite random wait times uniformly in a subsequent undisturbed delay.
This is a finite measurement and implication for ordinary material moved
by an overlap record.

On the mirror-symmetric guarded apparatus, test whether the resulting
ordinary source supplies the stationarity premise. A material tag
traverses restored contacts. Its center clock has independently measured
frequency ratio $209/240$ when an ordinary neighbor is present. A ball
records the occupied site if reached and one outside outcome otherwise.
Use the number of reached markers and the number of crossing faces,
scaled by the cube and square of the clocked range unit, for volume and
area. Mix successive balls with a public probability adjusted to hold
the mean proper volume fixed. Compare the overlap intervention with an
ordinary localized deposit on the identical reference. For every one
of the twelve allowed center, size and direction choices, the finite
count interval for the determinant of the two $(\dot A,\dot S)$ pairs
excludes zero. At the first forward near pair the gains
$-\dot S/\dot A$ lie in $[10.83,11.44]$ and $[5.39962,5.39965]$,
respectively. This regional record entropy fails the common-coefficient
equation even where ordinary exchange predicts the two probe readings
and the clock. Stationary small balls require an entropy and stress
identified on the same refining family.

The entropy of the regional quantum algebra is independently accessible
before full localization. On separate copies the observer uses ordinary
swaps and phases to rotate the reached one-load modes. The region also
has one outside vacuum outcome. The fresh reference fixes the chosen
basis. Fresh interference shots compare both quadratures on each pair
of reached modes. Their off-diagonal row sums bound
$e=\|\rho_0-D\|$, where $D$ is the reference diagonal in that basis.
The measured diagonal gives $m=\min_i D_{ii}-e>0$ on all 16 distinct
region and direction settings. The resolvent identity for the logarithm
gives $\|\log\rho_0-\log D\|\le e/m$. Ordinary and shared-intervention
mode counts thus bound $-\mathrm{Tr}\,\dot\rho\log\rho_0$ with a remainder
of at most $\|\dot\rho\|_1 e/m$. The reference entropy correction lies
between $-e/m$ and zero. These estimates carry the finite wait,
reference coherence, public choice and sampling errors into the comparison.
At every one of the twelve paired sizes the required overlap coefficient
$-\dot S/\dot A$ is strictly negative. At the first forward near pair it
lies in $[-13.49,-12.79]$, while the ordinary reference requires
$[8.2959,8.2961]$. Area and entropy increase together there at fixed
mean proper volume. This tests the coherent regional material algebra on
the prepared branch. The full gravitational entropy could also depend
on overlap or boundary records outside that algebra.

For any additional entropy $X$ on these same regions, eliminating the
common area coefficient gives a measured target
\[
 X'_r-(A'_r/A'_m)X'_m=-S'_r+(A'_r/A'_m)S'_m,
\]
where $r$ and $m$ denote the overlap and ordinary interventions.
At the first forward near pair the right-hand side is between $-145.69$
and $-142.68$ millionths of a nat per intervention. Appending the restored
seam labels contributes zero to this target. Each public ball choice
fixes those labels with certainty, so appending them preserves the
nonzero spectrum in each block.

\paragraph{A local energy input and the contraction stationarity would require.}
At the first forward near comparison, apply a sign change to the load
amplitude at the central gateway on a small fraction of preparations.
The controller remains external to the measured balls. All five bonds
whose work changes lie inside the six-port ball. Their measured extra
kinetic energy is $0.427914$ native units per unit intervention. Position
probabilities, the clock read determining the range scale, and the
area and volume of the counted markers are exactly unchanged at release.

Write $\rho$ for the regional record algebra, including arbitrary
pre-existing boundary records on which the phase acts trivially. The
two preparations are unitarily related there. For their mixture the
right entropy derivative at zero is
$D(U\rho U^\dagger\Vert\rho)$, including a possible infinite value.
Quantum relative entropy decreases on restriction to an observer's
measurement \href{https://doi.org/10.1007/BF01609396}{(Lindblad)}.
A balanced swap on the bridge internal to the larger ball exchanges
the probabilities $a,b$ of its two phase outputs, preserving absence.
After the public fair choice of radii the entropy derivative is
therefore at least $(a-b)\log(a/b)/2>0.19309$ nats. At the ordinary
material calibration $-\dot S/\dot A\simeq8.296$, fixed-volume
stationarity would require $\dot A<-0.02327$ in squared range units.
The counted-face area gives $\dot A=0$. This is a calibrated consequence of
imposing stationarity on this preparation. Applying the Einstein implication
requires a physical identification of the appropriate vacuum reference and
its intrinsic small-ball area.

A phase-sensitive boundary instrument measures a contraction of
transport. Imprint opposite quarter-turn phases on the inside and measure
the material crossing the cut on release. Its signed positive-phase
current is the sum of real coherences on crossing bonds in native units.
The central sign change reverses all five inner contributions and leaves
the outer cut invariant. The decrease in the fair mean current is exactly
half the injected energy. A separate finite flight at $t=1/64$ resolves
a decrease of $0.213900$. Testing this flux as an area on the original
overlap and ordinary interventions gives the common-coefficient
determinant in $[-0.00014553,-0.00014547]$. The measured transport
contraction does not give the universal ordinary area coefficient.

\paragraph{Ordinary work after free propagation.}
Follow the work input with an independent finite free wait. Its energy
average preserves the added kinetic energy. Auditing the two-stage
reference and the added wait gives a trace error below $2.86\times10^{-11}$.
On the guarded 24-port region, compare both the serial reader and the
complete reference reader, each at its own fresh preparation. A
separate cohort calibrates the public radius choice to match mean
proper volume. Held observers record radius, clock scale and enclosed
ordinary occupancy. At a quarter dose of work, each of the twelve
comparisons per instrument gives a smaller measured boundary and a
larger enclosed load fraction. The total ordinary rest load stays one.

For the complete reader the five seam measurements resolve $0.434665$
native units of work. At its smallest forward gateway pair the quarter
dose changes area by $-0.001043$ squared clock-length units and enclosed
load fraction by $+0.007518$. If $v_i,y_i$ are the two sharp-radius means
for volume and any readout, changing the public radius probability by
$\delta b$ changes them by $(v_1-v_0)\delta b$ and $(y_1-y_0)\delta b$.
Removing the measured residual volume times $(y_1-y_0)/(v_1-v_0)$
therefore compares readouts at the target mean volume exactly.

The independent phase and position counts require
$79.23<-\dot S/\dot A<79.25$ for work and
$35.93<-\dot S/\dot A<35.94$ for ordinary localization on this same
pair. Overlap gives $-16.10<-\dot S/\dot A<-14.76$. A common coefficient
would set $\dot A_1\dot S_2-\dot A_2\dot S_1=0$ for every two interventions.
Every one of the twelve complete-reader comparisons excludes zero for
work versus localization and for overlap versus localization. This
identifies ordinary matter moving into a contracting survey and tests
the stronger stationarity premise on precisely the same regions.
The count, area and regional entropy definitions here are those of this
instrument. The matched Einstein implication requires such stationarity
on a common physical refinement tower.

The smallest opposite-gateway pair supplies two ordinary controls with
opposite changes of enclosed mass, $m_w>0>m_d$. Mixing the complete
volume-matched protocols with localization probability $m_w/(m_w-m_d)$
holds the enclosed count fixed as well. For forward dispatch with the
complete reader, their measured first variations then give
$\dot A\in[0.00017284,0.00017287]$ and
$\dot S\in[0.37319,0.37323]$. The source histogram is fresh throughout.
Any differentiable correction depending only on mean enclosed load and
that histogram has zero derivative along this variation. At a finite
quarter intervention, fresh branch, clock and position counts verify
both matches within uncertainty and give an area increase in
$[4.4763,4.4771]\times10^{-5}$ squared clock-length units.

\paragraph{Adding locally retained histories to the entropy.}
Each gateway observer keeps its own write parity before restoring the
contacts. Conditional material tomography covers all 24 sites and both
phase quadratures on every port pair. Enumerate all deterministic
summaries of the bits accessible to each reached region. For a fixed
summary, opposite, fresh and shared preparations lie on one affine
mixture of the four sharp histories. Entropy concavity bounds the
tangent between the two measured secants, including where conditional
density matrices have zero eigenvalues. Including proper-volume
compensation, every one of the 1,920 inner and outer summary choices in
the twelve comparisons requires $-S'/A'<-7.28$. Full retention in ten
cases also makes the ordinary derivative infinite by populating a
mode with zero reference occupancy. These results test a specified family of material
regions and all deterministic compressions of their two write bits.
A different dynamical area or a different local algebra can be tested
against the ordinary phase-work and overlap readings separately.

\paragraph{Recovering a spherical mass from two source readings.}
In a coded twenty-station preparation each observer measures the summed
stored energy, starts a tag at port 3 and records arrival at port 6
after one preset native swap. The two readings distinguish all nine words at that
station. Their joint records across a pair of stations reproduce the
whole finite-alphabet modular expectation. Independent acquisitions give
$0.00329828$--$0.00331199$ nats per unit source change, without probing
a gravitational force. For a round angular surface ruler, minimizing the
later of two endpoint arrival times lifts the midpoint $x$ to
$(x,\sqrt{1-|x|^2})$ on a hemisphere. The optimum follows from the
Cauchy--Schwarz bound on the two endpoint offsets, attained along a
common normal. A gate of angular radius $R$ admits a pair at depth $d_i$
with probability $(c_i-c)_+/\sin R$, where $c_i=\cos d_i$ and
$c=\cos R$. With $r=L\sin R$, its measured modular variation obeys
\[
\frac{\sin R}{2\pi L}Q_R=\sum_i(c_i-c)_+ e_i,
\qquad e_i=\frac{\kappa w_i}{2\pi L}.
\]
Here $w_i$ is the observed shell fraction and $\kappa$ is the conditional
modular increment. The conversion to a physical stress is a hypothesis.

Parameterize a momentarily static spherical metric variation by
$g_{rr}^{-1}=1-r^2/L^2-2G\epsilon\mu(r)/r$ at fixed ordinary $G$. With
$c(x)=\sqrt{1-x^2/L^2}$, fixed-volume stationarity
$\delta A|_V=-4GQ_R$ requires
\[
 \int_0^r \frac{x\mu(x)}{c(x)^3}\,dx
 = L^2\sum_{d_i<R}(c_i/c-1)e_i .
\]
Indeed $\delta V|_r$ is $4\pi G$ times the integral and
$\delta A|_V=-(2c/r)\delta V|_r$. Differentiating in each empty radial
gap forces $\mu=\sum c_i e_i$ there. Conversely integrating this shell
profile gives the required area slope at every aperture. Two measured
aperture sums in each empty gap determine $\mu$ by eliminating the
enclosed proper mass $\sum e_i$. The complete finite preparation has a
central point source. Removing the ten antipodal pairs gives a regular
center and an independent nonlinear equal-volume check in five gaps.
In the complete third gap, at $r^2/L^2=0.514298$, the two apertures give
$\mu=0.457740$--$0.458199$ in units $\kappa/(2\pi L)$. Spherical dust
evolution at fixed background cosmological constant is an additional
hypothesis for the corresponding initial acceleration $-G\mu/r^2$.

A separate survey measures regions by actual paired delivery deadlines.
At equal mean marker count six of the eight measured boundaries expand
as the regional word entropy increases. The other two contract with
incompatible positive gains. Allowing arbitrary common coefficients of
two measured radial ordinary-energy terms leaves a strictly
positive residual. A stationary ordinary load of energy 42 leaves these
timed swaps unchanged. Thus this ruler supplies neither the ordinary
coupling nor the common entropy equilibrium required by the spherical
inference. See \texttt{pair\_clock\_information.json},
\texttt{paired\_balls.json} and \texttt{curved\_shells.json}.

\paragraph{How much of the source do six integrated curvatures recover?}
For each of six closed fillings of the thirty-clock survey define
\(F=\sum_e L_e\epsilon_e/V^{1/3}\), where \(\epsilon_e\) is the
Regge deficit of edge \(e\). Uniform rescaling preserves \(F\).
The Euclidean Schl\"afli identity reduces the numerator differential
to \(\sum_e\epsilon_e\,dL_e\). On the finite matched-entropy
ordinary and joint writes, the changes in \(F\) differ on every
filling. Five pairs change with opposite signs. The disagreement
also holds for equal entropy derivatives at their common reference.
If the reference slice is time symmetric, the derivative of
\(K^2-K_{ij}K^{ij}\) vanishes there. Extrinsic curvature on such a
slice cannot supply the first-order discrepancy under this scalar
stress assignment.

On the eighteen-dimensional fixed-marginal preparation slice, the
six \(F\)'s, anchor volume and eight material means have an interval
right-inverse certificate for their joint derivative at the prepared
reference. Its kernel contains a unit tangent with entropy derivative
greater than \(3.55\times10^{-7}\) nats. Positive preparation weights
and the implicit function theorem yield a curve of exactly constant
readings with varying modular charge. At the exact stationary
point the independently certified identity \(dS=-\eta\,dA_4\)
on the anchor-volume level gives a unit tangent with entropy slope
at least \(2.40\times10^{-7}\) nats and the same fifteen readings.
The finite equilibrium inequality forces an area contraction for
sufficiently small positive displacements along its level curve.
On the numerical tangent, two independently acquired source
populations give a modular contrast between \(2.16\times10^{-8}\)
and \(2.28\times10^{-8}\) nats. Five of ten internal link-transfer
contrasts independently exclude zero. This distinguishes
exact finite-dimensional identifiability from the precision of
individual finite clock acquisitions. See
\texttt{curvature\_source\_null.json} and
\texttt{finite\_dose\_curvature.json}.

\paragraph{A sign test for regional stationarity.}
On the 80-cell radar instrument let \(X\) be the measured paired-head
reference, \(P\) its parity-conditioned independent preparation, and
\(\Phi\) a physical read of the same source. On the straight path
toward \(P\) the individual marginals are fixed. The negative
derivative of \(D(X\Vert P)-D(\Phi X\Vert\Phi P)\) along this path
equals the decrease of the symmetrized relative entropy under
\(\Phi\). It is nonnegative for any fixed receiver, including
instruments with a timeout. The observer can establish a strict
value from the two input and output records without measuring
a local gravitational force.

The six measured stations admit six enrolled triangulated
three-spheres. At fixed proper volume below the first opposite face,
a vertex ball of solid angle \(\Omega\) has area slope
\(r^2\delta\Omega/3\). The independent-pairing intervention increases
the area at station 38 in all six geometries, and the ordinary
intervention decreases it. Their signed source slopes cannot share
a positive stationarity coupling. A changing radius supplies a term
proportional to the area slope, absorbed by ordinary calibration.
Allowing direction selection to move with the incident solid angles
also fails. Even hiding the direction label leaves a residual
exceeding \(0.1734\) nats per unit intervention there. This tests a
particular regional entropy and specific spatial completions. A
smooth proper-ball family carrying a measured stress requires its
own geometric and source evidence. See
\texttt{erasure\_balls.json}.

\paragraph{Measuring the cause of a clock response.}
A clock at \(i\) marks each completed visit to a uniformly chosen
neighbor and back. The round-trip mean between \(i\) and \(j\) on
the protected six-slot walk is \(6nR_{ij}\), where \(R\) is the
commute ruler and \(n\) is the number of stations. Hold the source
fixed for one cycle and renew it at the next. The degree-weighted
period is \(d_i T_i=6n\sum_{v\sim i}E R_{iv}\). At clean launches
the native single and double give
\[
  \Delta\ddot R_{ij}
    =\frac{d_i\Delta T_i+d_j\Delta T_j}{12n}.
\]
The four local pulse records thus predict both a stationary clock
contrast and an initial relative motion. Stations 82 and 48 have a
source-inferred differential log rate of
\((-1.075\pm0.050)\times10^{-10}\) from the independent register
sample. Their native initial relative phase slope, for either
conserved load, has zero source change on the clean contact blocks.
The interpretation as universal macroscopic proper time requires
a common material clock and a source that persists on larger regions.

There is a finite obstruction for this particular stationary clock.
The trace of \(L^+L\) constrains the sum of all changes of incident
edge resistances caused by passive, localized slowdowns. The sum of
absolute log-frequency changes across stations is bounded by twice
the source-weighted sum of added link periods, independently of
exterior size. A persistent Newtonian monopole violates this bound
on a family with arbitrarily many shells at fixed proper spacing.
The ten finite imaged disks also fail the tested absolute transfer
at the ordinary calibration. The proof and the simulation records
are in \texttt{sector\_neighbor\_ticks.py} and the companion dark
matter paper.

\appendix

\section{Smooth effective field descriptions}
\label{app:smooth-effective-descriptions}

This appendix proves that the finite readouts of a scalar wave on a radial
product with measurable screen coefficients have smooth relativistic
descriptions. The theorem needs no smooth exact coefficient field. The
measure of a radial projection of a polyhedral source onto the sphere, for
example, is a positive constant times $(\max_F n_F\cdot u)^{-3}$, where $n_F$
runs over the outward face normals. It is Lipschitz, and its one-sided
gradients jump across every edge by a nonzero tangent vector, so it has no
$C^1$ representative.

\paragraph{Setting.}
Fix $L>0$, the compact radial cylinder $X=[0,L]\times S^2$ and the reference
measure $\nu=(dr/L)\otimes(d\Omega/4\pi)$. Assume:
\begin{enumerate}\setlength{\itemsep}{1pt}
\item[(S1)] $\rho:S^2\to(0,\infty)$ is Lipschitz, so
  $0<\rho_-\leq\rho\leq\rho_+$;
\item[(S2)] $a_{\rm s}$ is a measurable field of symmetric screen tensors with
  $\lambda|\zeta|^2\leq a_{\rm s}(\zeta,\zeta)\leq\Lambda|\zeta|^2$ in the
  round metric, for constants $0<\lambda\leq\Lambda$.
\end{enumerate}
Write $a=1\oplus a_{\rm s}$, so the radial coefficient is one. On
$\mathcal H=L^2(X,\rho\nu)$ the closed form
\[
 E(f)=\int_X\rho\,\bigl(|\partial_rf|^2+a_{\rm s}(\nabla f,\nabla f)\bigr)\,d\nu,
 \qquad
 V=\{f\in H^1(X):f(0,\cdot)=f(L,\cdot)=0\},
\]
defines the self-adjoint spatial generator $B=-\partial_r^2+A$, where $A$ is
the screen operator of $(\rho,a_{\rm s})$; the constant screen mode belongs
to the domain with zero screen energy. Since $\rho$ and $a_{\rm s}$ do not
depend on $r$, separation of variables and the Dirichlet interval give
$B\geq(\pi/L)^2$.

A regular strongly local screen form comparable to the gradient energy has
this representation. For a smooth cutoff $v$ and a coordinate-linear
function $w=\zeta\cdot x$ in a chart, the chain rule for its energy measure
$\Gamma$ gives
\[
 E\Bigl(\frac{v\sin(nw)}{n}\Bigr)+E\Bigl(\frac{v\cos(nw)}{n}\Bigr)
 =\int v^2\,d\Gamma(w)+\frac{E(v)}{n^2}.
\]
The same identity holds for the gradient energy with $|\zeta|^2dx$ in place
of $d\Gamma(w)$. Comparing the two and letting $n\to\infty$ bounds
$\int v^2d\Gamma(w)$ between positive multiples of $\int v^2|\zeta|^2dx$ for
every cutoff, so $\Gamma(w)$ has a density between those multiples of
$|\zeta|^2$. Polarization gives measurable coefficients, and the chain rule
reconstructs the form from them.

\paragraph{The wave action and its metric.}
In local coordinates write $\mu\,d^3x$ for $\rho\nu$ and $a^{ij}$ for the
coordinate components of $a$. The wave action
$S(\phi)=-\tfrac12\int C^{\alpha\beta}\partial_\alpha\phi\,\partial_\beta\phi\,d^4x$
with coefficient density $C=\mu\operatorname{diag}(-1,a)$ has weak equation
$\mu\,\partial_t^2\phi=\partial_i(\mu a^{ij}\partial_j\phi)$, that is
$\partial_t^2\phi=-B\phi$. Since $\det C=-\mu^4\det a$, the tensor
\[
 g=\sqrt{-\det C}\;C^{-1}=\mu\sqrt{\det a}\,\operatorname{diag}(-1,a^{-1})
\]
satisfies $\det g=-\mu^4\det a$ and $\sqrt{-\det g}\,g^{-1}=C$. The weak
equation is therefore the scalar wave equation $\Box_g\phi=0$, and $g$ has
signature $(-,+,+,+)$ almost everywhere. When $\mu$ and $a$ are smooth, $g$
is a smooth static Lorentz metric on $\mathbb R\times X$ and the product
wave is exactly its free scalar field with the radial Dirichlet condition.

\paragraph{Readouts.}
The field theory of a generator $B$ is the quasi-free ground or thermal
state, at a fixed inverse temperature for the static time, on the Weyl
algebra of time-zero field and momentum smearings $F=(f,h)$ with
$f\in\mathcal H$ and $h\in V$. Its two-point functions, its symplectic form
and the time evolution of smearings are given by products of the functions
\[
 c(B)B^{-1/2},\quad c(B)B^{1/2},\quad \cos(t\sqrt B),\quad
 \frac{\sin(t\sqrt B)}{\sqrt B},\quad \sqrt B\sin(t\sqrt B),
\]
with $c(\lambda)=\coth(\beta\sqrt\lambda/2)$ in the thermal state and
$c=1$ in the ground state. A finite admissible menu is a finite list of
outcome probabilities of instruments whose effects are finite polynomials in
Weyl operators of finitely many listed smearings at finitely many times in
a bounded interval $[-T,T]$. Finite Weyl words and finite adaptive
instruments with Weyl feedback are of this kind, together with their
classical records. Since $W(F)W(F')=e^{-i\sigma(F,F')/2}W(F+F')$ and a
quasi-free state has $\omega(W(F))=e^{-Q(F)/2}$, every probability of the
menu is a finite combination of exponentials of the quantities
$\langle u,q(B)v\rangle$ for the displayed functions $q$, the listed
smearings $u,v$ and the listed time differences.

\begin{proposition}[Smooth effective field descriptions]
\label{prop:smooth-effective-descriptions}
Assume (S1) and (S2). Smoothing the angular coefficients while retaining the
radial Dirichlet interval gives norm-resolvent convergence of the transported
generators, with common gap $(\pi/L)^2$. For every fixed finite admissible
menu of observer readouts on bounded wave times and every
$\varepsilon>0$, a smooth static $(1+3)$-dimensional Lorentzian metric carries
a free scalar field whose complete predictions for the menu agree within
$\varepsilon$ with those of the product wave. If the readouts carry stated
source and clock errors, these add to $\varepsilon$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
\emph{Smoothing.} Mollify $\rho$ and $a_{\rm s}$ in a fixed finite atlas of
$S^2$ with a partition of unity and normalize. Along a sequence
$\varepsilon\to0$ this gives smooth positive $\rho_\varepsilon\to\rho$
uniformly, with $\sup|\nabla\rho_\varepsilon|$ bounded and
$\nabla\rho_\varepsilon\to\nabla\rho$ almost everywhere, and smooth
$a_{{\rm s},\varepsilon}\to a_{\rm s}$ almost everywhere with uniform
ellipticity constants, since mollification averages positive forms. Neither
depends on $r$. Let $B_\varepsilon$ be the generator of
$(\rho_\varepsilon,a_{{\rm s},\varepsilon})$ on
$\mathcal H_\varepsilon=L^2(X,\rho_\varepsilon\nu)$ with the same domain $V$;
it obeys $B_\varepsilon\geq(\pi/L)^2$.

\emph{One Hilbert space.} Put $w_\varepsilon=\sqrt{\rho_\varepsilon/\rho}$
and $U_\varepsilon f=w_\varepsilon f$. Then
$\|U_\varepsilon f\|_{\mathcal H}^2=\int|f|^2\rho_\varepsilon\,d\nu$, so
$U_\varepsilon:\mathcal H_\varepsilon\to\mathcal H$ is unitary. The
transported generator $\widetilde B_\varepsilon=U_\varepsilon B_\varepsilon
U_\varepsilon^{-1}$ has the form
$\widetilde E_\varepsilon(f)=E_\varepsilon(f/w_\varepsilon)$, with the same
domain $V$ because $w_\varepsilon^{\pm1}$ are Lipschitz and bounded, and
gap $(\pi/L)^2$ because the radial Poincar\'e inequality holds on every
screen fibre. Moreover $w_\varepsilon^{\pm1}\to1$ uniformly,
$\nabla w_\varepsilon$ is uniformly bounded, and
$\nabla w_\varepsilon\to0$ almost everywhere. The smearing of the smooth
theory is $U_\varepsilon^{-1}f$; it has the support of $f$ and converges to
$f$ in $\mathcal H$, and in $H^1$ for $f\in V$.

\emph{Mosco convergence.} Let $f_\varepsilon\rightharpoonup f$ weakly in
$\mathcal H$ with $\liminf\widetilde E_\varepsilon(f_\varepsilon)<\infty$.
Along a subsequence the energies are bounded. Uniform ellipticity and
$\nabla f_\varepsilon=w_\varepsilon\nabla(f_\varepsilon/w_\varepsilon)
+(f_\varepsilon/w_\varepsilon)\nabla w_\varepsilon$ bound $f_\varepsilon$ in
$H^1$, so by Rellich a further subsequence converges weakly in $H^1$ and
strongly in $L^2$ to $f\in V$. The difference
$\nabla(f_\varepsilon/w_\varepsilon)-\nabla f_\varepsilon/w_\varepsilon
=-f_\varepsilon\nabla w_\varepsilon/w_\varepsilon^2$ tends to zero in $L^2$:
split $f_\varepsilon\nabla w_\varepsilon=(f_\varepsilon-f)\nabla w_\varepsilon
+f\nabla w_\varepsilon$ and use the uniform bound for the first term and
dominated convergence for the second. Hence
$\xi_\varepsilon=\nabla(f_\varepsilon/w_\varepsilon)\rightharpoonup\nabla f$.
With $M_\varepsilon=\rho_\varepsilon a_\varepsilon$ and $M=\rho a$, the
pointwise inequality
$M\xi\cdot\xi\geq2\xi\cdot\psi-M^{-1}\psi\cdot\psi$ for bounded vector
fields $\psi$ gives
\[
 \liminf\widetilde E_\varepsilon(f_\varepsilon)
 \geq2\int\nabla f\cdot\psi\,d\nu-\int M^{-1}\psi\cdot\psi\,d\nu,
\]
because $M_\varepsilon^{-1}\to M^{-1}$ almost everywhere with a uniform
bound. The supremum over $\psi$ is $E(f)$. For the recovery sequence take
$f_\varepsilon=f\in V$: then
$\nabla(f/w_\varepsilon)\to\nabla f$ almost everywhere with an integrable
square majorant, and dominated convergence gives
$\widetilde E_\varepsilon(f)\to E(f)$. Thus $\widetilde E_\varepsilon$
Mosco-converges to $E$, and Mosco's theorem gives strong resolvent
convergence $\widetilde B_\varepsilon\to B$~\cite{mosco1994}.

\emph{Norm-resolvent convergence.} For $\|u\|\leq1$ the vector
$v=(\widetilde B_\varepsilon+1)^{-1}u$ obeys
$\widetilde E_\varepsilon(v)+\|v\|^2=\langle u,v\rangle\leq1$, so the
resolvents map the unit ball into one bounded subset of $H^1$, which is
relatively compact in $\mathcal H$. Suppose
$\|(\widetilde B_\varepsilon+1)^{-1}-(B+1)^{-1}\|\geq\delta>0$ along a
subsequence, attained up to $\delta/2$ at unit vectors
$u_\varepsilon\rightharpoonup u$. Compactness of $(B+1)^{-1}$ gives
$(B+1)^{-1}u_\varepsilon\to(B+1)^{-1}u$. A further subsequence has
$(\widetilde B_\varepsilon+1)^{-1}u_\varepsilon\to k$, and for every $\phi$,
$\langle(\widetilde B_\varepsilon+1)^{-1}u_\varepsilon,\phi\rangle
=\langle u_\varepsilon,(\widetilde B_\varepsilon+1)^{-1}\phi\rangle
\to\langle u,(B+1)^{-1}\phi\rangle$ by strong resolvent convergence. Hence
$k=(B+1)^{-1}u$, a contradiction. The convergence is in norm.

\emph{Readouts.} Norm-resolvent convergence gives
$\|q(\widetilde B_\varepsilon)-q(B)\|\to0$ for every continuous $q$ on
$[(\pi/L)^2,\infty)$ that vanishes at infinity, and strong convergence for
every bounded continuous $q$~\cite{reedsimon1980}. The gap keeps
$c(\lambda)$, $\lambda^{-1/2}$ and $\sin(t\sqrt\lambda)/\sqrt\lambda$
continuous and bounded on the spectrum; the last two, and the product
$c(\lambda)\lambda^{-1/2}$, vanish at infinity, and $t\mapsto\sin(t\sqrt\lambda)/\sqrt\lambda$ is Lipschitz in the
supremum norm, so that convergence is uniform for $|t|\leq T$. The bounded
function $\cos(t\sqrt\lambda)$ converges strongly, uniformly for $|t|\leq T$
on each fixed smearing in $V$, because
$|\cos(t\sqrt\lambda)-\cos(t'\sqrt\lambda)|\leq|t-t'|\sqrt\lambda$ and
$\|\widetilde B_\varepsilon^{1/2}h\|^2=\widetilde E_\varepsilon(h)$ is
bounded. A product function of the menu is unbounded only between two
momentum smearings $h,h'\in V$, and there it is at most a constant times
$\sqrt\lambda$, since $c$ is bounded on the spectrum. Split it with a
continuous cutoff $\chi_K$ equal to one on $[0,K]$ and zero beyond $2K$. The
cut-off part is bounded, continuous and of compact support. Since
$\sqrt\lambda\leq\lambda/\sqrt K$ for $\lambda\geq K$, the tail is bounded
by a constant times
$K^{-1/2}\widetilde E_\varepsilon(h)^{1/2}\widetilde E_\varepsilon(h')^{1/2}$,
uniformly in $\varepsilon$ because $\widetilde E_\varepsilon(h)\to E(h)$. Every quantity $\langle u,q(\widetilde
B_\varepsilon)v\rangle$ of the menu therefore converges to
$\langle u,q(B)v\rangle$, uniformly in the listed times, and so do the
finitely many probabilities built from them. One $\varepsilon$ serves the
whole menu.

\emph{Metric.} For that $\varepsilon$ the coefficient density
$C_\varepsilon=\mu_\varepsilon\operatorname{diag}(-1,a_\varepsilon)$ is
smooth, so $g_\varepsilon=\sqrt{-\det C_\varepsilon}\,C_\varepsilon^{-1}$ is
a smooth static Lorentz metric on $\mathbb R\times X$ whose free scalar field
with radial Dirichlet condition has generator $B_\varepsilon$. Its
predictions for the transported menu equal
$\langle u,q(\widetilde B_\varepsilon)v\rangle$ through $U_\varepsilon$, and
they lie within the requested tolerance of the product wave. Source and clock
errors of the readouts enter both sides identically and add by the triangle
inequality.
\end{proof}

The proposition proves a common finite-menu approximation. It does not
select the radial architecture, which is a declared design input, it gives no
smoothing rate for an unknown limiting tensor, and it produces no responsive
metric, curvature, interacting continuum or laboratory identification. One
smooth metric exact for every observation is not asserted. In this sense the
proposition is the field part of the relativistic $3{+}1$D description stated
with the hypothesis of this paper; with the rank-three spatial carrier of the
source quotient and the causal and count limits of
Appendix~\ref{app:source-causal-order}, that description is fixed by the
predictions for the observers' admissible readouts. The state of the
simulation is no spacetime, and the screen and its carrier are hardware of
the computation.

\section{Causal order, counts and clocks on source populations}
\label{app:source-causal-order}

The theorems of this appendix concern supplied source populations, read laws
and model clocks. They prove the causal, count and clock limits under those
premises; an executable process must produce the population and law before
the limits apply to it.

\paragraph{Travelling clocks of the executable model.}
The main candidate law of the executable model, Interval Echo~\cite{ophmodel},
uses the contrast-memory seam interaction at coupling $\kappa=8$ and gives a
finite observer patch a clock that it carries: a five-position hand, an
elapsed count and the number of its own repair opportunities since its last
verified handoff. A handoff closes an interval, and a bounded first-return
walk on local fair draws decides whether the closing mark advances the hand;
the clock reads no speed and no target table. The observers measure the
motion themselves: a home patch times a reciprocal light pulse and obtains
the one-way light time $d$, a pulse triggered by the arrival of the
traveller at the far end gives the outward duration $T_1$, and the rest of
the trip is the return duration $T_2$. At reunion both owners read the
traveller's and the home counts on one common seam, and their ratio is
compared with the proper-time forecast
\[
 \frac{\sqrt{T_1^2-d^2}+\sqrt{T_2^2-d^2}}{T_1+T_2}.
\]
On three journeys with outward and return leg speeds $(1/2,1/6)$,
$(1/6,1/2)$ and $(1/4,1/4)$ of the signal speed, each with $524{,}288$
paired journeys, the residuals
are ${-0.00105\pm0.00068}$, ${+0.00019\pm0.00052}$ and ${+0.00087\pm0.00064}$ (one
standard error). The dilation is $3.2$ to $4.4$ percent, so the travelling
clock runs slow by the relativistic factor, resolved at dozens of standard
errors and within about one part in a thousand of the forecast. Embeddings
of $1{,}280$ and $5{,}120$ cells give the same material handoff sequence. The
closing filter is calibrated on the reunion ratio of one supplied reciprocal
relay journey; the guide, custody transfers, gate selection, relative
cadence and read windows are supplied services, and the hand size, the
register widths and the walk budget are designed. Clock capacity behaves as
it does for a physical clock: on handoffs of eight services the three-bit
clock overflows, and a four-bit clock, which costs one more cell at each
checkpoint, extends the working range from seven to fifteen services and
restores the forecast, ${0.99005\pm0.00193}$ against $0.99216$. The causal and
count limits of this appendix and premise (G1) further require a jointly
authenticated causal order of the candidate's events whose counts converge
to volume.

\subsection{Finite modular covariance and compressed responses}
\label{sec:finite-modular-covariance}

Geometric modular covariance requires a comparison beyond the existence
of a finite modular clock. The following finite identity states a necessary
boundary of that comparison, independently of the repair scheduler.

\begin{proposition}[Finite modular dilation and its compression boundary]
\label{prop:finite-modular-dilation}
Let $G,P$ be Hermitian matrices on a finite Hilbert space, let
$U(s)=e^{isG}$, and let $\kappa\in\mathbb R$. Then
\[
 \|i[G,P]-\kappa P\|_{\mathrm{HS}}^2
 =\|[G,P]\|_{\mathrm{HS}}^2+\kappa^2\|P\|_{\mathrm{HS}}^2,
 \qquad
 \|U(s)PU(s)^*-e^{\kappa s}P\|
 \geq |e^{\kappa s}-1|\,\|P\|.
\]
For an orthogonal projection $Q$, write $G_Q=QGQ$, $P_Q=QPQ$ and
$B_Q=i(QG(1-Q)PQ-QP(1-Q)GQ)$. Then
\[
 Q\,i[G,P]Q=i[G_Q,P_Q]+B_Q,
 \qquad
 \|Q(i[G,P]-\kappa P)Q\|_{\mathrm{HS}}+\|B_Q\|_{\mathrm{HS}}
 \geq |\kappa|\,\|P_Q\|_{\mathrm{HS}}.
\]
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
Cyclicity gives $\operatorname{Tr}(P\,i[G,P])=0$, so the cross term
in the squared Hilbert--Schmidt norm vanishes. Unitary invariance of
the operator norm and the reverse triangle inequality give the
finite-parameter bound. Inserting $Q+(1-Q)$ into each product gives
the compressed identity. Apply the first identity to the compressed
matrices, then the triangle inequality, for the last bound.
\end{proof}

For an example, let $\sigma$ be a faithful diagonal reference with weights
$2/225$ and $2/45$ on two configurations $a$ and $b$, and take
$G=\log\sigma$; thus $s$ is the dimensionless modular parameter. On the
two-state corner of $a$ and $b$ declare
\[
 P=\begin{pmatrix}1&i/2\\-i/2&1\end{pmatrix},\qquad
 Q=|\psi\rangle\langle\psi|,\qquad
 |\psi\rangle=(|a\rangle+|b\rangle)/\sqrt2,
\]
and extend $P$ by zero. It is positive, with nonzero eigenvalues
$1/2,3/2$. For $\kappa=\log(5)/2$, the projected first derivative
is exactly $\kappa P_Q$, supplied entirely by $B_Q$; the internal
compressed commutator is zero. Nevertheless
\[
 \langle\psi|U(s)PU(s)^*|\psi\rangle
 =1+\tfrac12\sin(s\log5)\ne e^{s\log(5)/2}
\]
in general. At $s=1/\log5$ the two values are approximately $1.420735$
and $1.648721$. The declared probe is not identified with physical null
momentum.

Consequently a fixed finite Hilbert algebra cannot supply nonzero
global modular dilation. A spectral projection of $G$ has $B_Q=0$ and
retains the same obstruction. Restricted continuum comparisons must
specify states, observable domains and finite parameter windows, retain
the compression boundary, and control the actual responses on one
refining family. Unbounded continuum generators are not excluded by
these bounded-operator statements.
